Migrating TorchSpyreModelRunner to vLLM Model Runner V2

Investigation for torch-spyre/spyre-inference#906 · 2026-10-08 · spyre-inference 8403f33 (pins vLLM v0.28.0) · compared against vLLM v0.28.0, v0.29.0, v0.31.0 and main@bab0ac61f · static code reading only (nothing was run on Spyre hardware)

TL;DR

v0.32
V1 removal target (≈ mid/late Oct 2026 at the 2-week cadence)
4
hard breaks on the V1 path between 0.29 and 0.31 (MRV2 or not)
~20
MRV2 Triton entry kernels on the default per-step path, with no torch fallback
6–10 wk
estimated engineering effort for the recommended port (S1), excluding upstream review time
  1. The real obstacle is the Triton gate, not our runner code. MRV2 is only selected when HAS_TRITON is true. spyre-inference forbids Triton (pyproject.toml:77-78), so vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner is always False for us. Forcing VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1 raises "Model Runner V2 requires Triton." That flag isn't local to the worker: about 40 call sites read it (the scheduler, async scheduler, input processor and the inherited Worker), so building an MRV2-based runner from our own worker isn't enough. No backend runs MRV2 without some Triton today: CPU uses triton-cpu, XPU triton-xpu, Ascend triton-ascend. blocker
  2. The issue's stopgap needs correcting. VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=0 is unnecessary: we already fall back to V1 automatically because Triton is missing. It is harmless belt-and-braces. =1 can never work. Our TorchSpyreWorker builds its runner directly, so the 0.29 "default flip" never picks our runner anyway.
  3. Staying on V1 through 0.31 is cheap but not free. V1 is still changing (41 runner commits from v0.28 to main). We found three hard breaks in 0.29 (KV-cache tensor schema, new kv_cache_allocation_context kwarg, a dead slot-mapping patch) and two traps in 0.31 (an inherited CpuPlatform kernel override that calls the missing torch.ops._C, and the scheduler prefill cap silently stopping working). Details.
  4. The API mapping is tractable. Most of our ~14 V1 overrides plus the ~22 Spyre-only methods get cleaner homes in MRV2: typed public seams on a ModelState mixin (prepare_attn, prepare_inputs, custom_sampler), a PoolingRunner subclass, and a CudaGraphManager.dispatch() override for bucketing. Of the 47 rows mapped (two slices), 26 are trivial, 12 re-target and 9 redesign.
  5. The Spyre-specific work is concentrated in five places: (a) pure-CPU replacements for about 20 per-step Triton kernels (input prep, block tables, staged writes, sampler); (b) a dispatch override, without which MRV2 pads nothing and every distinct token count recompiles every block; (c) owning KV allocation and the attention-group setup, which MRV2 folds into one method; (d) pooling output, because AsyncPoolingOutput is hard-wired to CUDA events and a non-blocking D2H; (e) process-wide torch.cuda stream/event placeholders.
  6. Some layouts and designs survive unchanged: head-major and token-major KV, the attention kernels, the bucketer, batched-decode ordering (MRV2 always puts decodes first), encoder-only models (native in MRV2), TP/SpyreCommunicator, memory accounting, compile_guard, and the CUDA graphs that stay off. The "runner device=cpu, Spyre at the model boundary" design maps directly onto vLLM's own CPU MRV2 runner, a 16-line subclass.
  7. Several silent failures appear if someone ports naively. The sliding-window group split never runs, bucket padding vanishes, the spec-decode guard (hasattr(self, "drafter")) always passes, the force_attention argument is swallowed by **kwargs, and pooling output is dropped by the worker unless the flag is True. A checklist is below.
  8. Write our own kernels; don't adopt triton-cpu (details). triton-cpu ships only x86_64 wheels and is neither built nor tested on ppc64le or s390x. Upstream vLLM's own Power and Z CPU backends don't run MRV2 either: they still use the V1 CPUModelRunner, which subclasses the same V1 GPUModelRunner we do. Pure-torch/numpy kernels written for us can be contributed upstream, where Power, Z and Triton-less Arm need the same thing (vllm#55847).
  9. Recommendation: do the V1 bump fixes now (Phase 0). Build the Triton-kernel replacement library on the v0.31 dispatcher hook (useful even on V1). Subclass MRV2 GPUModelRunner with device=cpu (S1). At the same time, push upstream for a platform-level Triton-gate exemption and the component factory (S4). Keep a "vendor V1 for one release" contingency (S3) ready for 0.32. Yes, this warrants an EPIC; a 15-item breakdown is below.
Classification used throughout: no change re-target same logic, new hook redesign mechanism changes shape blocker nothing works until done / needs upstream

≈ 2026-10-15/20v0.32 expected (V1 removal target)Hard deadline per #906

Runway is short. At a 2-week cadence, v0.32 is days to weeks away, and our pin is three minors behind. Realistically, the V1 path has to survive at least the 0.29→0.31 bumps (Phase 0) while the MRV2 port is built against 0.32/main. If V1 really is deleted in 0.32 before the port is ready, the contingency is to vendor V1 for one release (S3) or to hold the pin at 0.31.x.

The real blocker: the Triton gate and the engine-wide flag

How the runner is chosen (main config/vllm.py:709-768)

  1. HiSparse is set → V2 (raises if env=0); watermark is set → V2. Both from 0.31.
  2. VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER is set → use it.
  3. ROCm exceptions.
  4. not HAS_TRITON → V1, with a warning. Spyre stops here at every version.
  5. Unsupported features → V1. On 0.29–0.31.0 this list still includes "stock torch.compile", our default mode.
  6. Otherwise V2.

If V2 is chosen, _validate_v2_model_runner raises "Model Runner V2 requires Triton." when Triton is absent (V31 :3316, main :3242).

Why "our worker builds the runner" doesn't help

  • TorchSpyreWorker.init_device (spyre_worker.py:99-166) fully replaces upstream's version and constructs TorchSpyreModelRunner at :163, so the V1/V2 choice never runs.
  • But the scheduler output protocol differs: V2 re-sends resumed requests as NewRequestData, and V1 alone gets all_token_ids and prev_step_scheduled_req_ids (main scheduler.py:1417,1449,1731).
  • The inherited Worker.execute_model calls runner.pool() only when the flag is True (main gpu_worker.py:1297,1323). With the flag False, an MRV2-based runner's pooling output is silently dropped.
  • The flag is also read by the async scheduler, input processor, max_concurrent_batches and the cudagraph sizing.

Ways past it (pick one or more; see decisions):

OptionMechanismProsCons
A. Upstream gate relaxationA platform hook like supports_mrv2_without_triton(), or relax HAS_TRITON when the platform registered overrides for every MRV2 kernel. Precedents: #50612 (TPU exemption, open), #55847 (CPU torch fallbacks, open). Comment on #57684.Clean, shared with OpenVINO, CPU-without-triton-cpu, RBLN and NeuronThe schedule isn't ours; #57684 has no maintainer answer yet
B. Plugin-side monkeypatchPatch vllm.config.vllm.HAS_TRITON (and the validator) from register(), plus register_kernels overridesUnblocks development todayFragile; flips HAS_TRITON semantics globally for anything else that reads it
C. Allow triton-cpu rejectedDrop the triton; sys_platform == 'never' ban and ship triton-cpu, as in-tree CPU MRV2 doesMost MRV2 kernels would run unmodified on device=cpuWheels only for x86_64; never built or tested on ppc64le/s390x (see below). It would also need a patch to vLLM's version-string check and would switch Triton on across all of vLLM.

Bump blockers on the V1 path (needed regardless of MRV2)

These hit the very next vLLM bump. Several of them (marked ★) are also the first step of the MRV2 port.

History check: the 0.23–0.28 bumps were each one PR merged in 1–7 days with about 40–300 lines of real change. But the runner has grown from 477 to 1952 lines (2 → 9 super() hooks), and most of the current override surface post-dates the 0.28 bump. The 0.29 bump is the first test of it, so expect about 1–2 weeks rather than days.

Axis 1 — API mapping: V1 hook → MRV2 seam

MRV2's design rule (docstring of gpu/model_runner.py) is that the runner holds only model-agnostic code, with behaviour pushed into helper objects. Our hooks therefore move from private runner methods wrapped with *args/**kwargs onto typed, public methods of three or four helper objects. That shift is a net improvement in robustness.

Our V1 hook (spyre_model_runner.py)What it does for SpyreMRV2 seam (v0.31 lines)Class

Suggested MRV2 shape

TorchSpyreModelRunnerV2(vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner.GPUModelRunner)   # device=cpu, like vLLM's CPU MRV2
├─ load_model()           Spyre load → .to(spyre) → per-block compile → wrap → THEN init_model_state / PoolingRunner / Sampler
├─ initialize_kv_cache()  window-split attn groups + builders · SpyreBlockTables · Spyre allocator · SpyreBucketManager
├─ pool()                 sync / Spyre-safe D2H instead of AsyncPoolingOutput
│
├─ SpyreModelStateMixin   built over resolve_model_state_cls(...) (Default / EncoderOnly / …) — or via wrapper.get_model_state_cls
│     prepare_attn        encoder plan, grid publish            (was _build_attention_metadata)
│     prepare_inputs      grid expansion, RoBERTa offset        (was _preprocess)
│     prepare_inputs_embeds   stash embeds for CLIP grid; Spyre-resident inputs_embeds buffers
│     custom_sampler      → SpyreSampler (sort-free top-k, log-space draw, torch ports)
├─ SpyrePoolingRunner(PoolingRunner)       pool, _dummy_pooler_run_task (host cursor, no dim-0 slice)
├─ SpyreBucketManager(ModelCudaGraphManager)  dispatch() → BatchExecutionDescriptor(NONE, bucket(n))
├─ SpyreBlockTables(BlockTables)           apply_staged_writes / gather_block_tables / compute_slot_mappings in numpy
└─ TorchSpyrePlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides()   numpy/torch impls for the ~20 per-step kernels
   + process-wide torch.cuda Stream/Event placeholders (pooling fence → torch.spyre.synchronize)

Difficulty counts

SliceRowsTrivialRe-targetRedesignNotes
Setup / lifecycle (A2)261268The hard ones: __init__, load_model, buffers, slot-mapping, attention backend init, KV init, padding hook
Per-step execution (A3)211461The only redesign is _pool; plus the sampler swap (counted under sampling)
Total4726129Not a rewrite of Spyre logic; most of the work is re-homing it plus the Triton replacements

The issue says "~45 overrides". An AST diff finds 14 V1 GPUModelRunner methods overridden (plus the dead get_dp_padding), 22 Spyre-only methods on the runner, the worker's own overrides, and 4 module-level patches and buffers.

Silently dead overrides under MRV2 (porting checklist)

If we re-base the runner on MRV2 without addressing each of these, they stop working with no error:

  • initialize_attn_backend is never called, so the sliding-window split is lost and gemma-style hybrids get one window on every layer.
  • _determine_batch_execution_and_padding is never called, so no bucket padding and a recompile per token count.
  • _make_buffer, _sync_device, _init_device_properties, get_dp_padding and _dummy_pooler_run_task (moved to PoolingRunner) are never called.
  • The force_attention=True kwarg is swallowed by **kwargs.
  • hasattr(self, "drafter") is always False (MRV2 uses self.speculator), so the spec-decode rejection always passes.
  • use_cuda_graph and cascade_attn_enabled are written but never read.
  • With use_v2_model_runner == False, the stock Worker.execute_model never calls pool(), so pooling output is dropped.

And these crash instead: self.sampler.topk_topp_sampler in __init__, self.inputs_embeds.gpu in load_model, cache.device on SpyrePagedKVCache, and every Triton kernel launch ('function' object is not subscriptable).

Axis 2 — Impact on Spyre-specific logic

AreaVerdictOne-line reason
Runner device placement (device=cpu, Spyre at the model boundary)no changeMRV2 puts all state on self.device; with CPU that is today's design and vLLM's CPU MRV2 precedent
Per-step input prep / block tables / staged writesblocker → redesign8 always-on Triton kernels with pointer tables; need numpy re-implementations
Shape bucketing (token / body buckets)re-targetOne seam, CudaGraphManager.dispatch(), about 40 LOC; highest-priority recompile risk
Logits row bucketing / lm_head / logits D2Hno changeSame model.compute_logits call site
KV cache layouts (head-major / token-major)re-targetBoth expressible (LBHNC/LBNHC); keep our allocator; override initialize_kv_cache
Attention metadata fieldsno changeEvery field our builder reads is still passed; only fields we never use were removed
Attention kernels, bucketer, online softmax, head-major storeno changeBelow the runner
Batched decode eligibility / orderingno changeMRV2 always sorts decodes first (sort_batch_req_ids); minor coverage loss from 1-token prefill tails
Sliding-window group splitre-targetBuilders are now created in the same function as the groups
Warmup attention recordingre-targetSelf-contained; finds builders via attn_groups
Warmup dummy runsredesignMRV2 dummies now execute attention (e.g. 256 reqs × 2 tokens through the per-sequence loop)
Per-block compile, compile_guard, recompile limitsno changeCall sites move with load_model; suppress MRV2's whole-model stock compile on main
CUDA graph captureno changeInert: support_static_graph_mode() is False, so NONE
Sampling (Gumbel, temperature, penalties, logprobs, …)blocker → redesignTriton end to end, even greedy; about 15 kernel ports; SpyreTopKTopPSampler has nothing to attach to
Pooling (embed / classify / token_classify)redesignAll our tasks are supported by MRV2, but the output path is CUDA-async, does a dim-0 slice on Spyre, and builds the cursor on the device
Encoder-only modelsre-targetNative in MRV2 via EncoderOnlyModelState (not a V1 fallback)
Multimodal (Pixtral, gemma-4, granite-vision, CLIP)re-targetSupported; wrapper must be installed before init_model_state; mm-embed staging buffer moves
Streams / events / async outputre-targetProcess-wide placeholders (as in cpu/shm.py), but with a real Spyre sync for pooling
Async schedulingno changeCan stay on for decoders (synchronous on CPU); off for pooling
TP / SpyreCommunicator / collectivesno changeMRV2 issues no TP collectives of its own
Memory accounting / sleep / profilerno changeWorker-level overrides keep working
Spec decode / LoRAre-targetMove the guards to the platform (the drafter guard dies under MRV2)

Input prep & batching

  • MRV2 keeps request state on device as the source of truth (RequestState: all_token_ids, num_computed_tokens, last_sampled_tokens, …). The host keeps only optimistic upper-bound mirrors. Decode input ids are produced from last_sampled_tokens by combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens, not from scheduler data, and post_update advances the state after sampling.
  • Spyre can't hold that state (no native int32, no index ops, no Triton). The answer is device=cpu: all of it becomes host tensors, and our wrapper keeps doing the int→Spyre-int64 H2D at the model boundary.
  • 8 always-on kernels need CPU versions: _apply_write (single + fused), gather_block_tables, compute_slot_mappings, prepare_prefill_inputs, prepare_pos_seq_lens, combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens, post_update and post_update_num_computed_tokens, plus get_num_sampled_and_rejected on the sampler side.
  • Implement them as vectorised numpy on the .numpy() views, not torch ops. Per #981, every extra CPU aten op per step lengthens torch-spyre's eager-op guard chain. Patch free functions in both the defining and the importing module (the kernels are imported by name into model_runner.py and sampler.py), or use the v0.31 dispatcher, which rebinds every alias.
  • UVA: our platform is OOT, so is_cpu() is False and is_uva_available() is False. MRV2 then uses NonUvaBuffer (a redundant CPU→CPU copy). It works; an alias shim like upstream CPU's would save the copy.
  • Estimate: about 450–700 LOC of production code (about 300 of it kernel ports), 300–500 LOC of tests, and about 250 LOC of V1 shims deleted; roughly 2–3 engineer-weeks, or about 1 week if triton-cpu is allowed. The biggest correctness risk is bit-exactness of combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens/post_update.

Shape bucketing & recompiles

MRV2 has exactly one padding decision: dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp → cudagraph_manager.dispatch() → BatchExecutionDescriptor.num_tokens, and the runner uses num_tokens_after_padding = max(num_tokens, desc.num_tokens) (v0.31 model_runner.py:1326). With CUDA graphs off and no override, it pads nothing, so every distinct prompt length recompiles every block class (dynamic=False) and trips compile_guard after arming. The fix is a SpyreBucketManager(ModelCudaGraphManager) whose dispatch returns BatchExecutionDescriptor(cg_mode=NONE, num_tokens=bucket, num_reqs=num_reqs). It replaces three V1 overrides.

  • Profile runs (need_eager) bypass dispatch(), but our worker never calls profile_run.
  • InputBatch.num_tokens_after_padding replaces the _encoder_buffer_rows side channel.
  • A second recompile source is the multimodal inputs_embeds path: MRV2 calls model.embed_input_ids(input_ids[:num_tokens]) unpadded and slice-assigns into a CPU buffer. That is unbucketed, and it hits the narrowed-destination H2D that torch-spyre#4730 rejects, unless the wrapper is what the model state holds and the buffer is Spyre-resident.

Attention & KV cache

ItemFindingClass
CommonAttentionMetadataAll fields we read are passed by attn_utils.build_attn_metadata. In eager mode MRV2 passes unpadded num_reqs/num_actual_tokens and a PAD-filled slot mapping at the padded length, which matches our KV write. Cheap hardening: prefer query_start_loc_cpu and seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound in build().no change
Block tables / slot mappingTriton with pointer indirection; SpyreBlockTables re-implements it at method level (our _compute_slot_mapping_impl math ports almost directly; add idx_mapping, -1 → PAD and multi-group handling).blocker
KV allocationSee Phase 0 #1 (schema) and #9 (layout API). MRV2's allocate_kv_cache wants one shared backing tensor, which torch-spyre can't re-view, so we keep our allocator by overriding initialize_kv_cache.redesign
Window splitFullAttentionSpec.merge still collapses windows, so the split is still needed. Make sure the discarded merged builder's install() side effect is harmless (the last install has to win). An upstreaming opportunity: add the window to init_attn_backend's group key.re-target
Warmup dummies run attentionDummy batch: num_reqs = min(num_tokens, max_num_reqs), seq_len == query_len, zeroed block tables, PAD slots. Options: force num_reqs=1 via dispatch for decoder dummies, use skip_attn+is_profile, or let the dummies replace part of the recording pass. Upside: publish_null_slots and the pooling seq_lens clamp become unnecessary. Verify on hardware with SPYRE_COMPILE_GUARD=error.redesign
Encoder-onlyNative EncoderOnlyModelState; encoder builders are created at load_model time, which is still before our block compile.re-target
Batched decodeMRV2 ignores reorder_batch_threshold but always sorts decodes first. A 1-token chunked-prefill tail sorted among the decodes cuts the batched prefix (correct, slightly less batched). An optional tweak is treat_short_extends_as_decodes=True.no change
Pre-existing (not MRV2)Our attention ignores gemma-4's mm-prefix bidirectional ranges in both runners.note

Compile & warmup

  • load_model strategy: MRV2's load_model now owns about 150 lines of state setup. Two options. (1) Call super with get_model_loader wrapped (to install the head-pad hooks) and compile_model_with_stock_torch patched to a no-op, then device-move, compile per block, wrap, and re-point model_state.model, encoder_runner.model and pooling_runner.model. (2) A full override that re-creates the post-load wiring. Don't flip compilation_config.mode to NONE around super: SpyreAttentionImpl reads it at construction.
  • MRV2's main-branch stock compile is a whole-model model.compile(fullgraph=True) without dynamic=False. It would nest over our block compiles, introduce SymInts Spyre rejects, and hide from compile_guard. Suppress it.
  • Our warmup driver stays (the worker override is retained). Drop force_attention. _dummy_sampler_run now goes through the MRV2 sampler. Don't adopt MRV2's warmup_kernels (Triton sampler and bitmask).
  • compile_guard.py depends only on torch internals: no change. Its tests that patch GPUModelRunner._dummy_run must be re-baselined, and MRV2 now runs grid expansion during warmup, which changes what warmup compiles (probably better coverage).

Sampling & logits

  • Every sampled token, greedy included, goes through the Triton _gumbel_sample_kernel; there is no argmax branch. The default-path ports are temperature, gumbel, min_p, logit_bias, penalties+bincount, bad_words, logprob ×3, prompt_logprob, the sampling mask, the grammar bitmask, and get_num_sampled_and_rejected and post_update from input_batch.py.
  • The torch apply_top_k_top_p fallback uses allow_cpu_sync=current_platform.is_cpu(), which is False for us, so it takes the sorting path that SpyreTopKTopPSampler exists to avoid. Fold sort-free top-k and the log-space draw into a SpyreSampler.
  • RNG semantics change: MRV2 seeds per position with murmur3 of (seed, pos, vocab offset), independent of batch composition. A port should reproduce it if we want upstream-compatible seeded outputs. That will change seeded golden outputs.
  • Logprobs are used by our quality and determinism tests, so they need 3 kernel ports. Prompt logprobs (untested) use 1024-row chunks, which miss our lm_head row buckets; reject them or add a bucket.
  • Structured outputs: StructuredOutputsWorker creates a torch.cuda.Stream in load_model (outside the scoped _torch_cuda_wrapper) and uses a Triton bitmask kernel. Use the xgrammar CPU path instead.
  • Custom logits processors: v0.31+ MRV2 accepts only its new interface. We use none; document it.

Pooling / encoder

  • MRV2's PoolingRunner supports embed, classify, token_embed, token_classify and embed&token_classify, which covers all ~12 of our pooling models (granite-embedding, e5, MiniLM, bge, bge-reranker, bert-NER, CLIP).
  • We need a SpyrePoolingRunner (host cursor, no dim-0 slice, grid-direct CLS/MEAN, unpad, the FP32-head CPU branch), a SpyreEncoderOnlyModelState (grid expansion, encoder plan, token_type_ids), and a runner pool() override so we don't go through AsyncPoolingOutput, or a fence that calls torch.spyre.synchronize(). finished_mask now comes from cursor.get_finished_mask().
  • The per-step encoder state (_encoder_grid, forced rectangles, counters) moves from the runner onto the model state; the pooling runner keeps a back-reference to it.
  • Re-point the unit tests that construct fake runners (test_encoder_inline_dispatch.py, test_encoder_dense_grid.py).

Multimodal

  • MRV2 supports gemma-4 multimodal and our other vision models; none of them use M-RoPE, so its Triton kernel is never reached.
  • Ordering: MRV2 captures self.model in init_model_state and EncoderRunner inside load_model. If our wrapper isn't installed first, pixel_values stay on CPU and embed_input_ids skips bucketing.
  • mm-embed staging (commit 2623972) moves to EncoderRunner.inputs_embeds. That buffer is allocated on self.device (CPU), so it must be replaced by a Spyre tensor. We also need to confirm on hardware that MRV2's buf[:n] = x (__setitem__) hits torch-spyre's self-copy skip the same way V1's .copy_ does.
  • Pin cudagraph_mm_encoder off, because gemma-4 declares encoder CUDA-graph support.

Streams, async output, TP, memory, sleep, profiler

  • MRV2 uses torch.cuda.Stream (constructor), main_stream, and torch.cuda.Event/set_stream in AsyncOutput on every step. Our scoped _torch_cuda_wrapper only covers __init__, so we need permanent placeholders. Don't import cpu/shm.py as-is: it no-ops torch.accelerator.synchronize and swaps buffer classes in ways that need Spyre-specific judgement.
  • Async scheduling stays on for decoders (synchronous on a CPU device, so it is exact) and off for pooling.
  • TP: MRV2 adds no TP collectives. Reject batch-sharded sampling (its compute_logits_local bypasses the wrapper's H2D) and PCP>1.
  • Memory, sleep and the profiler: the worker overrides keep working; _maybe_get_memory_pool_context is now also passed into KV allocation (still a nullcontext).

Triton kernel inventory (what "Spyre-native replacements" means concretely)

@triton.jit count under vllm/v1/worker/gpu/: 95 at v0.31.0 and 97 at main (31 files). Only the default path matters for us:

KernelFile (main)WhenNeeded for us

_gather_block_tables_kernel, _compute_slot_mappings_kernelblock_table.py:247,287every stepyes
_prepare_prefill_inputs_kernel, _prepare_pos_seq_lens_kernel, _combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens_kernelinput_batch.pyevery stepyes
_post_update_kernel, _post_update_num_computed_tokens_kernel, _get_num_sampled_and_rejected_kernelinput_batch.pyevery sample / pool stepyes

Delivery vehicle: TorchSpyrePlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides() → vllm.triton_utils.dispatcher.register_kernels({"qualified.name": impl}), which keeps the kernel[grid](...) launch syntax, handles the no-Triton placeholder, and rebinds every alias. Two caveats: the impl receives the kernel's positional args, including pointer tables, so pointer-table kernels are better overridden at the method level. And overrides are keyed by qualified name, so a coverage test that fails when any default-path @triton.jit lacks an override is essential against silent drift on bumps.

triton-cpu on Power / Z, and how upstream Power / Z run today

Follow-up investigation, 2026-10-09. Source reading and package-index checks only; no build was attempted, because the investigation host is x86_64.

Is triton-cpu available?

Sourcex86_64aarch64ppc64les390x
triton-cpu releases (latest v3.8.0, 2026-09-22)✅❌❌❌
triton-cpu CI✅ Intel x86macOS arm64 only❌❌
vLLM requirements/cpu.txt✅ ("triton-cpu, only for x86_64 CPUs")❌❌❌
IBM wheel index (wheels.developerfirst.ibm.com)——upstream GPU Triton 3.6.0 only (nvidia/amd backends, no cpu)nothing
conda-forge triton✅✅❌❌

Is it buildable?

Probably, but nobody has proven it:

  • Codegen is generic. The CPU backend emits LLVM IR for the host triple (llvm::sys::getProcessTriple()). Its CMake only requires the LLVM native target (third_party/cpu/CMakeLists.txt:43). The x86 and Arm code (AMX, AVX-512, NEON/SVE, oneDNN, XSMM) is optional fast paths. On Power and Z, math falls back to plain libm, which is fine for our integer bookkeeping kernels.
  • No prebuilt LLVM. Triton publishes LLVM only for x64 and arm64 (python/build_helpers.py:396-424). You'd build LLVM at the pinned hash b010a18 with targets Native;NVPTX;AMDGPU (NVPTX and AMDGPU are linked unconditionally, CMakeLists.txt:396-398). That's a multi-hour build per architecture, repeated on every triton-cpu bump.
  • SLEEF (a required dependency) supports PowerPC VSX and s390x vector, and conda-forge ships it for ppc64le.
  • Risks: untested on both architectures, so we'd own the bugs. s390x is big-endian, which no Triton CI exercises. There's an open PowerPC LLVM-symbol build issue (triton#3448) against upstream Triton.
  • Catch on every architecture, x86 included: vLLM only accepts Triton's CPU backend when the installed vLLM version string contains "cpu" (vllm/triton_utils/importing.py, vllm_version_matches_substr("cpu")). Our build is 0.28.0+empty, so even an installed triton-cpu would leave HAS_TRITON False until we patch that. Installing Triton would also switch Triton on across the rest of vLLM.

How upstream vLLM runs on Power and Z today

  • Power (ppc64le) is supported on the CPU backend:
    • docker/Dockerfile.ppc64le, using IBM's prebuilt wheel index (#46017)
    • Power C++ kernels in csrc/cpu
    • hardware CI (.buildkite/scripts/hardware_ci/run-cpu-test-ppc64le.sh)
    Main's installation docs have no Power page. IBM Z (s390x) has a docs page, a Dockerfile and hardware CI.
  • Neither runs MRV2. No triton-cpu means HAS_TRITON is False, so use_v2_model_runner is False and CPUWorker builds the V1 CPUModelRunner (cpu_worker.py:161-177). That class subclasses the same V1 GPUModelRunner we do. IBM's GPU-only Triton wheel doesn't change this, because the vLLM CPU build accepts only a Triton with a cpu backend.
  • So upstream Power, Z and Triton-less Arm have exactly our problem. They break when V1 is deleted unless one of these lands first:
    • #55847, open: torch fallbacks for 13 per-step MRV2 kernels, and relaxing the Triton check, but only for vLLM's built-in CPU platform
    • a V1 support window past v0.32 (#57684)
    This is also the strongest reason to expect the V1 removal to slip.

Decision: implement our own pure kernels, written to be upstreamable. Spyre deploys on Z and Power, so whatever we use must work on s390x. Pure numpy/torch kernels work on every architecture and run in ordinary CPU CI. They also match what upstream needs for its own Power, Z and Triton-less Arm CPU backends. triton-cpu on x86 alone would double the test matrix without helping the deployment architectures.

  • Interface: target the same register_kernels dispatcher entries that #55847 uses. Our implementations can then be contributed there, or replaced by upstream's if theirs land first.
  • Who to work with: engage the vLLM Power and Z CPU maintainers and the #55847 authors.
  • Upstream ask: request that the Triton check be relaxed through a platform capability (in-tree CPU and out-of-tree platforms alike), not through is_cpu().
  • Optional experiment (not needed for the decision): build LLVM at b010a18 and triton-cpu on a Power pod and a Z pod, then run python/test/unit. Expect about a day per architecture; Z/big-endian is the real unknown.

Ecosystem precedent

BackendApproachTriton?Status
vLLM CPU (in-tree)16-line CPUModelRunner(MRV2 GPUModelRunner) + global cpu/shm.py shim; V1 CPU runner still existstriton-cpu (x86_64)Shipping; Arm wants #55847 torch fallbacks

So far, every backend that actually runs MRV2 ships some Triton. We'd be the first Triton-free MRV2 port, and that's why the upstream asks matter.

Proposed sub-issues for the EPIC

  1. [0.29 bump] KV-cache allocation for the new KVCacheTensor schema, plus the kv_cache_allocation_context kwarg.
  2. [0.29 bump] Re-target the slot-mapping patch (ComputeSlotMappingKernel.kernel); delete dead overrides (get_dp_padding, use_cuda_graph, profiler activity map).
  3. [0.29 bump] Test plugin: upstream attention-backend KV layout rewrite (#51718); declare supported_kv_cache_layouts().
  4. [0.31 bump] Override TorchSpyrePlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides (don't inherit the CpuPlatform _C impls); max_num_active_reqs scheduler fix; torch-threads audit; profile(**kwargs).
  5. Platform hardening: reject spec decode, watermark, HiSparse, batch-sharded sampling, PCP, cudagraph_mm_encoder, thinking budget and trace replay; assert use_v2_model_runner; inline _copy_pooler_output_to_cpu.
  6. Pure-numpy/torch MRV2 kernel library + dispatcher registration + coverage test, written to be upstreamable (aligned with vllm#55847, so it also serves vLLM's Power, Z and Triton-less Arm CPU backends).
  7. MRV2 runner skeleton (device=cpu, stream/event placeholders, worker selection flag, Triton-gate workaround), decoder eager TP1.
  8. SpyreSampler on MRV2 (sort-free top-k/top-p, Gumbel/murmur3 port, logprobs, structured outputs via xgrammar CPU).
  9. SpyreBucketManager + warmup/compile integration (suppress the stock compile, attention recording, compile guard, dummy-run redesign). Needs vLLM #59079.
  10. KV cache + attention groups on MRV2 (initialize_kv_cache, window split, head-major/token-major, SpyreBlockTables).
  11. Pooling/encoder on MRV2 (SpyrePoolingRunner, SpyreEncoderOnlyModelState, sync output).
  12. Multimodal on MRV2 (wrapper before init_model_state, Spyre inputs_embeds, self-copy check on hardware).
  13. TP>1, FP8 and LoRA parity on MRV2.
  14. Upstream engagement:
    • comment on #57684 and #55847 (Power, Z and Arm are affected the same way);
    • contribute our kernels;
    • ask for a platform-capability Triton-gate opt-in rather than one keyed on is_cpu();
    • review or co-author #52719/#53895.
  15. Flip the default to V2; delete the V1 runner and shims; update the docs and the upgrade-vllm procedure.

Test strategy

  • While both runners exist, parametrize the e2e / attention / pooling / distributed suites over runner=v1|v2. Compare golden outputs between V1 and V2 on Spyre: token-exact greedy for decoders, cosine for pooling. Expect seeded-sampling differences from the RNG change.
  • Assert that compile_guard records zero new graphs after warmup on V2 (bucket regressions surface immediately).
  • Kernel-override unit tests run on CPU with no Spyre needed, so most of phases 1–2 are covered by regular CI. Remember that "all green" on a non-Spyre host proves nothing for device paths, so keep the hardware-gated jobs.
  • Upstream MRV2 tests worth adopting once a Spyre MRV2 runner exists: tests/v1/worker/test_attn_utils.py, test_gpu_batch_ordering.py and test_gpu_model_runner_v2.py (mostly mocked), and tests/config/test_v2_model_runner_selection.py if #58703 lands. The current upstream_tests.yaml entries are end-to-end and exercise whichever runner is active, so they are the regression net.
  • Local tests tied to V1 internals that need rewriting: test_compile_guard_warmup_coverage.py, test_warmup_logits_widths.py, test_spyre_cpu_gpu_buffer.py, test_spyre_pooler_select_rows.py, test_attn_group_window_split.py, test_spyre_topk_sampler.py, test_encoder_inline_dispatch.py, test_encoder_dense_grid.py, and test_scheduler.py (for 0.31).

Upstream threads to track

  • #57684: support path for platform plugins that can't install Triton (unanswered; worth commenting).
  • #51212: RFC MRV2 Pluggable Design (L1 dispatcher is done; L2 component factory is open; L3 hooks are contested).
  • #52719 / #53895: platform-provided runner component factory (open).
  • #58703: Platform.supports_v2_model_runner() (open). Useful for the stopgap, but it points the opposite way from what the port needs.
  • #50612: TPU exemption from the Triton gate (open, precedent).
  • #55847: CPU torch fallbacks for 13 MRV2 kernels (open). Possible shared code.
  • #43048: Triton kernel dispatcher (merged, v0.31). #59079: MRV2 + STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE (merged, 0.32).
  • #47172 MRV2 remaining TODOs · #41286 V1→V2 migration roadmap · v0.29.0 release notes · MRV2 design doc (no separate announcement blog was found; the design doc is the canonical write-up).

Method & caveats

  • Nine parallel research passes, each with a slice: MRV2 architecture and ecosystem; setup mapping; execution mapping; worker/platform/stopgap; attention/KV; batching/input prep; sampling/pooling/MM; compile/warmup/TP; history and strategy. Their raw findings are in the appendices below, with file:line references against tag checkouts of vLLM v0.28.0, v0.29.0, v0.31.0 and main@bab0ac61f.
  • Static reading only. No vLLM was installed and nothing ran on Spyre, so every "verify on hardware" item is genuinely unverified. Effort figures are rough engineer-time estimates, not measurements.
  • Where slices disagreed, I checked against the code. One case: UVA. Our platform's _enum is OOT, so is_cpu() is False and MRV2 takes the NonUvaBuffer path.
  • Line numbers in spyre-inference refer to 8403f33. Upstream main moves daily, so treat main line numbers as approximate.

Appendices: raw findings per slice

Click to expand. These are the unedited notes from each research pass; they contain more detail and file:line references than the summary above.

A1. MRV2 architecture, runner selection, CUDA dependencies, ecosystem, churn (01_mrv2_architecture.md)

01 — MRV2 architecture, runner selection, CUDA dependencies, ecosystem, churn

Scope: spyre-inference issue #906 (migrate TorchSpyreModelRunner off V1 GPUModelRunner). Paths are relative to each vLLM checkout under /home/senuser/.claude/jobs/709f9980/tmp/ (vllm-v0.28.0, vllm-v0.29.0, vllm-v0.31.0, vllm-main = bab0ac61f, 2026-10-08). MRV2 code lives in vllm/v1/worker/gpu/. "V1 runner" = vllm/v1/worker/gpu_model_runner.py.

0. TL;DR

  • MRV2 has been in-tree since 2025-11-21 (#25266). It became the default for dense generate models from v0.22.0 (first tag after #39337 "oracle ... qwen3 dense by default", 2026-05-14; in v0.21.1rc0), for pooling models (#48290, v0.28.1), and for all models in v0.29.0 (#53183, 2026-08-27). The pinned v0.28.0 already defaults to V2 for non-MoE generate models when Triton is present.
  • v0.29.0 release notes: "we are considering Model Runner V1 deprecated and are targeting v0.32 for its removal. We do not intend to accept any more MRV1-specific improvements." No runtime deprecation warning is emitted when V1 runs (only a code comment at config/vllm.py:3021 on main).
  • Spyre today never reaches MRV2: spyre-inference forbids Triton (pyproject.toml:77-78, "triton; sys_platform == 'never'"), so HAS_TRITON=False and VllmConfig.use_v2_model_runner silently returns False ("Model Runner V2 requires Triton; using the V1 model runner instead"). Forcing VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1 raises ValueError("Model Runner V2 requires Triton.") (_validate_v2_model_runner, main config/vllm.py:3242). Also the worker is spyre's own (TorchSpyreWorker.init_device builds TorchSpyreModelRunner directly), so the stock Worker._make_model_runner switch (main gpu_worker.py:542-558) is bypassed anyway.
  • But use_v2_model_runner is not only a runner switch — the scheduler, async scheduler, input processor, Worker and several connectors branch on it (different SchedulerOutput protocol, e.g. resumed requests sent as new requests, all_token_ids behaviour). An MRV2 Spyre runner therefore needs vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner == True, which today requires Triton (or an upstream change such as #58703/#55847-style relaxation, or a hack).
  • MRV2 is deeply GPU-native: 95 @triton.jit kernels in v0.31.0 (97 on main, 31 files), UVA views of pinned CPU memory, torch.cuda.Stream/Event for async D2H output copy, a CudaGraphManager that calls torch.cuda.CUDAGraph(). ~13–20 Triton kernels sit on the per-step path even without spec-decode. There is no non-Triton fallback inside MRV2 itself; the only escape hatches are (a) NonUvaBuffer for no-pinned-memory platforms (#56908, v0.30), (b) the new Triton kernel dispatcher register_kernels + Platform.register_triton_kernel_overrides() (#43048, v0.31.0), and (c) global monkey-patching (what in-tree CPU MRV2 does in vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py).
  • No platform factory hook exists in MRV2 (RFC #51212 / PR #53895 open, PR #58703 open). The only seams are: subclassing GPUModelRunner (in-tree CPU/XPU/MMEncoder and vllm-ascend all do this), model.get_model_state_cls() (model-provided ModelState), pcp_manager_cls property, attention backend / metadata builder, kernel overrides via the dispatcher.
  • API churn is high: v0.28→main touched ~12.5k lines in gpu/ (model_runner.py ±1.1k lines, sample/ ±2.7k); 47–56 commits per release touch gpu/. Public method names on GPUModelRunner are stable; signatures and internals move each release (e.g. prepare_inputs gained num_active_loras in v0.31; initialize_kv_cache, capture_model, profile_run signatures changed in v0.29/v0.31). RFC #51212 itself says "No backwards compatibility currently, since v2 is still under develop."

1. MRV2 architecture (v0.31.0 and main)

The module docstring at gpu/model_runner.py:3-17 sets the policy: the runner "must only contain code that is common to every model ... Be paranoid about changing this file ... Model-specific behavior belongs in the appropriate model-specific files." Design doc: docs/design/model_runner_v2.md (main, 206 lines) — sections: persistent batch decoupled from per-step inputs; async-first ("the core model execution loop is a CUDA stream with no CPU synchronization points"); removing the async barrier via pinned temp copies; StagedWriteTensor; GPU-native input prep in Triton + UVA; Triton-native sampler (Gumbel-max, top-k logprobs before softmax); modularity; execute_model handles dummy runs; explicit CUDA graph management.

__init__ (v0.31 :185-350) builds, by direct instantiation (no factories): torch.cuda.Stream output_copy_stream (:212), EncoderCache, get_ec_connector, init_speculator, DraftTokensHandler, RequestState (:291), InputBuffers (:301), PPHandler, LoraState, EPLBController, offloader. load_model (v0.31 :367-527) loads the model, calls init_model_state(...) (:413), then builds Sampler / GPUWatermarkSampler, RejectionSampler, PromptLogprobsWorker, StructuredOutputsWorker, PoolingRunner. On main it then calls compile_model_with_stock_torch when mode == STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE (#59079, 2026-09-28; not in v0.31.0, which lists "stock torch.compile" as unsupported → V1 fallback). initialize_kv_cache (v0.31 :578-771) runs init_attn_backend, builds BlockTables, PCP manager, ubatch runner, resolves cudagraph mode, constructs ModelCudaGraphManager, calls init_kv_cache, and creates the KV connector. reload_weights/update_config still delegate to V1 GPUModelRunner methods (v0.31 :538-553: "TODO(Wentao): Use full version instead of import when fully migrated to v2") — so V1 removal must first move these.

1.2 Step lifecycle

Worker.execute_model → runner.execute_model(scheduler_output) (v0.31 :1676):

  1. State update (non-dummy): update_pp_decode_requests, finish_requests (preempted == finished), free_states, add_requests (stages writes into RequestState, ModelState, BlockTables, Sampler then apply_staged_writes), update_requests (append block ids, CPU num_computed_tokens_np, zero new blocks, CoW copies), block_tables.apply_staged_writes().
  2. gather_batch_req_state (CPU, numpy; sorts req ids via sort_batch_req_ids, decides prefill/decode, uniform-decode token count).
  3. dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp(cudagraph_manager, ...) → BatchExecutionDescriptor (cg_mode, padded num_tokens/num_reqs) + DP sync.
  4. prepare_inputs (:1318) → InputBatch: idx_mapping H2D, query_start_loc H2D, then Triton: prepare_prefill_inputs, prepare_pos_seq_lens, combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens (+expand_idx_mapping with drafts). Input ids for decode are read on-GPU from req_states.last_sampled_tokens — the CPU never knows the sampled token at prep time.
  5. prepare_attn → BlockTables.gather_block_tables + compute_slot_mappings (Triton).
  6. model_state.preprocess_state, LoRA activation, model_state.prepare_attn(...) → attn_utils.build_attn_metadata (CommonAttentionMetadata with seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound, always common_prefix_len=0; calls each group's attn_metadata_builder.build(...), or build_for_cudagraph_capture).
  7. model_state.prepare_inputs_embeds (MM/prompt embeds), model_state.prepare_inputs (M-RoPE positions etc.).
  8. Forward: FULL → cudagraph_manager.run_fullgraph(batch_desc); PIECEWISE → run_pw_graph; NONE → self.model(**model_inputs) under set_forward_context.
  9. Stash ExecuteModelState; return None (last PP rank) / IntermediateTensors.

Worker.sample_tokens → runner.sample_tokens(grammar_output) (v0.31 :2050): sample() (compute_logits, grammar bitmask Triton, Sampler.__call__ / RejectionSampler), prompt logprobs, build ModelRunnerOutput, AsyncOutput (D2H copies on output_copy_stream with torch.cuda.Event(blocking=True); get_output() syncs the event and builds lists — async_utils.py:115-218), postprocess_sampled → Triton post_update (writes num_computed_tokens.gpu, last_sampled_tokens, output_bin_counts, all_token_ids), speculator propose, draft tokens handler, KV connector post_forward. Pooling: pool() (:2249) with AsyncPoolingOutput. The worker calls pool() when use_v2_model_runner and is_pooling_model (main gpu_worker.py:1297-1323).

1.4 Extension seams (is there a platform hook?)

There is no platform hook for swapping MRV2 components on main. current_platform is touched in gpu/ only for is_xpu (pp_utils), is_rocm (model_runner), graph_pool_handle/ get_global_graph_pool (cudagraph), device_type (NonUvaBuffer). What exists:

  1. Subclass GPUModelRunner and override methods — what in-tree CPU (vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py, 16 lines), XPU (XPUModelRunnerV2, wrapping super().__init__ in _torch_cuda_wrapper()), MMEncoderModelRunner, and vllm-ascend (worker/v2/model_runner.py:125) do. Components are built by direct instantiation in __init__/load_model/initialize_kv_cache, so replacing one means overriding (or re-doing after) those methods.
  2. Model-provided ModelState via model.get_model_state_cls() (model_states/__init__.py).
  3. pcp_manager_cls property.
  4. Attention backend (get_attn_backend_cls) and its AttentionMetadataBuilder — unchanged API.
  5. Triton kernel overrides: vllm/triton_utils/dispatcher.py:147 register_kernels({...}) with KernelOverride:39 (keeps kernel[grid](...) launch syntax; rebinds every module attribute that aliased the original), and Platform.register_triton_kernel_overrides() (platforms/interface.py:376) called from worker_base.py:282 before the worker class is resolved. Merged #43048 (2026-09-25, first in v0.31.0). CPU uses it (platforms/cpu.py:673-710) but only for V1-path kernels. This does not lift the HAS_TRITON gate; without Triton, @triton.jit functions are TritonPlaceholder objects and the dispatcher reads their signature via .func (dispatcher.py:30-36), so overriding is possible but MRV2 still won't be selected.
  6. Proposed only: RFC #51212 "Model Runner V2 Pluggable Design" (open, 2026-08-06; kernel dispatcher + Platform.get_v2_runner_components() + optional hooks; LucasWilkinson: Layer 3 "too invasive"), PR #53895 (open, factory), PR #58703 (open, Platform.supports_v2_model_runner() opt-out).

2. Runner selection

2.1 VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER and VllmConfig.use_v2_model_runner

  • Env var: envs.py:306 (main) — tri-state bool | None (unset = auto).
  • Property: v0.28.0 config/vllm.py:615; v0.29.0 :645; v0.31.0 :701; main :709.

Main logic (config/vllm.py:709-768):

  1. attention_config.hisparse_config set → V2 (raises if env=0).
  2. watermark_config set → V2 (overrides env=0).
  3. env var set → return it.
  4. ROCm + ROCM_DEFAULT_MRV1_ARCHITECTURES (:76: DeepseekV32, DeepseekV4, GlmMoeDsa) → V1 unless V1 can't serve it.
  5. not HAS_TRITON → V1 with warning "Model Runner V2 requires Triton; using the V1 model runner instead."
  6. _get_v2_model_runner_unsupported_features() non-empty → V1 with warning.
  7. else V2.

Then __post_init__ (main :2048-2052): V2 → _disable_cudagraphs_for_v2_stock_torch_compile() (:3220) and _validate_v2_model_runner() (:3242: raises if no Triton or unsupported features); V1 → _validate_v1_model_runner() (raises on V1-unsupported features). The platform's check_and_update_config runs before that (:2023), apply_config_platform_defaults at :1792.

V2-unsupported (auto-fallback to V1) conditions on main (:2969-3008): PP with external_launcher; spec methods suffix, medusa, mlp_speculator, custom_class; EAGLE parallel drafting (P-Eagle); heterogeneous-vocab draft models; DBO feature combos (_get_dbo_unsupported_features :3140 — DBO itself unless env explicitly set). Plus "no Triton".

v0.31.0 list (:3051-3103) additionally: stock torch.compile, sequence parallelism (TP>1), ngram/ngram_gpu, elastic EP, mamba_cache_mode='all'. v0.29.0 list (:2539-2613): stock torch.compile, SP, external_launcher+PP, ngram, several spec methods, P-Eagle, EAGLE3+PP, DBO, elastic EP, custom logits processors, KV-sharing fast prefill. v0.28.0 (:2369-2477) adds: PCP-related, adaptive verification combos, prompt embeds; and v0.28.0 only defaults to V2 for _is_default_v2_model_runner_model() (generate runner, non-MoE unless whitelisted, not hybrid/attention-free) (:690-712).

V1-unsupported (forces V2 / errors) on main (:3010-3061): PCP>1, PP>1 + async scheduling (V1 rejected since #56250), DSpark, adaptive verification, mixed SWA DFlash drafts, DFlash candidate heads, diffusion models, batch-sharded sampling. Also V2-only features elsewhere: HiSparse, watermarking, AuxOutput/routed-experts, prompt_logprob_token_ids (input_processor.py:164), CPU EC connector, RecoverSSM align mode.

2.2 Where the worker picks the runner

  • GPU: Worker._make_model_runner() main gpu_worker.py:542-558 (V2 → vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner.GPUModelRunner or MMEncoderModelRunner; else V1). Worker caches self.use_v2_model_runner at :231 and branches on it at :498, :917 (JIT kernel warmup), :1003 (V1-only sampler warmup), :1075 (graph-capture profiler), :1297/:1323 (pool()).
  • CPU: cpu_worker.py:161-177 (V2 → vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py CPUModelRunner(GPUModelRunnerV2)).
  • XPU: xpu_worker.py:217-225.
  • Other consumers of the flag: v1/core/sched/scheduler.py:352,1417,1449,1731 (V2: resumed requests are re-sent as NewRequestData with all token ids; V1-only prev_step_scheduled_req_ids), async_scheduler.py:52 (PP decode stagger), engine/input_processor.py:99,164 (logits-processor validator, prompt_logprob_token_ids), compilation/backends.py:1097, config/compilation.py (cudagraph mode resolution), offloading/EC connectors, several models (longcat, qwen3_dflash, deepseek_v4, kimi_k3).

2.3 Effect on OOT platforms (spyre)

  • spyre sets parallel_config.worker_cls = "spyre_inference.v1.worker.spyre_worker.TorchSpyreWorker" (spyre_inference/platform.py:732-735); TorchSpyreWorker.init_device constructs TorchSpyreModelRunner itself (spyre_worker.py:163). So runner selection in the stock worker is bypassed. However the scheduler/engine side reads vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner, so the flag must agree with whatever runner spyre ships. Today it does (no Triton → False).
  • Stopgap when bumping to 0.29–0.31: nothing to do (no Triton → auto V1), or set VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=0 explicitly to silence the warning path. Caveat: V2-only features (HiSparse, watermark, routed experts) force V2 and then fail validation — irrelevant to spyre today.
  • After v0.32 (V1 removal): the import from vllm.v1.worker.gpu_model_runner import GPUModelRunner (spyre_model_runner.py), vllm.v1.worker.block_table, cpu_model_runner._torch_cuda_wrapper, and the V1 branches in scheduler/Worker are expected to disappear. MRV2 still borrows reload_weights/update_config from V1 (v0.31 model_runner.py:538-553), and LoRAModelRunnerMixin is shared, so removal will need some reshuffling upstream; exact shape unknown.
  • To use MRV2, spyre needs use_v2_model_runner=True without Triton. Options: (a) upstream relaxation for OOT platforms (no PR does exactly this today; #55847 relaxes it for CPU only; #58703 is the opposite direction — explicit opt-out), (b) install triton (forbidden in pyproject — reason should be confirmed; triton-cpu is what in-tree CPU uses, x86_64 only), (c) monkeypatch vllm.config.vllm.HAS_TRITON from the plugin (fragile).

3. Hard CUDA dependencies in MRV2

3.1 Triton kernels

grep -c @triton.jit: v0.31.0 = 95, main = 97 across 31 files. No in-MRV2 torch fallback. Per-step path for a plain decoder without spec-decode / LoRA / PP (what spyre would hit):

Kernel File:line (main) When
_apply_write_kernel buffer_utils.py:301 every apply_staged_writes (RequestState, BlockTables, sampler states)
_prepare_prefill_inputs_kernel input_batch.py:270 any step with prefill
_prepare_pos_seq_lens_kernel input_batch.py:347 every step
_combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens_kernel input_batch.py:405 every step (decode input ids + logits_indices)
_post_update_kernel input_batch.py:556 every sample step
_post_update_num_computed_tokens_kernel input_batch.py:658 pooling / non-last PP
_gather_block_tables_kernel block_table.py:247 every step
_compute_slot_mappings_kernel block_table.py:287 every step
_temperature_kernel, _gumbel_sample_kernel (+ murmur3 helpers) sample/gumbel.py:22,274 every sample step
_penalties_kernel, _bincount_kernel sample/penalties.py:105,217 penalties set (bincount on add_request)
_min_p_kernel sample/min_p.py:9 min_p set
_topk_log_softmax_kernel, _ranks_kernel, _fill_logprob_token_ids_kernel sample/logprob.py:17,62,193 logprobs requested
_bias_kernel / _bad_words_kernel sample/logit_bias.py, sample/bad_words.py logit_bias / allowed tokens / bad words
_prompt_logprobs_token_ids_kernel sample/prompt_logprob.py prompt logprobs
_apply_grammar_bitmask_kernel structured_outputs.py:162 structured output
_num_nans_kernel metrics/logits.py NaN metrics
_prepare_rope_positions_kernel mm/rope.py M-RoPE models
_apply_prompt_embeds_kernel model_states/prompt_embeds.py prompt embeds
_expand_idx_mapping_kernel, _get_num_sampled_and_rejected_kernel, rejection sampler (12 in rejection_sampler_utils.py), speculators (eagle/autoregressive/dflash/dflash2/mtp/...) various spec decode only
_scatter/_fill_num_accepted_kernel, _postprocess_recoverssm_align_kernel model_states/mamba_hybrid.py, recoverssm.py hybrid models
_dcp_local_seq_lens_kernel, cp_local_slot cp_utils.py DCP/PCP
watermark (philox_*), trace_replay, thinking_budget, batch_shard, acceptance_estimator, _compact_sampling_mask_kernel sample/*, spec_decode/* opt-in features

Note: these kernels are written against GPU pointer semantics (e.g. _apply_write_kernel loads pointers from a pointer table, _load_ptr at buffer_utils.py:339; block-table kernels index _make_ptr_tensor pointer arrays, block_table.py:91). Torch replacements are feasible (PR #55847 does 13 of them for CPU) but they must reproduce semantics like in-place writes into persistent state.

3.2 UVA / pinned memory

  • UvaBuffer (buffer_utils.py:29-38): torch.zeros(..., pin_memory=True) + get_accelerator_view_from_cpu_tensor (CUDA/XPU only, utils/torch_utils.py:905-925).
  • NonUvaBuffer (buffer_utils.py:41-58, #56908): explicit CPU→device copy on every .uva() — selected when not is_uva_available(). StagedWriteTensor(uva_instead_of_gpu=True) (used for RequestState.all_token_ids, states.py:34) falls back to a device tensor when UVA is unavailable.
  • pin_memory=PIN_MEMORY in structured_outputs.py:127, mm/encoder_runner.py:155,221, model_states/encoder_only.py:130, model_states/encoder_decoder.py:167, pool/pooling_runner.py:123.
  • async_tensor_h2d (utils/torch_utils.py) used for idx_mapping, query_start_loc, cu_num_logits.

3.3 Streams / events

  • model_runner.py:224 output_copy_stream = torch.cuda.Stream(self.device); main_stream (:575-577) = torch.cuda.current_stream.
  • async_utils.py:36-45 timing torch.cuda.Event(enable_timing=True); :134,:232 torch.cuda.Event(blocking=True); :269-277 torch.cuda.set_stream.
  • structured_outputs.py:91,106,125,134; spec_decode/utils.py:32-60 (copy stream, record_stream); spec_decode/adaptive_verification.py:142,177,276; pp_utils.py:23,73-74,179-280; ubatch_utils.py:327-329,478-479 (torch.cuda.current_blas_handle).
  • In-tree workarounds: XPU aliases torch.cuda.Stream/current_stream/stream/Event to torch.xpu.* in _torch_cuda_wrapper (xpu_model_runner.py:60-71); CPU globally replaces them with placeholders, fakes pin_memory, no-ops torch.accelerator.synchronize, replaces async_tensor_h2d and swaps gpu_buffer_utils.UvaBuffer for a plain-CPU one, at import of vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py (imported first thing by cpu_worker.py:5). Spyre already uses V1's cpu_model_runner._torch_cuda_wrapper.

3.4 CUDA graphs

  • cudagraph_utils.py:175 dict[BatchExecutionDescriptor, torch.cuda.CUDAGraph], capture at :484-508 (torch.cuda.CUDAGraph(), torch.cuda.graph(...)), :906 graph_pool_handle, mm/encoder_runner.py:74 encoder graphs, spec-decode graph managers.
  • Non-CUDA path: cudagraph_mode=NONE → dispatch returns NONE, needs_capture() False, eager self.model(**model_inputs) (v0.31 model_runner.py:1985-1987). On main, STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE forces cudagraph_mode=NONE (config/vllm.py:3220-3240) and load_model calls model.compile(fullgraph=True, backend=...) (compilation/wrapper.py, compile_model_with_stock_torch) — note this is a whole-model compile, unlike spyre's per-block compile in _compile_for_spyre; spyre would need to override/skip it.
  • capture_model (:1019/:1034) uses torch.accelerator.get_memory_info /empty_cache (platform-agnostic) but the worker only calls it when not enforce_eager.

3.5 Other GPU-isms relevant to Spyre

  • Request state (token ids, num_computed_tokens, last sampled tokens) lives on device and is mutated by device kernels; decode input ids are produced on device from the last sample. Spyre's V1 runner does input prep on CPU (with SpyreCpuGpuBuffer, _FuncWrapper Triton shims, _compute_slot_mapping_kernel torch replacement). Running all of these as tiny torch-spyre ops would hit FallbackWarning/CPU fallback and many small device launches; the natural Spyre mapping is "device" = CPU for bookkeeping tensors, copying only model inputs to Spyre — which cuts against MRV2's self.device-everywhere construction.
  • CommonAttentionMetadata from MRV2 carries seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound (upper bound, not exact) and GPU seq_lens; common_prefix_len=0 always (no cascade).

4. Ecosystem (non-CUDA backends)

(Condensed from a sub-survey; refs against main unless noted.)

Backend Approach Key links Status
vLLM CPU (in-tree) Two runners: V1 cpu_model_runner.py CPUModelRunner(V1 GPUModelRunner); V2 = 16-line vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py CPUModelRunner(GPUModelRunnerV2) overriding only warming_up_model; relies on triton-cpu wheel (#52092, x86_64) + global shim cpu/shm.py #43225 (reverted), #46365, #50073, #52092, #58140, #56908, #55847 (open: torch fallbacks for 13 per-step MRV2 kernels, relaxes HAS_TRITON for CPU only) MRV2 on x86 with triton-cpu; Arm asks for #55847 (10-08)

Key upstream threads:

  • https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/57684 — "Support path for platform plugins that cannot install Triton (MRV2 requires it)". Q1 (V1 support window after v0.32) and Q2 (whether non-Triton work covers plugins that replace the runner) unanswered by maintainers as of 10-08.
  • https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/51212 — RFC MRV2 Pluggable Design (open).
  • https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/53895 — platform-provided runner component factory (open, no review).
  • https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/58703 — Platform.supports_v2_model_runner (open).
  • https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/43048 — Triton kernel dispatcher (merged 09-25).
  • https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/pull/55847 — CPU torch fallbacks for MRV2 kernels (open, conflicts).
  • https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/45133 — RFC Triton Kernel Dispatcher (closed 09-30).

Pattern: every backend that actually runs MRV2 (CPU, XPU, Ascend) has some Triton (triton-cpu, triton-xpu, triton-ascend). No backend runs MRV2 without Triton today.

5. API stability (churn)

Changed lines (+/-, excluding headers) under vllm/v1/worker/gpu/:

Range total model_runner.py model_states/ sample/ attn_utils cudagraph_utils input_batch states block_table buffer_utils
v0.28.0 → v0.29.0 3104 277 443 877 392 277 0 0 64 0
v0.29.0 → v0.31.0 6517 745 92 1465 243 211 47 5 21 66
v0.31.0 → main 4410 133 122 467 14 35 0 7 4 4
v0.28.0 → main 12477 1095 621 2733 621 505 47 12 85 66

Commits touching gpu/ (model_runner.py): v0.28→v0.29: 47 (18); v0.29→v0.30: 48 (26); v0.30→v0.31: 56 (27); v0.31→main (6 days): 31 (15). Total gpu/ size: 18.7k (v0.28) → 20.6k (v0.29) → 25.0k (v0.31) → 26.1k LOC (main). model_runner.py: 2018 → 2136 → 2410 → 2454. (V1 gpu_model_runner.py for comparison: 8011 → 7502.)

Public method surface of GPUModelRunner (names) is fairly stable:

  • v0.28→v0.29: initialize_kv_cache gained is_profiling, kv_cache_allocation_context; post_kv_cache_wake_up removed.
  • v0.29→v0.31: init_routed_experts_capturer removed; profile_run(randomize_inputs); capture_model(*, profile_only); needs_cudagraph_capture() added; prepare_inputs gained num_active_loras.
  • v0.31→main: dp_size/dp_rank properties, release_late_interaction_query_cache, _get_drafter_hidden_states; stock torch.compile in load_model.

The stable files are the low-level bookkeeping (states.py, input_batch.py, block_table.py, buffer_utils.py); the churn is concentrated in sample/, spec_decode/, model_states/, attn_utils, cudagraph_utils and the body of execute_model/sample_tokens/load_model/initialize_kv_cache — i.e. exactly the methods a Spyre subclass would need to override. Expect each vLLM bump to require re-syncing overridden method bodies unless the overrides are kept to a few narrow seams.

6. Risks and open questions

  1. Triton gate is the blocker, not the runner code. No upstream path lets a Triton-less OOT platform select MRV2. Need either (a) an upstream change (e.g. generalize #55847's CPU-only relaxation to a platform method, or the #51212/#53895 track), (b) install triton/triton-cpu in the spyre env (why is it forbidden? torch-spyre/inductor interaction should be confirmed), or (c) a plugin-side monkeypatch of HAS_TRITON plus register_kernels overrides for ~15–20 per-step kernels.
  2. What exactly gets removed in v0.32? If gpu_model_runner.py, V1 gpu_input_batch.py, block_table.py, cpu_model_runner._torch_cuda_wrapper and the scheduler's V1 protocol all go, spyre cannot stay on a vendored V1 copy cheaply (the scheduler protocol differs). No removal PR/RFC found yet; #57684 Q1 is unanswered. Worth commenting there.
  3. Device placement mismatch: MRV2 keeps request state on self.device and mutates it with kernels each step. On Spyre, small bookkeeping ops would be torch-spyre launches or CPU fallbacks. A Spyre subclass likely wants bookkeeping on CPU and only model inputs on Spyre — needs an override of construction in __init__/initialize_kv_cache (no factory hook).
  4. Compile model: main's MRV2 STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE path does a whole-model model.compile (v0.31.0 doesn't support stock compile at all → would fall back to V1). Spyre's per-block compile and warmup-time attention recording must replace load_model's tail and capture_model/warmup.
  5. Async-first output path (AsyncOutput with CUDA events/streams) needs Spyre equivalents or placeholders; spyre's synchronous model wrapper (_SpyreModelWrapper moving tensors CPU↔Spyre) would make "async" a no-op but still must satisfy the AsyncModelRunnerOutput contract.
  6. API churn ~3–6k lines per release in gpu/; pin carefully and keep overrides minimal.
  7. Timeline: v0.32 likely ~mid/late October 2026 given the 2-week cadence; the removal target could slip, but MRV1 already gets no fixes (v0.31 rejects MRV1+PP+async+structured output).
A2. Runner lifecycle / setup half: V1 → MRV2 mapping (02_runner_setup_mapping.md)

02 — TorchSpyreModelRunner lifecycle/setup half: V1 -> MRV2 API mapping

Scope: the module-level helpers and setup-side methods of spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_model_runner.py (abbrev. SMR) and spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_worker.py (abbrev. SW), mapped against

  • V1 base: vllm/v1/worker/gpu_model_runner.py at v0.28.0 (abbrev. V1)
  • MRV2 base: vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py at v0.31.0 (abbrev. V2), plus gpu/attn_utils.py, gpu/block_table.py, gpu/buffer_utils.py, gpu/input_batch.py, gpu/model_states/*, gpu/async_utils.py; differences on vllm-main called out.
  • Worker: vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py (abbrev. GW).

Difficulty buckets: trivial (no change / delete / rename), re-target (same idea, different hook or attribute), redesign (MRV2 has no seam where we hook today; needs a new mechanism).

0. Headline structural differences that drive most rows

  1. No _make_buffer / CpuGpuBuffer dual-buffer pattern in MRV2. V2 allocates every persistent buffer as a plain torch.zeros(..., device=self.device) (InputBuffers, gpu/input_batch.py:19-36; RequestState; BlockTables, gpu/block_table.py:17-89; EncoderRunner.inputs_embeds, gpu/mm/encoder_runner.py:58; DefaultModelState.inputs_embeds, gpu/model_states/default.py:49) or as StagedWriteTensor / UvaBackedTensor / UvaBufferPool (gpu/buffer_utils.py). The "self.device = CPU, float .gpu on Spyre, int .gpu aliased to CPU" trick that SMR is built around (SMR:15-33, 209-263, 602-614, 1900-1925) has no seam in V2. Every buffer is on exactly one device: self.device.
  2. MRV2 is Triton-native. Persistent-state updates and per-step input prep are Triton kernels (_gather_block_tables_kernel, _compute_slot_mappings_kernel in gpu/block_table.py:242,282; _apply_write_kernel in gpu/buffer_utils.py:301; _prepare_prefill_inputs_kernel, _prepare_pos_seq_lens_kernel, _post_update_kernel, ... in gpu/input_batch.py:270-709). The block-table kernels take raw data_ptr() pointer tensors (_make_ptr_tensor, block_table.py:91-95), so a pure-PyTorch drop-in for the kernel symbol (what _compute_slot_mapping_impl + _FuncWrapper do today) is not possible; the methods that launch them must be overridden instead. vLLM's own config refuses MRV2 without Triton: VllmConfig.use_v2_model_runner returns False when not HAS_TRITON (config/vllm.py:745-749 @v0.31, same on main) and _validate_v2_model_runner raises "Model Runner V2 requires Triton." if forced (config/vllm.py:3315-3318 @v0.31). The in-tree CPU MRV2 (vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py, 16 lines) only works because it uses Triton's CPU backend (triton_utils/importing.py:37-49) plus global torch.cuda monkeypatches in vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py.
  3. Attention-backend / KV-cache init is a single method calling module-level functions. V2 initialize_kv_cache (V2:578-771) calls init_attn_backend(...) (attn_utils.py:189, groups + kernel block sizes + metadata builders in one go) and init_kv_cache(...) (attn_utils.py:367, allocate_kv_cache + bind_kv_cache_to_layers). There are no overridable initialize_attn_backend, initialize_metadata_builders, may_reinitialize_input_batch, or initialize_kv_cache_tensors methods. Both functions are imported by name into vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner, so the only hook points are (a) override all of initialize_kv_cache, or (b) monkeypatch the names in the model_runner module namespace.
  4. Model-dependent components are built inside load_model. V2 load_model (V2:367-527) creates self.model_state = init_model_state(...) (holds a reference to self.model), self.decode_query_len, self.sampler (None until then, V2:324), rejection_sampler, prompt_logprobs_worker, structured_outputs_worker, pooling_runner = PoolingRunner(self.model, ...), EPLB registration, intermediate_tensors, get_offloader().post_init(). On main it also ends with compile_model_with_stock_torch(self.model, ...) when mode is STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE (main model_runner.py:535-536 -> compilation/wrapper.py:53-61, model.compile(fullgraph=True, backend=vllm backend)), which collides with our per-block compile.
  5. Dummy runs go through execute_model(dummy_run=True) and build attention metadata by default. V2 _dummy_run (V2:785-940) has no force_attention; skip_attn is only legal with is_profile. Non-profile dummy runs build metadata from prepare_dummy_attn (V2:1540-1554: zeroed block-table rows + slot mapping filled with PAD_SLOT_ID) and call model_state.prepare_attn (V2:1855-1878). Padding comes from dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp (V2:1740, gpu/dp_utils.py:213) -> CudaGraphManager.dispatch (cudagraph_utils.py:499), not from _determine_batch_execution_and_padding.
  6. CUDA-only objects created at init and used every step: self.output_copy_stream = torch.cuda.Stream(self.device) (V2:220), main_stream = torch.cuda.current_stream (V2:556), AsyncOutput/AsyncPoolingOutput create torch.cuda.Event and switch streams via torch.cuda.set_stream (async_utils.py:121-140, 226-232, 269-277), StepTimingEvents uses torch.cuda.Event (async_utils.py:36-45), torch.accelerator.synchronize/empty_cache/get_memory_info (V2:988, 1038-1039, 1076, 2302, 2332), DeviceMemoryProfiler in load_model (V2:372). SMR's scoped _torch_cuda_wrapper() around super().__init__ (SMR:607) covers only the init-time torch.cuda.Stream; the runtime uses need a process-wide patch (as cpu/shm.py does) or overrides.

1. Module-level helpers and monkeypatches

Ours (file:line) What / why (Spyre reason) V1 base it targets MRV2 equivalent (v0.31; main) Difficulty Notes
_compute_slot_mapping_impl SMR:144-193, _FuncWrapper SMR:196-206, installed in __init__ SMR:632-642 into vllm.v1.worker.block_table._compute_slot_mapping_kernel Pure-torch slot-mapping replacement; Triton is mocked (VLLM_TARGET_DEVICE=empty, no _C.so). _FuncWrapper fakes kernel[grid](...). V1 MultiGroupBlockTable/BlockTable.compute_slot_mapping launching _compute_slot_mapping_kernel[(num_reqs+1,)] (v0.28 block_table.py:195) No drop-in. BlockTables.compute_slot_mappings (gpu/block_table.py:195-227) launches _compute_slot_mappings_kernel[(num_groups, num_reqs+1)] (block_table.py:282) with block_table_ptrs (uint64 data_ptr() tensor) and multi-group strides. Same for gather_block_tables (block_table.py:153-180, _gather_block_tables_kernel) and StagedWriteTensor.apply_write / FusedStagedWriter.apply (buffer_utils.py:196-227, 251, kernel 301). Main adds redirect_writes_to_null_block (block_table.py) — no API change. redesign Patch target module is V1-only; under MRV2 the patch is dead and the real kernels crash/no-op without Triton. Replacement: subclass BlockTables (override compute_slot_mappings, gather_block_tables, apply_staged_writes) and StagedWriteTensor.apply_write in torch; inject by overriding initialize_kv_cache (where BlockTables(...) is constructed, V2:666) or patching vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner.BlockTables. Also idx_mapping == -1 dummy semantics (block_table.py:318-321) must be reproduced. The other input_batch.py Triton kernels belong to the execution slice but share the fix.
SpyreCpuGpuBuffer SMR:209-263 .cpu on CPU, float .gpu on Spyre (fp16), int/bool .gpu aliased to .cpu; async H2D non_blocking=True, caller must _sync_device. Exists because Spyre lacks int32/bool support and the scatter/index bookkeeping must stay on host. vllm.v1.utils.CpuGpuBuffer, produced by V1 _make_buffer (v0.28:1095-1103) None in MRV2's core path. MRV2 uses CpuGpuBuffer only in model_states/mamba_hybrid.py:161 and spec_decode/adaptive_verification.py:169. Host staging is UvaBuffer/NonUvaBuffer/UvaBufferPool/UvaBackedTensor (buffer_utils.py:29-130). Spyre gets NonUvaBuffer (platform is_pin_memory_available() is False, platform.py:583, and device_type="cpu"), whose _uva is a second CPU tensor — harmless. redesign The device-placement decision must be remade for MRV2: either (a) runner device=cpu (current choice) -> all V2 buffers incl. float inputs_embeds / intermediate_tensors on CPU, so the wrapper must also H2D floats; or (b) device=spyre -> int32/bool/int64 bookkeeping tensors on Spyre (not viable today). (a) mirrors the CPU MRV2 precedent. Main changes NonUvaBuffer.uva to copy_(..., non_blocking=True) (buffer_utils.py:57-58 @main): CPU->CPU so still fine.
_make_buffer override SMR:1900-1925 Builds SpyreCpuGpuBuffer by dtype. V1 _make_buffer v0.28:1095; called ~20x in V1 __init__ (v0.28:813-893, 997, 1115) and by V1 block_table.py:105,110 none (0 hits for _make_buffer under gpu/) redesign (dead override) Silently dead under MRV2 — never called.
_set_spyre_compilation_settings SMR:1928-1952 Inductor freezing on only when max_autotune; warns that it defeats per-block sharing. Mirrors CPU _set_global_compilation_settings. none (local ctx manager; V1 cpu_model_runner analog) n/a — pure local trivial Keep as-is.
_compile_granularity, _block_sharing_defeated_by, _is_decoder_attention_like, _is_vision_tower_path, _repeated_block_lists SMR:266-344 Per-block compile selection; checks vllm.utils.torch_utils._USE_LAYERNAME. none n/a trivial _is_decoder_attention_like has a "remove when upgrading to vLLM 0.30" TODO (SMR:293) — independent of MRV2. Confirm _USE_LAYERNAME still exists at the target vLLM.
_SpyreModelWrapper SMR:347-557 Transparent model wrapper: int inputs -> Spyre int64; D2H of outputs for generative; compute_logits pads to warmed row buckets and H2Ds; embed_multimodal moves floats to Spyre + capture_scalar_outputs; embed_input_ids buckets/pads and stages result into upstream's persistent inputs_embeds buffer (d2d self-copy, torch-spyre#4730). V1 call sites: _model_forward / _dummy_run call self.model(...); V1 _preprocess calls self.model.embed_input_ids; execute_model/sample_tokens call self.model.compute_logits; staging buffer self.inputs_embeds.gpu (V1 _make_buffer, v0.28:847) Call sites move: forward in V2 execute_model (V2:1960ff, possibly via cudagraph_manager/ubatch_runner); embed_input_ids from DefaultModelState.prepare_inputs_embeds (model_states/default.py:100-126: self.inputs_embeds[:n] = self.model.embed_input_ids(...)) and EncoderRunner.get_inputs_embeds (mm/encoder_runner.py:294); embed_multimodal from EncoderRunner; compute_logits from V2 sample (V2:1579) and _dummy_sampler_run (V2:942-953). Staging buffer becomes self.model_state.encoder_runner.inputs_embeds (mm) or self.model_state.inputs_embeds (prompt-embeds), allocated on self.device. re-target (1) init_model_state(vllm_config, self.model, ...) and PoolingRunner(self.model, ...) capture whatever self.model is at that moment — construct them with the wrapper, then hand the wrapper the model_state's buffer (circular: set _inputs_embeds_buffer after with object.__setattr__). (2) If runner device=cpu, that buffer is CPU, so the "stage on device, d2d self-copy" trick (SMR:490-537) inverts; the wrapper's __call__ must H2D float inputs_embeds too (today it converts only ints, SMR:392-398). (3) resolve_model_state_cls calls hasattr(model, "get_model_state_cls") and model.modules() (model_states/init.py:28-60) — works through __getattr__, but get_model_state_cls is also the natural hook for a SpyreModelState. (4) get_supported_generation_tasks uses is_text_generation_model(self.model) (runtime_checkable Protocol) on the wrapper, not get_model() — verify it passes via __getattr__.

2. TorchSpyreModelRunner methods (setup half)

Ours (file:line) What / why (Spyre reason) V1 base overridden / super() MRV2 equivalent (v0.31 file:line; main) Difficulty Notes
__init__ SMR:571-642 Stores _spyre_device; pooling encoder tables; super().__init__(cfg, torch.device("cpu")) under _torch_cuda_wrapper(); swaps self.sampler.topk_topp_sampler = SpyreTopKTopPSampler(...); sets use_cuda_graph=False, cascade_attn_enabled=False; spyre_shape_bucketer=None; _spyre_kv_caches={}; installs slot-mapping patch. Main moves RequestState earlier and makes dp_size/dp_rank properties. redesign (1) self.sampler.topk_topp_sampler -> AttributeError: NoneType — the V2 Sampler (gpu/sample/sampler.py:42) has no topk_topp_sampler; it calls apply_top_k_top_p / flashinfer / xpu functions (sampler.py:324-342).
