5. MRV2 code that is CUDA/Triton-only and reachable from setup

  • torch.cuda.Stream(self.device) V2:220; main_stream V2:556; torch.cuda.Event in async_utils.py:36-45,134,232; torch.cuda.set_stream async_utils.py:274-277.
  • DeviceMemoryProfiler() in load_model V2:372 (avoided if we fully override).
  • torch.accelerator.* in profile_run, capture_model, shutdown.
  • Triton kernels constructed/launched from setup objects: BlockTables (built in initialize_kv_cache V2:666; dcp_sharded/pointer tensors at construction), StagedWriteTensor (_apply_write_kernel), FusedStagedWriter (multi-group), plus the per-step kernels in input_batch.py.
  • ModelCudaGraphManager (V2:720) — torch.cuda.CUDAGraph, current_platform.get_global_graph_pool() when cudagraph_mode is non-NONE (cudagraph_utils.py:173-174).
  • init_kv_cache -> allocate_kv_cache single shared backing allocation (unsupported on Spyre).
  • Config gate: HAS_TRITON (config/vllm.py:745-749, 3317).

6. Silently dead overrides under MRV2 (no error, behaviour lost)

_make_buffer, initialize_attn_backend (=> sliding-window split lost), initialize_kv_cache_tensors (replaced by a crash, not silence), _init_device_properties, _sync_device (base never calls), get_dp_padding (already dead on v0.28), _determine_batch_execution_and_padding (=> no bucket padding), _dummy_pooler_run_task (moved to PoolingRunner), the block_table._compute_slot_mapping_kernel patch (V1 module), the force_attention kwarg (swallowed by **kwargs), hasattr(self, "drafter"), use_cuda_graph / cascade_attn_enabled writes. Crashes rather than silence: self.sampler.topk_topp_sampler in __init__, self.inputs_embeds.gpu in load_model, cache.device on SpyrePagedKVCache in V2 initialize_kv_cache.

7. Open questions

  1. Runner device: keep cpu (CPU-MRV2 precedent; floats then also live on host and every model input must H2D at the wrapper) or try spyre (needs int32/bool/int64 device tensors)? This decides the fate of SpyreCpuGpuBuffer and the embed_input_ids staging trick.
  2. Triton: is triton with the CPU backend installable alongside torch-spyre? If yes, device=cpu
    • Triton-CPU makes most block-table/input-batch kernels "just work" (that is how vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py runs). If no, every Triton kernel needs a torch override and use_v2_model_runner stays False, so GW's V2-gated branches (pooling pool()) must be handled in SW.
  3. Will v0.32 delete V1 and the use_v2_model_runner gating in GW (making the worker issue moot), or keep the property and its HAS_TRITON check?
  4. Does torch-spyre implement torch.accelerator.synchronize/empty_cache/get_memory_info for PrivateUse1? If so, V2's direct calls are fine; cpu/shm.py must not be imported as-is because it no-ops torch.accelerator.synchronize.
  5. With V2 dummy runs building attention metadata (zeroed block tables, PAD slots), does SpyreAttentionImpl / the head-major KV store tolerate PAD (-1) slots, and does it compile unbucketed variants during warmup? Is publish_null_slots still required?
  6. Is a get_model_state_cls-provided SpyreModelState (default.py subclass) the right home for the inputs_embeds staging, custom sampler (custom_sampler), and post-KV hook (initialize_kv_cache on main)?
  7. Is is_text_generation_model(_SpyreModelWrapper) (runtime_checkable Protocol through __getattr__) True? V2 get_supported_tasks no longer goes through get_model().
  8. Upstreaming opportunity: per-layer sliding_window as part of init_attn_backend's group key would remove _split_attn_groups_by_layer_window; a per-layer (non-shared) allocation path in init_kv_cache / an overridable allocator would remove most of the KV-init override.
A3. Runner per-step execution half: V1 → MRV2 mapping (03_runner_execution_mapping.md)

03 - Runner per-step execution: V1 -> MRV2 API mapping

Scope: the per-step half of TorchSpyreModelRunner (/home/senuser/spyre-inference/spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_model_runner.py, "SMR" below) and _SpyreModelWrapper. V1 refs are vllm-v0.28.0/vllm/v1/worker/gpu_model_runner.py ("GMR"). MRV2 refs are vllm-v0.31.0/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/... unless noted. vllm-main notes come from a diff of the same files against main (bab0ac61).

0. Headline findings

  1. MRV2 has real seams for almost everything we hook, but they live on helper objects, not the runner. V1 hooks are private runner methods we wrap with *args/**kwargs. In MRV2 the same points are public methods on ModelState (prepare_attn, prepare_inputs, prepare_inputs_embeds, custom_sampler), on PoolingRunner (pool, _dummy_pooler_run_task) and on the cudagraph manager (dispatch). Each one gets a typed InputBatch instead of positional args. So the work is a re-target onto 3 subclasses: a Spyre ModelState mixin, a SpyrePoolingRunner, and a bucketing dispatcher. It is not a rewrite of the logic.
  2. The step state has to move. _encoder_grid, _encoder_buffer_rows, _forced_encoder_rect, _force_encoder_ragged and the dispatch counters live on the runner today. In MRV2 they belong on the Spyre ModelState, which owns both prepare_attn (where the plan gets built) and prepare_inputs (where the grid gets expanded). SpyrePoolingRunner needs a reference to that ModelState. _encoder_buffer_rows goes away entirely, because InputBatch.num_tokens_after_padding carries the padded row count.
  3. ModelState is picked by resolve_model_state_cls (model_states/__init__.py:28): the model's own get_model_state_cls() if it has one, otherwise EncoderDecoder / EncoderOnly / MambaHybrid / Default. Our plan hook must apply to whichever class wins. Pooling encoders resolve to EncoderOnlyModelState; decoder-as-pooler and the CLIP text tower resolve to DefaultModelState. So the Spyre piece should be a mixin over the resolved class, built dynamically in our overridden load_model, not a single subclass.
  4. The pooling output path is the hardest part. GPUModelRunner.pool() (model_runner.py:2249) always wraps output in AsyncPoolingOutput (async_utils.py:220). That class uses torch.cuda.Stream/Event and .to("cpu", non_blocking=True), which is exactly the D2H our copy_pooler_output_to_cpu exists to avoid. PoolingRunner.pool also does hidden_states[: input_batch.num_tokens], the Spyre dim-0 slice our _pool avoids, and builds the cursor on hidden_states.device (Spyre) instead of CPU. We have to override both PoolingRunner.pool and runner pool(), or have SpyrePoolingRunner.pool return CPU tensors and shim streams/events. Shimming is what the upstream CPU MRV2 runner does, vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py, by globally patching torch.cuda.Stream/Event.
  5. Cross-cutting blocker (other slices, but it sits in front of every hook here): MRV2's prepare_inputs runs Triton kernels: prepare_prefill_inputs (:1414), prepare_pos_seq_lens (:1425), combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens (:1436). So do slot mappings, post_update and gumbel_sample. VllmConfig.use_v2_model_runner returns False when not HAS_TRITON (config/vllm.py, v0.31 and main). The upstream CPU MRV2 runner works only because Triton-CPU exists. Spyre has no Triton, so every one of those kernels needs a pure-torch/numpy replacement, like our existing _compute_slot_mapping_kernel patch, before any hook below matters.
  6. V1 is still there in main. gpu_model_runner.py exists in v0.29, v0.31 and main. The signatures of _determine_batch_execution_and_padding, _build_attention_metadata, _preprocess, _pool and _dummy_pooler_run_task are byte-identical from v0.28 through main. Today's fragility is about upstream body changes (return-tuple arity, kwarg names), not signature churn.

main vs v0.31 for these files: input_batch.py, model_states/{default,encoder_only,__init__}.py, mm/encoder_runner.py and dp_utils.py are unchanged. model_states/interface.py adds initialize_kv_cache(kv_cache_config, block_tables) and capture_inner_cudagraphs(...) hooks. The first is useful for warmup and attention recording. pooling_runner.py adds release_late_interaction_query_cache and a LateInteractionRunner(enable_flash=...) ctor arg. model_runner.py adds compile_model_with_stock_torch(self.model, ...) at the end of load_model under STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE. That is irrelevant while we override load_model, but matters if we ever call super().load_model(). dp_size/dp_rank became properties, and ExecuteModelState.num_spec_tokens_to_schedule was added. No per-step seam in this slice moved.

3. Per-method mapping

Difficulty buckets: trivial (moves unchanged or near-unchanged), re-target (same logic, new host object or new input source), redesign (MRV2's control flow differs enough that the logic itself has to change).

# Our method (SMR line) What it does / Spyre reason V1 hook point (v0.28) MRV2 hook point (v0.31) Difficulty Notes
1 _build_attention_metadata (1103) After upstream builds metadata, for pooling picks the encoder path for the step: rect EncoderRectPlan vs ragged list, built on the host because the builder does D2H/H2D that would become graph nodes. Attaches it to every SpyreAttentionMetadata.encoder_plan, publishes the grid mask, sets self._encoder_grid. Rows checked against num_tokens_padded or _encoder_buffer_rows. Override of private GMR._build_attention_metadata (def 2350; calls 4501 real, 6178 dummy). *args/**kwargs passthrough, reads kwargs["num_tokens_padded"] (None unless FULL CG) and the return tuple shape (out[0]). Override prepare_attn on a Spyre mixin over the resolved ModelState (EncoderOnlyModelState.prepare_attn encoder_only.py:162, DefaultModelState.prepare_attn default.py:169). Call super(), then walk the returned dict[layer -> md] (a plain dict, not a tuple). Rows = input_batch.num_tokens_after_padding. re-target Stable public, typed seam (abstract on ModelState, interface.py:215). The return type is simpler: always a dict, no ubatch list, no (md, spec_md) tuple. Runs on dummy runs too (MRV2 always builds metadata unless skip_attn), which our docstring already requires. _dummy_block_table/slot mapping for encoder groups are allocated on the ModelState device (CPU for us), which is fine. Spyre state (_encoder_grid, rectangles, width caps, budget) must live on the ModelState.
2 _forced_encoder_plan (1182) Warmup only: builds an EncoderRectPlan for a declared rectangle, ignoring the dummy batch's even split. Called from #1. Reads self._forced_encoder_rect. Same, called from the mixin's prepare_attn. _forced_encoder_rect moves to the ModelState, or stays on the runner and is read via a back-ref. trivial Pure helper. A unit test calls TorchSpyreModelRunner._forced_encoder_plan(runner) with a SimpleNamespace (tests/runtime/test_encoder_inline_dispatch.py:322), so the test needs re-pointing.
3 _record_encoder_dispatch (1208) Counts rect/ragged steps and body occupancy, logs every 200 steps. Called from #1. Same, on the ModelState. trivial Counters (spyre_encoder_*) are only referenced in SMR.
4 _determine_batch_execution_and_padding (1235) Injects shape-bucket padding through upstream's CUDA-graph padding channel: returns (NONE, BatchDescriptor(num_tokens=padded), False, None, None) once the bucketer is warmed, and stores _encoder_buffer_rows. Override of private GMR._determine_batch_execution_and_padding (def 4055; calls 4390 real, 6046 dummy). Re-declares the full 11-param signature and forwards every kwarg by name to super, the most brittle override in the slice (though unchanged v0.28 to main). Also ignores force_eager, so profile runs pad too. Replace self.cudagraph_manager (built at model_runner.py:720 in initialize_kv_cache) with a subclass of ModelCudaGraphManager whose dispatch(num_reqs, num_tokens, uniform_token_count, num_active_loras, max_query_len=None, num_ubatches=1) (cudagraph_utils.py:499) returns BatchExecutionDescriptor(cg_mode=NONE, num_tokens=padded, num_reqs=num_reqs). re-target Real seam, but it is a method on a helper object swapped after super().initialize_kv_cache(), not a runner hook. Caveats: (a) dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp skips dispatch() when need_eager=is_profile or skip_compiled, so profile and encoder-decoder-with-encoder-input steps stay unpadded (a behaviour change from V1, probably harmless). (b) _encoder_buffer_rows is no longer needed: InputBatch.num_tokens_after_padding = max(num_tokens, desc.num_tokens) (1318ff). (c) The alternative, monkeypatching model_runner.dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp at module level, is more fragile. (d) FastPrefillHelper also holds the manager; kv-sharing fast prefill is irrelevant to us.
5 _spyre_bucket_batch_descriptor (1286) bucketer.dispatch(num_tokens) -> BatchDescriptor(padded) or None. Helper for #4. Body of the custom dispatch(). Return a BatchExecutionDescriptor instead of a BatchDescriptor. trivial BatchExecutionDescriptor is a frozen dataclass (cudagraph_utils.py:63). The runner builds the forward-context BatchDescriptor itself from num_tokens_after_padding in NONE mode.
6 _preprocess (1521) On the rectangular path, expands packed ids/positions (or CPU-side inputs_embeds for mm pooling) and token_type_ids into the dense [width*extent] grid on the host, then converts back. Otherwise delegates to #7. Override of private GMR._preprocess (def 3613, call 4524). *args/**kwargs. Unpacks input_ids, inputs_embeds, positions, *rest and scans rest for the model_kwargs dict. Depends on the 6-tuple return order. Override prepare_inputs(input_batch, req_states) on the mixin (abstract on interface.py:205; Default 146, EncoderOnly 120). Call super() (gets token_type_ids / M-RoPE positions), then return overrides {"input_ids": grid_ids, "positions": grid_pos, "token_type_ids": ..., "inputs_embeds": ...}. The runner merges them over its defaults (model_runner.py:1915-1922). re-target Cleaner seam: a dict merge, no tuple-arity scanning. Gaps: (a) prepare_inputs gets no inputs_embeds, so the mm-pooling (CLIP) branch must capture it by also overriding prepare_inputs_embeds (default.py:100) and stashing the result on the state. (b) In MRV2 prepare_inputs also runs on dummy runs, where V1 _dummy_run skipped _preprocess. Expansion now runs during warmup: correct, and arguably better coverage, but it changes what warmup compiles. (c) input_batch.input_ids/positions are InputBuffers slices on the runner device (CPU for us) of length num_tokens_after_padding. MRV2 does not zero the positions pad tail the way V1 _preprocess did. (d) For non-first PP ranks the runner nulls input_ids/inputs_embeds afterwards, but PP>1 is rejected anyway.
7 _offset_preprocess_positions (1610) Packed path: adds the RoBERTa padding_idx+1 offset to host positions before the wrapper's H2D (stock SDSC cannot schedule an int add on device). Post-processes out[2] of super()._preprocess. Same mixin prepare_inputs: {"positions": offset_host_positions(input_batch.positions, delta)}. trivial CommonAttentionMetadata.positions keeps the un-offset buffer in both runners. Now also applied on dummy runs (positions zero there, so harmless).
8 _rectangular_pooling (1626) Decides whether a homogeneous CLS/MEAN batch can read the grid directly, skipping the unpad gather. Helper for _pool. Reads self.model.pooler, pooling_metadata.tasks, get_pooling_cursor().num_scheduled_tokens_cpu. Move into SpyrePoolingRunner, reading self.model.pooler (PoolingRunner keeps the model) and the grid from the ModelState back-ref. trivial Test tests/runtime/test_encoder_dense_grid.py:269 builds a fake runner and needs re-pointing.
9 _encoder_pad_token_id (1657) hf_config.pad_token_id or 0, for grid filler. Helper for #6. ModelState (has model_config). trivial
10 _dummy_pooler_run_task (1662) Copy of upstream with the cursor built on CPU (device=cpu) instead of hidden_states.device, because the cumsum-offset views are not stick-aligned on Spyre. Override of private GMR._dummy_pooler_run_task (6471), called by _dummy_pooler_run (6528). Override PoolingRunner._dummy_pooler_run_task(hidden_states, task) (pooling_runner.py:161) in SpyrePoolingRunner. Entry is the public dummy_pooler_run (206). re-target Still a private-name override, now on a small class. Upstream body changed: remainder spread over the last num_extra reqs, prompt_token_ids=None plus a requires_token_ids branch, no OOM wrapper difference. Copy the new body, keeping device=cpu. MRV2 runs every supported task once and no longer re-runs max_task.
11 _pool (1706) Pools on Spyre and D2Hs only the pooled vectors. Builds the cursor on CPU. No dim-0 crop. Rect CLS/MEAN read the grid via patched first_token_indices_gpu / SpyrePoolingCursor, otherwise _unpad_encoder_hidden. Late-interaction postprocess. copy_pooler_output_to_cpu. Asserts no async scheduling. FP32-head models stay on CPU and call super()._pool. Override of private GMR._pool (3528, call 4592). Reads self.input_batch.{num_reqs,pooling_params,get_pooling_metadata(),req_ids,req_id_to_index}, self.optimistic_seq_lens_cpu, self.late_interaction_runner. Two overrides: (a) SpyrePoolingRunner.pool(hidden_states, input_batch, req_states) (pooling_runner.py:132) for the cursor, no slice, grid handling, Spyre->CPU convert; (b) runner pool() (model_runner.py:2249), or a global stream/event shim, because it wraps the result in AsyncPoolingOutput (CUDA streams/events plus .to("cpu", non_blocking=True)). Inputs: PoolingRunner._get_pooling_metadata(input_batch, req_states, device), input_batch.seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound, input_batch.num_scheduled_tokens, input_batch.query_start_loc; late_interaction_runner now lives on the PoolingRunner. redesign The output contract changes. MRV2 is async-output only (no use_async_scheduling switch to assert on). finished_mask comes from cursor.get_finished_mask(), not seq_len == prompt_len. Supported pooling tasks are restricted to {embed, classify, token_embed, token_classify, embed&token_classify} (pooling_runner.py:20); other tasks raise at construction. If SpyrePoolingRunner.pool returns CPU tensors, AsyncPoolingOutput's .to("cpu") becomes a no-op and only the stream/event shim is still needed (as in cpu/shm.py). PoolingRunner is built in load_model (510), which we override, so constructing the subclass is straightforward. The CPU-pooler (FP32 head) branch also has to move into the subclass.
12 _warm_pooler_row_widths (1346) Warmup sweep compiling pooler row gathers and heads at every power-of-two row width (plus the MEAN grid reduce per rectangle). Called from SMR warming_up_model. Touches no upstream hook except get_model().pooler. Unchanged. Called from our own warmup. self.get_model() is our override; MRV2's base returns self.model, i.e. the wrapper. trivial Test tests/runtime/test_warmup_pooler_row_widths.py uses __new__, so it still works if the attribute names are kept.
13 _warm_token_pool_widths (1410) Same, for SpyreAllPool's length ladder. Same. Unchanged. trivial
14 _warm_encoder_inline_paths (1441) One _dummy_run(budget, force_attention=True) per declared rectangle with _forced_encoder_rect set, plus one forced-ragged run. Relies on V1 _dummy_run(force_attention=) building metadata so #1 runs. MRV2 _dummy_run (785) always builds attention metadata (skip_attn defaults False). Drop force_attention: it is swallowed silently by **kwargs, so it fails neither loudly nor at all. The flags must be visible to the ModelState prepare_attn. re-target Behaviour carries over. Watch (a) the dummy path now also runs mixin prepare_inputs (expansion), which the identity-length forced rect keeps a no-op; (b) _dummy_run's return changed to (hidden, hidden[logits_indices]).
15 _warm_encoder_unpack (1478) Compiles _unpad_encoder_hidden's full-height select_rows gather once. Called from SMR warmup. Unchanged. trivial
16 _unpad_encoder_hidden (1489) Re-compacts the rect grid into packed order at the buffer's row count (no per-total recompile). Host-Python index build (#981). Helper for _pool. Moves into SpyrePoolingRunner, with the grid from the ModelState. trivial
17 _SpyreModelWrapper.__call__ (387) int tensors -> Spyre int64 (no int32 add on SDSC). Resets the Llama-4 scale cache. D2H output unless pooling-on-Spyre. Every self.model(...) (GMR 3981, 6266). self.model(**model_inputs) (2007) and cudagraph_manager.run_pw_graph (never reached in NONE). trivial The kwargs set is the same plus optional token_type_ids. In MRV2 the wrapper must also be the object passed to init_model_state, PoolingRunner and (via ModelState) EncoderRunner/get_rope_state, so more isinstance(model, Protocol) and hasattr checks run against it (resolve_model_state_cls uses hasattr(model, "get_model_state_cls") and model.modules(); get_rope_state asserts isinstance(model, SupportsMRoPE); has_compiled_submodule(self.model) in initialize_kv_cache calls model.modules()). __getattr__ forwarding covers runtime-checkable protocols and attribute probes, but not checks against concrete classes.
18 _SpyreModelWrapper.compute_logits (422) Pads the sampled-row count onto warmed row buckets, H2Ds hidden to Spyre for the lm_head, trims. Logits come back on CPU via SpyreLogitsProcessor. GMR.execute_model 4600, _dummy_sampler_run 6367, prompt logprobs 5802. GPUModelRunner.sample (1579), _dummy_sampler_run (944), and prompt_logprobs_worker.compute_prompt_logprobs/compute_prompt_token_id_logprobs(self.model.compute_logits, ...). trivial Same seam. New bypass: with parallel_config.enable_batch_sharded_sampling, MRV2 calls self.model.compute_logits_local (1574), which __getattr__ forwards without H2D. Wrap it too or reject that flag. hidden_states[input_batch.logits_indices] is still a CPU index.
19 _SpyreModelWrapper.embed_multimodal (451) Float mm kwargs -> Spyre fp16; capture_scalar_outputs patch for eager vision towers. GMR._execute_mm_encoder (3240/3271), profile_run (6606). EncoderRunner.execute_mm_encoder (mm/encoder_runner.py:168), reached via ModelState.execute_mm_encoder / prepare_inputs_embeds and profile_encoder_cache. trivial Works only if the EncoderRunner's self.model is the wrapper, i.e. we pass the wrapper to init_model_state. group_and_batch_mm_kwargs(device=self.device) uses the ModelState device (CPU), same as V1. The encoder-cudagraph manager is off when enforce_eager or no cudagraph_mm_encoder; make sure it stays off.
20 _SpyreModelWrapper.embed_input_ids (474) Buckets the token count (TP collective schedules), pads ids/is_mm on CPU, H2Ds, merges on device. Stages into the persistent inputs_embeds buffer at bucket shape and returns its prefix view, so upstream's copy is a skipped self-copy (torch-spyre#4730 narrowed-H2D rejection). GMR._preprocess 3663/3675/3710 (inputs_embeds.gpu[:n].copy_(...)). Buffer passed as inputs_embeds_buffer=self.inputs_embeds.gpu (SMR 733; a SpyreCpuGpuBuffer whose float .gpu is on Spyre). EncoderRunner.get_inputs_embeds (300; then self.inputs_embeds[:x.shape[0]] = x), DefaultModelState.prepare_inputs_embeds non-mm prompt-embeds branch (self.inputs_embeds[:n] = ...), PromptEmbedsState. re-target The buffer moves: model_state.encoder_runner.inputs_embeds (mm, allocated at encoder_runner.py:58 with device=ModelState.device, i.e. CPU for us) or model_state.inputs_embeds (prompt-embeds). We must (a) replace that tensor with a Spyre-resident one after init_model_state, otherwise the wrapper's staged copy is a D2H and the model gets CPU embeds (the wrapper only converts int tensors), and (b) point _inputs_embeds_buffer at it after construction, since the wrapper must exist before the ModelState. dummy_inputs_embeds returns the same buffer, so dummy runs get a Spyre tensor like V1. Upstream uses __setitem__ rather than .copy_, which should hit the same self-copy skip; verify on device. Called with input_batch.input_ids[:num_tokens] (unpadded), same as V1.
21 _SpyreModelWrapper.__getattr__/__setattr__ (548/551) Transparent passthrough. everywhere everywhere trivial See #17 on more consumers holding the wrapper.
22 (context) sampler swap in __init__ (self.sampler.topk_topp_sampler = SpyreTopKTopPSampler) Sort-free top-k. GMR._sample -> Sampler.topk_topp_sampler. MRV2 Sampler (sample/sampler.py) has no topk_topp_sampler. It calls module-level apply_top_k_top_p plus Triton gumbel_sample. Seam: ModelState.custom_sampler(sampler) -> (sampler, rejection_sampler) (interface.py, called model_runner.py:482). redesign A different sampler class, Triton-dependent (gumbel, penalties, logprob, min_p, bad_words...). Probably owned by the sampler slice. Listed because it is the per-step _sample hook.
23 (context) _dummy_run (1308) Forces D2H during the dummy forward, force_attention for pooling, publish_null_slots. GMR._dummy_run 5940. GPUModelRunner._dummy_run (785, *args/**kwargs). re-target Another slice's item. Noted because #14 depends on it: force_attention is a dead kwarg in MRV2.

Counts (rows 1-21, the methods in this slice): trivial 14 (2, 3, 5, 7, 8, 9, 12, 13, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 21; 19 with caveats), re-target 6 (1, 4, 6, 10, 14, 20), redesign 1 (11). Context rows 22-23 add 1 redesign (sampler) and 1 re-target (_dummy_run).

(Row 21 is bookkeeping. Without it: 13 trivial / 6 re-target / 1 redesign.)

4. Fragile private-V1 wrapping today, and MRV2's replacement seam

V1 private method we override How we wrap Fragility MRV2 seam Stability
_build_attention_metadata *args, **kwargs, reads kwargs["num_tokens_padded"], out[0] if tuple Silently gets rows=0 if upstream renames the kwarg. Upstream only passes it when FULL CG, so we depend on the _encoder_buffer_rows side channel. ModelState.prepare_attn (abstract, public, typed InputBatch) good
_determine_batch_execution_and_padding Full explicit signature, forwards every kwarg Breaks on any new kwarg. Unchanged v0.28 to main. CudaGraphManager.dispatch (public method on a swappable object) ok: object swap after initialize_kv_cache
_preprocess *args, **kwargs, unpacks a 6-tuple prefix and scans for the dict Tuple order/arity ModelState.prepare_inputs dict override (+ prepare_inputs_embeds) good
_pool Explicit 4-arg signature, super()._pool for the CPU pooler Body copy of upstream (late-interaction, ModelRunnerOutput fields) PoolingRunner.pool (public) + runner pool() (public) ok, but AsyncPoolingOutput is hard-wired
_dummy_pooler_run_task Explicit, body copy Body copy PoolingRunner._dummy_pooler_run_task (still private) weak, but on a 200-line class
_dummy_run (context) *args, **kwargs force_attention vanishes in MRV2 with no error _dummy_run(**kwargs) weak: silent kwarg drop

The wrapper methods (compute_logits, embed_input_ids, embed_multimodal) are model-interface calls, not runner privates, and they keep working in MRV2. That is the most portable part of our design.

5. V1 attributes and data structures we read, and their MRV2 counterparts

V1 (what we read) Where MRV2 counterpart
kwargs["num_tokens_padded"] / self._encoder_buffer_rows #1, #4 input_batch.num_tokens_after_padding (dummy: == num_tokens == batch_desc.num_tokens)
num_tokens (unpadded) arg #4 input_batch.num_tokens; dispatch gets num_tokens
num_scheduled_tokens_np (np int32 per req) #4, #11 input_batch.num_scheduled_tokens (np, an upper bound under adaptive verification; exact for us)
self.input_batch (persistent InputBatch of gpu_input_batch.py) #11 No persistent batch. Per-step InputBatch (gpu/input_batch.py:38) plus persistent self.req_states: RequestState (states.py), indexed by input_batch.idx_mapping_np
self.input_batch.num_reqs #11 input_batch.num_reqs (num_reqs_after_padding for padded)
self.input_batch.req_ids / .req_id_to_index #11 input_batch.req_ids; MRV2 builds {rid: i} itself (model_runner.py:2275)
self.input_batch.pooling_params / .get_pooling_metadata() #11 PoolingRunner.pooling_params[req_index] + PoolingRunner._get_pooling_metadata(input_batch, req_states, device)
self.optimistic_seq_lens_cpu[:num_reqs] #11 input_batch.seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound[:num_reqs] (exact for pooling)
self.query_start_loc.{cpu,gpu} (CpuGpuBuffer) upstream CAM; super()._pool input_batch.query_start_loc (tensor on the runner device), input_batch.query_start_loc_np
self.seq_lens (tensor) upstream CAM input_batch.seq_lens (from InputBuffers.seq_lens, written by the Triton prepare_pos_seq_lens)
self.positions (tensor) _preprocess output positions input_batch.positions (InputBuffers.positions[:num_tokens_after_padding], int64); M-RoPE: model_state.rope_state.get_positions(...)
self.input_ids.gpu (int32, aliased to CPU by our _make_buffer) _preprocess output input_ids input_batch.input_ids (InputBuffers.input_ids, int32, device=self.device). Our _make_buffer/SpyreCpuGpuBuffer trick has no MRV2 equivalent: InputBuffers is plain tensors on self.device, so self.device must stay CPU
self.inputs_embeds.gpu (Spyre-resident float buffer) wrapper _inputs_embeds_buffer model_state.encoder_runner.inputs_embeds (mm) / model_state.inputs_embeds (prompt embeds), both allocated on the ModelState device. Must be replaced with Spyre tensors
model_kwargs["token_type_ids"] (_init_model_kwargs) #6 EncoderOnlyModelState.prepare_inputs -> model_inputs["token_type_ids"] (built from pooling_params.extra_kwargs["compressed_token_type_ids"])
per-layer attn_metadata dict (or list under ubatch) + spec_decode_common_attn_metadata #1 dict[layer_name -> metadata] from model_state.prepare_attn
CommonAttentionMetadata (built in GMR ~2499) builder input Built in attn_utils.build_attn_metadata (498) for KV groups and EncoderOnlyModelState._build_encoder_attn_metadata (206) for encoder-only layers, with the same fields (query_start_loc{,_cpu}, seq_lens, seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound, num_actual_tokens= unpadded in NONE mode, positions, is_prefilling) plus req_idx
BatchDescriptor (forward_context) #4/#5 return BatchExecutionDescriptor (cudagraph_utils.py:63) from dispatch; the runner derives BatchDescriptor(num_tokens=num_tokens_after_padding) itself
self.late_interaction_runner #11 self.pooling_runner.late_interaction_runner
self.use_async_scheduling (asserted False) #11 No equivalent: MRV2 output is always AsyncOutput/AsyncPoolingOutput
self.model (wrapper), self.get_model() many self.model, and also model_state.model, model_state.encoder_runner.model, pooling_runner.model. All must be the wrapper. MRV2 get_model() returns self.model unwrapped by nothing, so keep our override
self.scheduler_config.max_num_seqs, self.max_num_reqs, self.model_config, self.compilation_config.mode warmup, #1 same names on the MRV2 runner; ModelState has model_config, scheduler_config, vllm_config, max_num_reqs
self.supports_mm_inputs, self.model_config.is_encoder_decoder warmup self.supports_mm_inputs, self.is_encoder_decoder; ModelState: supports_mm_inputs
self.sampler.topk_topp_sampler __init__ none; use ModelState.custom_sampler

6. Suggested MRV2 shape (for the design doc)

  • SpyreModelStateMixin (built in our load_model as type("Spyre"+cls.__name__, (SpyreModelStateMixin, cls), {}) over resolve_model_state_cls(vllm_config, inner_model), or exposed via _SpyreModelWrapper.get_model_state_cls). It owns the encoder tables and step state, prepare_attn (= #1-3), prepare_inputs_embeds (stash embeds), prepare_inputs (= #6, #7, #9), custom_sampler (= row 22), and replaces encoder_runner.inputs_embeds / inputs_embeds with Spyre buffers in __init__.
  • SpyrePoolingRunner(PoolingRunner): pool (= #8, #11, #16), _dummy_pooler_run_task (= #10), with a back-ref to the ModelState for the grid. It returns CPU tensors.
  • SpyreBucketDispatcher(ModelCudaGraphManager): dispatch (= #4, #5), installed after super().initialize_kv_cache().
  • Runner pool() override, or a global stream/event shim, for AsyncPoolingOutput.
  • Warmup helpers (#12-15) stay on the runner unchanged, minus force_attention.

7. Open questions

  1. Triton-free MRV2: is anyone upstream making MRV2 run without Triton (the use_v2_model_runner gate says no as of main), or do we carry pure-torch replacements for prepare_prefill_inputs, prepare_pos_seq_lens, combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens, post_update, post_update_num_computed_tokens, expand_idx_mapping, the block-table/slot-mapping kernels, gumbel_sample, penalties and the rest? This gates everything above.
  2. Streams/events: do we adopt the CPU runner's global monkeypatch of torch.cuda.Stream/Event (vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py), or does torch-spyre provide torch.spyre.Stream/Event that AsyncOutput/AsyncPoolingOutput can use? GPUModelRunner.__init__ does torch.cuda.Stream(self.device) unconditionally (model_runner.py:220).
  3. self.device: keep it CPU, as today. InputBuffers, RequestState, BlockTables, the ModelState and EncoderRunner all allocate on it, and our _make_buffer CPU/Spyre split has no MRV2 counterpart. Then which float buffers need explicit relocation to Spyre? So far: encoder_runner.inputs_embeds, DefaultModelState.inputs_embeds, PromptEmbedsState buffers, intermediate tensors.
  4. Does __setitem__ (self.inputs_embeds[:n] = x in get_inputs_embeds) take torch-spyre's self-copy skip the same way V1's .copy_ does, given that x is a prefix view of the same buffer? This needs an on-device check (torch-spyre#4730 context).
  5. Do we accept that MRV2 runs prepare_inputs (our grid expansion) on dummy runs? It changes what warmup compiles. It probably improves coverage, but the compile-guard warmup-coverage tests (tests/runtime/test_compile_guard_warmup_coverage.py) will need re-baselining.
  6. Profile runs (need_eager=True) bypass dispatch(), so they would no longer be bucket-padded. Does our worker even call profile_run (it overrides determine_available_memory)? If it does, is an unpadded profile shape a compile risk?
  7. Does MRV2's restricted pooling task set (_SUPPORTED_TASKS) cover every pooling model we test, including scoring/cross-encoder ("classify") and late-interaction?
  8. enable_batch_sharded_sampling -> compute_logits_local bypasses the wrapper's H2D. Reject it in check_and_update_config, or wrap it?
A4. Worker, platform, scheduler, imports, tests, stopgap (04_worker_platform_stopgap.md)

04 — Worker, platform, scheduler, and the stopgap question (spyre-inference #906)

Paths: SI = /home/senuser/spyre-inference, V28/V29/V31/MAIN = vllm-v0.28.0 / vllm-v0.29.0 / vllm-v0.31.0 / vllm-main under /home/senuser/.claude/jobs/709f9980/tmp/ (vllm-main == vllm-src HEAD bab0ac61f, 2026-10-08).

TL;DR

  1. The "default flip" does not reach the Spyre runner. TorchSpyreWorker.init_device (SI spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_worker.py:99-166) overrides Worker.init_device completely, never calls super, and constructs TorchSpyreModelRunner itself (line 163). The upstream V1/V2 selection in Worker.init_device / Worker._make_model_runner is bypassed at every version.
  2. But vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner is an engine-wide switch, not a worker-local one. The scheduler, async scheduler, input processor, compilation config and the inherited Worker.execute_model / compile_or_warm_up_model all branch on it. It must be False for our V1-derived runner to receive the SchedulerOutput shape it expects. Today it is False on Spyre only because HAS_TRITON is False (pyproject.toml:76-78 forbids triton: "triton; sys_platform == 'never'"). That gate makes use_v2_model_runner return False (with the warning "Model Runner V2 requires Triton; using the V1 model runner instead.") at 0.28, 0.29, 0.31 and main.
  3. VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=0 works for us at 0.29-0.31 and main, but we don't need it. It short-circuits the property before the Triton probe, so the outcome is the same False; the only gain is that the warning goes away and the result no longer depends on the Triton probe. The flag is ignored only for HiSparse (raises) and watermarking (forces V2), both 0.31+. VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1 cannot work on Spyre: _validate_v2_model_runner raises ValueError("Model Runner V2 requires Triton.") (V28 config/vllm.py:2480, V29 :2726, V31 :3317, MAIN :3244). So an MRV2 migration needs either Triton on the host (and an active driver; HAS_TRITON also drops to False when Triton has no active driver, V28 triton_utils/importing.py:46-56) or an upstream gate change (open PRs #58703 and #50612, below).
  4. Removal timeline: the v0.29.0 release notes say "we are considering Model Runner V1 deprecated and are targeting v0.32 for its removal." As of main (2026-10-08) nothing has been removed: vllm/v1/worker/gpu_model_runner.py, gpu_input_batch.py (InputBatch, CachedRequestState), vllm/v1/utils.py:CpuGpuBuffer all still exist. There are no runtime deprecation warnings and no open "remove MRV1" PR (gh searches for "remove V1 model runner", "Remove Model Runner V1", "delete gpu_model_runner", "MRV1 cleanup" all return nothing). vLLM's own CPUModelRunner (V1) still subclasses V1 GPUModelRunner at main (MAIN vllm/v1/worker/cpu_model_runner.py:18,23), and CPUWorker still defaults to it whenever the flag is False (MAIN cpu_worker.py:161-177). Open PR #58600 even proposes defaulting CPU back to V1. V1 can't be deleted until the CPU backend moves off it, which is the strongest signal that the v0.32 date may slip, but don't plan on that.
  5. Two bump blockers in this slice that have nothing to do with MRV2:
    • TorchSpyreScheduler's cap silently stops working at v0.31. Since #56758 (first released in v0.31.0) the waiting loop admits against self.max_num_active_reqs (V31 scheduler.py:893, MAIN :912), not max_num_running_reqs. Our cap lowers max_num_running_reqs (SI spyre_inference/v1/core/scheduler.py:52-60), so after the bump it never trips, and the only trace is a lost prefill cap. The fix is to lower max_num_active_reqs (keeping max_num_running_reqs as is when it is present). tests/runtime/test_scheduler.py should catch this (test_default_admits_one_prefill_per_step), but test_streaming_slots_counted_as_occupancy (line ~150) asserts on max_num_running_reqs and has to be updated.
    • The slot-mapping kernel patch is a no-op from v0.29. SI spyre_model_runner.py:636-642 assigns block_table._compute_slot_mapping_kernel, which exists only at V28 (block_table.py:380). From V29 the kernel is ComputeSlotMappingKernel.kernel, a VllmJitKernel (V29 block_table.py:397, instance _COMPUTE_SLOT_MAPPING_KERNEL at :529). At V31+, the inherited CpuPlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides (V31 platforms/cpu.py:651, MAIN :673; called from WorkerWrapperBase, MAIN worker_base.py:282) registers C++ fallbacks that call torch.ops._C.compute_slot_mapping_kernel_impl (MAIN utils/cpu_triton_utils.py:32). Our VLLM_TARGET_DEVICE=empty build has no _C. TorchSpyrePlatform should override register_triton_kernel_overrides and register our pure-torch kernel through vllm.triton_utils.dispatcher.register_kernels ("vllm.v1.worker.block_table.ComputeSlotMappingKernel.kernel": ...). This is also the hook that would carry MRV2's Triton kernels later.

1. Worker

1a. How gpu_worker selects the runner

ver flag captured selection
0.28 self.use_v2_model_runner = vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner gpu_worker.py:185 inline in init_device, :424-438 (vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner.GPUModelRunner vs gpu_model_runner.GPUModelRunner)
0.29 :218 inline :457-476 (adds MMEncoderModelRunner for mm-encoder-only)
0.31 :227 inline :507-526
main :231 init_device → _init_workspace_and_model_runner() (:524-540) → new _make_model_runner() :542-558, docstring "Construct the platform's model runner; subclasses override this."

The property itself (VllmConfig.use_v2_model_runner):

  • V28 config/vllm.py:615-689: env var first, then the PCP/dspark/dflash/diffusion forces, then _is_default_v2_model_runner_model() (dense generate models only), then HAS_TRITON (:651), then _get_v2_model_runner_unsupported_features() (:2369; includes "stock torch.compile" at :2380, our default mode).
  • V29 :645-677: "default for all models" (#53183). Order is env var → ROCm exceptions → HAS_TRITON (:662) → unsupported features ("stock torch.compile" still listed, :2546).
  • V31 :701-760: adds the HiSparse (raise if =0) and watermark (override =0) forces ahead of the env var. "stock torch.compile" still listed (:3057).
  • MAIN :709-768: same order. "stock torch.compile" was removed from the unsupported list by #59079 (merged 2026-09-29, after 0.31). _disable_cudagraphs_for_v2_stock_torch_compile (:3220) now forces cudagraph NONE instead. Validation call at :2048-2052 (_validate_v2_model_runner or _validate_v1_model_runner).

On Spyre every version stops at HAS_TRITON → False, and even with Triton present, 0.28-0.31 would still go to V1 for the default compiled mode (STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE). With --enforce-eager (CompilationMode.NONE), at main, plus Triton present, the result would be True while we still build a V1-derived runner.

1b. TorchSpyreWorker overrides vs upstream Worker

our override (spyre_worker.py) upstream at 0.29/0.31/main MRV2 impact
_maybe_get_memory_pool_context :91 → nullcontext() unchanged shape at main :364 (still only short-circuits for cuda_alike/xpu/is_cpu()) none; keep
init_device :99-166 (full replacement, builds TorchSpyreModelRunner(vllm_config, torch.device("spyre")) :163) main splits into init_device + _init_workspace_and_model_runner (also init_workspace_manager(...)) + _make_model_runner at main we could override only _make_model_runner, but our init_device also does env/dlopen ordering, set_device, custom-op registration, dist init, so keep the full override. Note we never call init_workspace_manager; MRV2 code paths that use current_workspace_manager() would need it
determine_available_memory :168 same signature; @torch.inference_mode() at main :598 none

(inherited) _get_cudagraph_capture_context MAIN :1075 casts runner to V2 if the flag is set n/a
monkey_patch_torch_profiler_activity_map :60 "PrivateUse1" is already in TorchProfilerActivityMap at V28 profiler/wrapper.py:169 (V31 :186, MAIN :219) dead code; can delete

Imports used by the worker (get_worker_rank_suffix, TorchProfilerWrapper, set_random_seed, Worker, init_worker_distributed_environment, CompilationTimes) all still exist at main (see §3).

2. Platform & stopgap

2a. Platform hooks for the runner choice

  • vllm/platforms/interface.py has no supports_v2_model_runner / get_model_runner_cls / get_v2_runner_components at V28, V29, V31 or main. Only is_out_of_tree() (MAIN :218) and, new in V31, register_triton_kernel_overrides() (V31 :374, MAIN :376; "runs during model runner init, before any kernel launch", invoked from WorkerWrapperBase MAIN worker_base.py:282).
  • is_out_of_tree() is used by the config only to exempt OOT platforms from the AuxOutput→MRV2 requirement (V31 config/vllm.py:1166-1174, MAIN :1171-1179: "TPU and out-of-tree platforms bring their own model runners").
  • Open upstream PRs:
    • #58703 "[Platform] Add supports_v2_model_runner capability for out-of-tree platforms" (open, 2026-09-25). Adds a classmethod defaulting to True. False selects V1 with no Triton probe or warning, and an explicit V2 request raises. This is what we want for the stopgap (pin V1 semantics engine-wide and make =1 fail clearly), and it is the opposite of what we need for the migration.
    • #50612 "[TPU] Exempt TPU from the V2 model runner PCP and Triton gates" (open). Precedent for an upstream Triton-gate exemption, which is what a non-Triton MRV2 port on Spyre would need.
    • #58600 "[Bugfix][CPU] Default to V1 model runner on CPU backend" (open). Keyed on is_cpu(), which is False for our OOT enum, so it doesn't apply to us.
    • Issue #57684 (OpenVINO plugin, the same situation as ours: replaces the runner, can't ship Triton, consumes gpu_input_batch.InputBatch/ CachedRequestState). It asks maintainers for the V1 support window after v0.32; Q1/Q2 are still unanswered. Worth commenting there.
    • RFC #51212 "Model Runner V2 Pluggable Design" (open): Layer 1 = Triton kernel dispatcher (landed as vllm/triton_utils/dispatcher.py, RFC #45133), Layer 2 = Platform.get_v2_runner_components() (proposed, impl attempt #53895), Layer 3 hooks (contested).
    • Tracking: #47172 "Model Runner V2 Remaining TODOs" (STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE ✓ #59079, pooling ✓, custom logits procs ✓), #41286 migration roadmap.

2b. Is VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=0 relevant during a 0.29.x–0.31.x bump?

  • Effect: the property returns False at the env-var check (V29 :646-648, V31 :718-720, MAIN :726-728), before the Triton probe. That equals today's result, so behaviour doesn't change; it's belt-and-braces. It keeps us on V1 semantics if someone installs a Triton with an active driver (at 0.29/0.31 our default compiled mode would still fall back to V1 via "stock torch.compile", but at main --enforce-eager, or any mode after #59079, would flip).
  • Stronger, in-code stopgap that needs no user env var: set os.environ.setdefault("VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER", "0") in TorchSpyrePlatform.check_and_update_config, or better in pre_register_and_update/plugin register() (envs are read lazily via envs.__getattr__, and the property is evaluated repeatedly, so it must be set before the first VllmConfig.__post_init__ reads it). Also reject watermark_config and HiSparse there, because those force V2 regardless of the flag (V31+).
  • After v0.32, if V1 is deleted, the flag means nothing to us: the stopgap only buys time until upstream deletes the V1 classes we subclass/import.
  • Validation side: with False, _validate_v1_model_runner runs (V29+), and its unsupported list (PCP, PP+async, dspark, diffusion, batch-sharded sampling — MAIN :3010-3050) is all stuff Spyre already rejects or never enables.

2c. Engine-wide consumers of the flag (why it must match our runner)

  • Scheduler: V28 scheduler.py:299/1194/1223/1510; MAIN :352, :1417 (V2: resumed reqs are folded into scheduled_new_reqs as NewRequestData), :1449 (V1 only: prev_step_scheduled_req_ids), :1731 (V1 only: all_token_ids sent for reqs not scheduled in the previous step). A V1 runner with the flag True would get no all_token_ids/resumed-request data and corrupt state; an MRV2 runner with the flag False would get V1-shaped output.
  • AsyncScheduler MAIN async_scheduler.py:52; input processor MAIN v1/engine/input_processor.py:99, :164; compilation config post_init_cudagraph_sizes(use_v2_model_runner=...) (MAIN config/compilation.py:1372/1492); kernel_warmup MAIN :164.
  • TorchSpyreScheduler itself has no flag-dependent logic, so it works under either runner, apart from the max_num_active_reqs break above.

2d. CpuPlatform changes 0.28 → main that matter to a subclass

(diff of V28 vs V31/MAIN vllm/platforms/cpu.py)

  • New register_triton_kernel_overrides (V31 :651, MAIN :673): when HAS_TRITON is False (our case) it registers C++ (torch.ops._C.*) fallbacks for block_table slot mapping, eagle/dflash spec-decode helpers, rejection sampler, and mamba batch_memcpy. We inherit it, and we have no _C. Override it (register our torch impl of slot mapping; leave the rest unregistered or map to pure-torch). The same hook would carry pure-torch/Spyre replacements for MRV2's ~97 @triton.jit kernels under vllm/v1/worker/gpu/ (input_batch, block_table, buffer_utils, sample/*, …) if we port to MRV2 without Triton.
  • New check_and_update_config warning at V31: "Triton is not installed. triton-cpu is expected on x86 CPUs." (cosmetic; logs on every boot on x86 hosts).
  • New get_mla_prefill_backend_cls, AMX MLA / DeepSeek-V4 block-size logic, mamba _cpu_mamba_backend dtype logic, VLLM_SSM_CONV_STATE_LAYOUT default now skipped if set and picks "DS" for NIXL: none of it is on our path (no MLA, no mamba support on Spyre).
  • VLLM_CPU_CI_ENV now read via envs (bool) instead of os.environ (cosmetic).
  • CpuPlatform has no MRV2-specific branches at any version (grep use_v2_model_runner vllm/platforms/cpu.py → none).

2e. vLLM's own CPU worker/runner at main

  • CPUModelRunner(GPUModelRunner) V1, MAIN cpu_model_runner.py:18-23 (unchanged since 0.28).
  • MRV2 CPU runner: vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py (16 lines! class CPUModelRunner(vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner.GPUModelRunner) adding only warming_up_model), plus cpu/buffer_utils.py (CPU UvaBuffer) and cpu/shm.py, which monkeypatches gpu.buffer_utils.UvaBuffer. Present since V28. It is a good template for a TorchSpyreModelRunnerV2, but it relies on triton-cpu (HAS_TRITON on vllm-cpu builds requires the triton "cpu" backend, V28 triton_utils/importing.py:58-66).
  • CPUWorker.init_device picks V2 iff the flag is set (MAIN cpu_worker.py:161-177). _torch_cuda_wrapper (which we import) is unchanged and still lives in cpu_model_runner.py (V31/MAIN :145).

3. V1-worker-internal (and adjacent) imports in spyre_inference/ and tests/

All exist at V29, V31 and main unless noted (checked by symbol definition per version).

import where (SI) 0.31 / main status goes away with V1 removal?
vllm.v1.worker.gpu_worker.Worker, init_worker_distributed_environment spyre_worker.py:35; tests/probes/tp_probe.py:421 present (MAIN :186, :1596) no (Worker is shared V1/V2)
vllm.v1.worker.worker_base.CompilationTimes spyre_worker.py:36 present (:39) no
vllm.v1.worker.gpu_model_runner.GPUModelRunner spyre_model_runner.py:67; tests/runtime/test_compile_guard_warmup_coverage.py:43 (patches GPUModelRunner._dummy_run) present (V31 :479, MAIN :478) yes
vllm.v1.worker.gpu_model_runner._copy_pooler_output_to_cpu spyre_inference/v1/pool/spyre_pooler.py:111 (lazy) present (V31 :379, MAIN :378) yes. MRV2 pooling lives in vllm/v1/worker/gpu/pool/
vllm.v1.worker.cpu_model_runner._torch_cuda_wrapper spyre_model_runner.py:66 present, unchanged (MAIN :145) yes (module is V1 CPU runner)
vllm.v1.utils.CpuGpuBuffer (subclassed as SpyreCpuGpuBuffer, spyre_model_runner.py:209) spyre_model_runner.py:65; tests/runtime/test_spyre_cpu_gpu_buffer.py present, same __init__ (utils.py:110) likely (V1-runner-only consumer; MRV2 uses gpu/buffer_utils.py UvaBuffer/StagedWriteTensor)
vllm.v1.worker.block_table (patching _compute_slot_mapping_kernel) spyre_model_runner.py:636-642 symbol gone since V29 → ComputeSlotMappingKernel.kernel V1 block_table.py likely deleted; MRV2 has gpu/block_table.py (Triton)
vllm.v1.worker.utils.AttentionGroup spyre_model_runner.py:1070; tests/runtime/test_attn_group_window_split.py:30 present (MAIN utils.py:259) no: MRV2 uses it too (MAIN gpu/attn_utils.py:39, gpu/cudagraph_utils.py:50)
vllm.v1.worker.utils.bind_kv_cache spyre_model_runner.py:1825 present (MAIN :653) uncertain; MRV2 uses bind_kv_cache_to_layers (gpu/attn_utils.py:42)

vllm.v1.sample.ops.topk_topp_sampler.{TopKTopPSampler,apply_top_k_top_p_pytorch} spyre_inference/v1/sample/topk_topp_sampler.py:16 present (MAIN :202, :473; file grew) V1 sampler path: MRV2 samples via vllm/v1/worker/gpu/sample/* (Triton gumbel/top-k etc.), so our sampler override won't be called by an MRV2 runner
vllm.v1.core.sched.{scheduler.Scheduler, output.SchedulerOutput} scheduler.py:17-18; tests/runtime/test_scheduler.py present no
vllm.v1.attention.backends.utils.split_decodes_and_prefills, vllm.v1.attention.backend.*, kv_cache_interface.* attention backends present no
