Not imported anywhere in SI: gpu_input_batch / InputBatch / CachedRequestState, ubatch*, gpu_ubatch_wrapper. The runner inherits them through GPUModelRunner (self.input_batch, self.requests) rather than importing them. _cudagraph_support = AttentionCGSupport.NEVER (spyre_attn.py:320) still applies under MRV2 (gpu/attn_utils.py reads backend CG support).

4. Tests

Local tests tied to V1 internals (would need changes under MRV2)

  • tests/runtime/test_compile_guard_warmup_coverage.py:43,230-243: patches GPUModelRunner._dummy_run (V1). The MRV2 _dummy_run/warmup has a different signature and location.
  • tests/runtime/test_warmup_logits_widths.py:74: replaces runner._dummy_run on the V1-derived runner.
  • tests/runtime/test_spyre_cpu_gpu_buffer.py: SpyreCpuGpuBuffer (V1 CpuGpuBuffer subclass). Rewrite against MRV2 buffer types or drop it.
  • tests/runtime/test_attn_group_window_split.py:30: AttentionGroup survives in MRV2, but _split_attn_groups_by_layer_window would move to wherever MRV2's init_attn_backend (gpu/attn_utils.py:196) is hooked.
  • tests/attention/test_spyre_encoder_attn.py:745, tests/runtime/test_encoder_inline_dispatch.py:308: docstrings/assumptions about upstream V1 _dummy_run splitting tokens across max_num_seqs.
  • tests/pool/test_spyre_pooler_select_rows.py: V1 _copy_pooler_output_to_cpu path.
  • tests/runtime/test_scheduler.py: works with both runners, but must be updated for max_num_active_reqs at 0.31 (line ~150 asserts on max_num_running_reqs).
  • tests/runtime/test_platform.py: add an assertion that Spyre ends up with vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner is False (stopgap) or True (post-migration), so a silent flip is caught.
  • No test, CI file, doc or Makefile in SI mentions VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER / MRV2 today (grep → none).

Upstream tests

  • tests/plugin/spyre_testing_plugin/upstream_tests.yaml lists no tests/v1/worker/* files (rel_paths: models/language generation & pooling, transformers backend, v1/attention/test_attention_backends.py, gsm8k, basic_correctness, multimodal processing, grpc health). All of them go through vllm.LLM/the server, so they exercise whichever runner the flag + our worker produce. They should pass unchanged on the stopgap, and they're the regression net for the MRV2 port.
  • MRV2 test candidates at MAIN tests/v1/worker/: test_gpu_model_runner_v2.py (8 tests, mostly SimpleNamespace-mocked runner, sets device=cuda at :90. Adaptable), test_gpu_input_batch_v2.py (uses current_platform.device_type, but one test is CUDA-only, :62), test_gpu_batch_ordering.py (13, shape logic), test_attn_utils.py (15; init_attn_backend/grouping, relevant to our window split), test_encoder_runner.py (14), test_late_interaction_runner.py (pooling; the CUDA parts are skipif). test_gpu_block_table.py is CUDA-only (module skipif :11). Of these, test_attn_utils.py, test_gpu_batch_ordering.py and test_gpu_model_runner_v2.py look like the cheapest wins once a Spyre MRV2 runner exists. Everything Triton-backed will fail collection or launch without Triton or dispatcher overrides.
  • Also worth adopting: MAIN tests/config/test_v2_model_runner_selection.py if #58703 lands (it tests the platform capability).

Recommendation for this slice

  • Stopgap for the 0.29–0.31 bump (cheap and safe): keep the full init_device override. Pin VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=0 from the platform (or rely on the Triton gate and assert on it in a test). Reject HiSparse/watermark configs. Fix TorchSpyreScheduler for max_num_active_reqs. Override register_triton_kernel_overrides and re-target the slot-mapping patch. Accept **kwargs in profile. Drop the dead profiler monkeypatch.
  • Migration: MRV2 can't be selected on Spyre without Triton at any version, so the port needs (a) an upstream Triton-gate exemption for OOT platforms that bring their own runner (comment on #57684 / #58703; the #50612 TPU precedent), or a plugin-side monkeypatch of vllm.config.vllm.HAS_TRITON, and (b) torch/Spyre replacements registered through the Triton dispatcher for every MRV2 kernel on the dense decode/prefill/pooling path. The V1 classes we subclass are slated for removal "targeting v0.32", but they aren't deleted at main, have no deprecation warnings, and vLLM's own CPU V1 runner still depends on them.
A5. Attention and KV cache (05_attention_kv.md)

05 — MRV2 impact on Spyre attention and KV cache

Scope: spyre_inference/v1/attention/** and the attention-facing parts of spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_model_runner.py, checked against MRV2 (vllm/v1/worker/gpu/) at v0.31.0 and main. Refs: SI = /home/senuser/spyre-inference, V28/V31/MAIN = the vllm checkouts under /home/senuser/.claude/jobs/709f9980/tmp/. On main the relevant MRV2 files are essentially identical to v0.31 (input_batch.py, model_states/{default,encoder_only}.py have no diff; attn_utils.py and block_table.py have only small unrelated changes; sort_batch_req_ids is unchanged), so v0.31 line numbers stand for both unless noted.

Classification key: no change / re-target (same logic, new hook point or field) / redesign (our mechanism has to change shape) / blocker (nothing works until it's done).

Summary table

# Item Class
1 CommonAttentionMetadata fields our builder reads no change (fields exist); one robustness re-target
2 Host vs device placement of metadata (seq_lens, query_start_loc, block_table) re-target (keep runner device=cpu)
3 Slot mapping / block tables are Triton kernels with pointer indirection blocker (torch re-impl at method level, not kernel swap)
4 KV cache allocation: KVCacheTensor semantics + get_kv_cache_shape removal (v0.29+, hits V1 too) blocker / re-target, silent-corruption risk
5 MRV2 initialize_kv_cache is monolithic; self.kv_caches reads .device re-target (override whole method)
6 Per-layer sliding-window group split re-target (builders are created inside init_attn_backend)
7 _build_attention_metadata encoder-plan hook re-target to model_state.prepare_attn
8 Warmup attention recording vs CudaGraphManager / warmup.py coexist; padding hook re-target
9 MRV2 _dummy_run now always builds attn metadata and runs attention redesign of warmup dummy shapes
10 Encoder-only (pooling) attention supported by MRV2 (no V1 fallback); re-target
11 Batched-decode eligibility / batch ordering no change (minor coverage note)
12 Attention bucketer, kernels, head-major KV store, attn_layer slot publish no change
13 use_v2_model_runner gate at v0.31 lists "stock torch.compile" note for compile slice; removed on main

1. CommonAttentionMetadata as built by MRV2 vs what SpyreAttentionMetadataBuilder.build reads

Our builder (SI/spyre_inference/v1/attention/backends/spyre_attn.py:673-987) reads: seq_lens, query_start_loc, max_seq_len, max_query_len, block_table_tensor, slot_mapping (682-687), num_actual_tokens, num_reqs (699-700, 723), causal (712-714, bool or tensor), is_prefilling (875, via split_decodes_and_prefills), and query_start_loc_cpu indirectly (inside split_decodes_and_prefills, V31/vllm/v1/attention/backends/utils.py:832). It does not read seq_lens_cpu, num_computed_tokens(_cpu), _seq_lens_cpu or seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound (grep over SI/spyre_inference confirms).

MRV2 builds it in V31/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/attn_utils.py:429-555 (build_attn_metadata), called from model_states/default.py:166-238 (DefaultModelState.prepare_attn). Every field above is passed:

  • query_start_loc = device slice, query_start_loc_cpu = torch.from_numpy(query_start_loc_np) (default.py:189-192)
  • seq_lens = input_batch.seq_lens (device, produced by the Triton _prepare_pos_seq_lens_kernel, input_batch.py:383-401, called from model_runner.py:1425)
  • max_seq_len = seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound.max() (default.py:201); max_query_len = num_scheduled_tokens.max()
  • block_table_tensor = gather_block_tables(...) output, slot_mapping = per-group row of compute_slot_mappings (model_runner.py:1520-1538)
  • causal = bool True per group (attn_utils.py:465-467); is_prefilling = torch.from_numpy(is_prefilling_np) (default.py:229) — always non-None
  • seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound, positions, req_idx, rswa_prefix_lens, mm_req_doc_ranges also passed

Field-level API changes since v0.28 (V28/vllm/v1/attention/backend.py:449 vs V31/.../backend.py:389): _seq_lens_cpu, _num_computed_tokens_cpu and the seq_lens_cpu / num_computed_tokens_cpu properties are gone in v0.31; dcp_local_seq_lens_cpu became ..._cpu_upper_bound; req_idx added. We use none of the removed ones, so: no change. Our build_for_variant / build_for_batched_decode_variant (spyre_attn.py:989-1050) construct CommonAttentionMetadata with only fields that still exist.

Padding semantics differ: V1 0.28 passes num_reqs=num_reqs_padded, num_actual_tokens=num_tokens_padded (V28/.../gpu_model_runner.py:2496-2511); MRV2 in NONE/PIECEWISE mode passes unpadded num_reqs/num_tokens (default.py:178-185) while the slot mapping is sized to num_tokens_after_padding and PAD-filled. Our impl never reads num_actual_tokens/num_seqs to slice q/k/v, and the slot mapping is published at the padded length (spyre_attn.py:868-870, clamp-to-null in attn_layer.py:67-72), so no change.

Robustness re-target (cheap): prefer query_start_loc_cpu and seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound (exact without spec decode) over the device fields in build() (spyre_attn.py:756-758 .item(), 724 subtraction, 749 .tolist()), so the builder stops depending on the runner keeping int "device" tensors on the host (see #2).

2. Host vs device placement

Today: TorchSpyreModelRunner runs V1 with self.device = cpu (spyre_model_runner.py:602-611) and SpyreCpuGpuBuffer aliases int .gpu to .cpu (209-266, 1900-1926), so every "device" int tensor the builder sees is a host tensor and .item()/.tolist()/host indexing of block_table (spyre_attn.py:866, 895) are free.

MRV2 allocates its own int buffers directly with device=self.device (InputBuffers, input_batch.py:19-37; BlockTables.__init__, block_table.py:57-87; StagedWriteTensor, buffer_utils.py:132-175), not through _make_buffer. So the SpyreCpuGpuBuffer aliasing trick has no hook in MRV2. The only workable placement is to construct the MRV2 runner with device=cpu (as vLLM's own CPU MRV2 runner does, V31/vllm/v1/worker/cpu_worker.py:168-175, cpu/model_runner.py) and keep the Spyre device only at the model boundary (_SpyreModelWrapper). Then all attention metadata inputs stay host-side and our builder's host reads keep working. UVA: is_uva_available() returns true when current_platform.is_cpu() (V31/vllm/utils/platform_utils.py:51-57); our device_type="cpu" (SI/spyre_inference/platform.py:118-119), so UvaBuffer paths resolve to CPU. Re-target; keep device=cpu, do not try to put MRV2 int buffers on Spyre (int32/bool are not native there, per spyre_model_runner.py:600-603).

3. Block tables and slot mapping (Triton) — blocker

V1 today: one Triton kernel, vllm.v1.worker.block_table._compute_slot_mapping_kernel, replaced by a pure-torch _compute_slot_mapping_impl behind _FuncWrapper that mimics kernel[grid](...) (spyre_model_runner.py:144-206, patched in 633-642). Triton itself is excluded from our env (SI/uv.lock:68 triton ... marker = "sys_platform == 'never'"), so every @triton.jit is vLLM's placeholder.

MRV2 attention-path Triton kernels (all must be replaced):

  • _gather_block_tables_kernel (block_table.py:241-278) — per-step gather of persistent per-request rows into the batch-ordered input_block_tables, zeroing padded rows.
  • _compute_slot_mappings_kernel (block_table.py:281-366) — multi-group, PAD-fills [num_tokens, max_num_tokens), handles idx_mapping == -1 dummy rows.
  • _apply_write_kernel (buffer_utils.py:300-336) via StagedWriteTensor.apply_write (196-227) and FusedStagedWriter.apply (251-297) — this is how block IDs reach the block table at all (BlockTables.append_block_ids/apply_staged_writes, 117-151, called from model_runner.py:1695).
  • _prepare_pos_seq_lens_kernel (input_batch.py:346-401) — produces positions and seq_lens that the builder reads. (Plus non-attention kernels — _prepare_prefill_inputs_kernel, _combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens_kernel, _post_update_kernel, etc. — other slices.)

The _FuncWrapper kernel-swap trick does not carry over cleanly: MRV2 kernels take pointer tables (block_table_ptrs, input_block_table_ptrs built from t.data_ptr() as uint64, block_table.py:91-115, dereferenced with _load_ptr, buffer_utils.py:338). A torch function cannot dereference those. Re-implement at method level instead: a SpyreBlockTables(BlockTables) overriding apply_staged_writes, gather_block_tables, compute_slot_mappings, get_dummy_* using self.block_tables[i].gpu directly (CPU tensors), and a torch prepare_pos_seq_lens. The math of our existing _compute_slot_mapping_impl ports almost directly (it is the CP_SIZE==1 branch); add idx_mapping indirection (req_state_idx row lookup), the idx_mapping < 0 -> PAD rule and per-group slot_mapping_enabled. Our per-request Python loop for req_indices (spyre_model_runner.py:182-186) should become torch.repeat_interleave while at it. Vectorised torch on CPU is fine: these are tiny host tensors.

4. KV cache allocation — blocker on the bump (V1 and MRV2 alike)

These changes landed in v0.29 (layout RFC #42082) and hit V1 too, but MRV2's init_kv_cache uses the same allocator, so they must be handled for the migration regardless.

(a) KVCacheTensor semantics changed (V28/vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.py:934-937 vs V31/.../kv_cache_interface.py:1433-1438): shared_by -> layers ("layer names in L order"), size is now "total size of the backing allocation" shared by all tensors (allocate_kv_cache asserts one size, V31/vllm/v1/worker/utils.py:410-412), with per-layer layer_stride/block_stride/offset (constructed in V31/vllm/v1/core/kv_cache_utils.py:1795-1818). Our initialize_kv_cache_tensors (spyre_model_runner.py:1817-1871) does num_blocks = kv_cache_tensor.size // spec.page_size_bytes (1849) and binds one SpyrePagedKVCache to every name in shared_by (1856-1857). A mechanical rename to layers would make every layer of a spec share one page cache — silent KV corruption — and size // page_size would over-allocate by ~num_layers. Fix: allocate one cache per layer name with num_blocks = kv_cache_config.num_blocks_of(tensor) (or kv_cache_config.num_blocks), keep shared_kv_cache_layers aliasing (1859-1860, MRV2 equivalent get_shared_kv_cache_layers, attn_utils.py:150-156). Re-target, but correctness-critical.

(b) AttentionBackend.get_kv_cache_shape / get_kv_cache_stride_order removed from the base class (present V28/vllm/v1/attention/backend.py:89,119; absent at v0.29/v0.31/main). Replaced by a logical per-layer view [B, H, N, C] with C = (head_size + head_size_v) * dtype_size bytes (K and V packed in the content axis, V31/vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.py:295-311, 521-525) and a model-wide KVCacheLayout stride permutation resolved once in the engine core (V31/vllm/v1/kv_cache_layout.py:15-60, V31/vllm/v1/attention/backends/utils.py:259-...), declared by backends via supported_kv_cache_layouts() (backend.py:371-374).

  • Our get_kv_cache_shape overrides (spyre_attn.py:1092-1102 token-major [B, N, H, D]; spyre_head_major_attn.py:132-139 head-major [B, H, N, D]) become dead code.
  • Shapes map onto the RFC layouts: head-major ~ LBHNC (legacy "HND"), token-major ~ LBNHC ("NHD"). But our cache is two separate tensors (SpyrePagedKVCache(k_pages, v_pages), spyre_attn.py:109-130; allocate_pages 1576-1590, head-major 171-183) whereas vLLM's view packs K||V in C. As long as we keep our own allocator (we already bypass _allocate_kv_cache_tensors/_reshape_kv_cache_tensors), the stride semantics don't bind us; declare supported_kv_cache_layouts() = (LBHNC,) / (LBNHC,) per SPYRE_ATTN_KV_LAYOUT so engine-core resolution and any KV connector agree, and treat "vLLM-allocated packed KV" as unsupported. No layout is lost; head-major and token-major are both expressible. Re-target. (Only KV-connector / offload code that assumes the packed C axis would be violated — out of scope today.)

5. MRV2 initialize_kv_cache is monolithic

V1 gives us separate hooks: initialize_attn_backend (we override, spyre_model_runner.py:1057-1059) and initialize_kv_cache_tensors (1817). MRV2 has neither: GPUModelRunner.initialize_kv_cache (V31/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py:578-771) does init_attn_backend (642), builds BlockTables (668), PCP/ubatch, resolve_cudagraph_mode_and_sizes (707), ModelCudaGraphManager (720), then init_kv_cache (750, attn_utils.py:367-415) and finally self.kv_caches = [cache for cache in kv_caches_dict.values() if cache.device == self.device] (759-761) — SpyrePagedKVCache is a NamedTuple with no .device, so this line raises even if we patch init_kv_cache. Options: (a) override initialize_kv_cache wholesale (copy the ~190 lines, drop hisparse/PCP/ubatch/speculator, use SpyreBlockTables); (b) monkeypatch the names init_attn_backend / init_kv_cache imported into vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner and make the returned dict hold something with .device (e.g. give SpyrePagedKVCache a device property). (b) is smaller but fragile across bumps; (a) matches how much MRV2 we disable anyway. bind_kv_cache -> bind_kv_cache_to_layers (attn_utils.py:405-411). Re-target. _init_kv_zero_meta/KVBlockZeroer is gated on needs_kv_cache_zeroing in both V1 and MRV2 (V31/vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py:823-826) — no change.

6. Per-layer sliding-window group split

Ours: _split_attn_groups_by_layer_window (spyre_model_runner.py:1061-1101) rewrites self.attn_groups after super().initialize_attn_backend and before V1 creates builders (V28/.../gpu_model_runner.py:7775 attn backend, 7790 initialize_metadata_builders). The underlying reason persists: FullAttentionSpec.merge still collapses windows (V31/vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.py:598-631, merge_window_sizes).

MRV2 init_attn_backend (attn_utils.py:189-281) groups by (backend.full_cls_name(), layer_kv_cache_spec, num_heads_q) (232-243, same key as V1) but creates the builders in the same function (Phase 3, 249-279) and computes cudagraph support from them (280). So a post-hoc split would leave groups without builders, and the merged group's builder would already have run attn_layer.install / install_encoder on all its layers (spyre_attn.py:376-382). Re-target: wrap init_attn_backend — call it, split attn_groups, then group.create_metadata_builders(vllm_config, device, kernel_block_size, num_metadata_builders=1) for the new groups — and make sure the discarded merged builder's install() side effect is harmless (the split builders re-install with their own layer subsets; SlotMapping is per-builder so the last install must win). Or do the split by customising the per-layer spec before grouping (get_kv_cache_spec calls backend.customize_spec(spec), attn_utils.py:141-145, but that's per-layer before merge so it doesn't by itself stop merge). Re-target. _attn_metadata_builders() (spyre_model_runner.py:1044-1055) uses V1's _attn_group_iterator; MRV2 has plain self.attn_groups lists and group.get_metadata_builder(0).

7. _build_attention_metadata (encoder plan hook)

Ours: _build_attention_metadata override (spyre_model_runner.py:1103-1180) post-processes V1's per-layer metadata to attach encoder_plan, reading kwargs["num_tokens_padded"]. MRV2 has no such method; metadata comes from self.model_state.prepare_attn(...) (model_runner.py:1870-1881), which for encoder-only models is EncoderOnlyModelState.prepare_attn -> _build_encoder_attn_metadata (model_states/encoder_only.py:162-231). Re-target: subclass/wrap the model state's prepare_attn (model state is created in GPUModelRunner.load_model via init_model_state, model_runner.py:414-416, model_states/__init__.py:15-59; a model can also supply get_model_state_cls), use input_batch.num_tokens_after_padding as rows. Re-target.

8. Warmup attention recording vs CudaGraphManager / warmup.py / padding

  • Our recording (_record_attention_graphs, spyre_model_runner.py:996-1042; SpyreAttentionImpl.record_graphs, spyre_attn.py:1344-1405) is self-contained: it builds synthetic metadata from the builder's bucketer (build_for_variant, build_for_batched_decode_variant) and calls the impl directly. It depends only on compilation_config.mode and on having the builders and KV caches. No change, beyond finding builders via MRV2's attn_groups (#6) and caches via our own dict (#5).
  • SpyreAttentionMetadataBuilder._cudagraph_support = NEVER (spyre_attn.py:320) flows into get_attn_cg_support (attn_utils.py:284-309) and resolve_cudagraph_mode_and_sizes (V31/vllm/config/compilation.py:1370-1386, returns NONE immediately if the mode is already NONE). ModelCudaGraphManager with NONE builds no candidates (cudagraph_utils.py:249-253) and dispatch returns an unpadded NONE descriptor (cudagraph_utils.py:499-529). Coexist.
  • warmup.py::warmup_kernels (real fake-request steps for JIT kernels) is only invoked from gpu_worker.Worker.compile_or_warm_up_model (V31/vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py:873-879); TorchSpyreWorker overrides that method (SI/spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_worker.py:177-200), so it never runs. Coexist (could be adopted later to replace parts of our warmup).
  • Token-bucket padding: V1 hook _determine_batch_execution_and_padding (spyre_model_runner.py:1235-1284) returning BatchDescriptor(num_tokens=padded). MRV2 equivalent is the BatchExecutionDescriptor from dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp (model_runner.py:1740-1755, dp_utils.py:213) -> cudagraph_manager.dispatch. Re-target: a SpyreCudaGraphManager (or override of the dispatch call) returning BatchExecutionDescriptor(cg_mode=NONE, num_tokens=bucket, num_reqs=None|num_reqs). With NONE, attention metadata stays unpadded and the slot mapping is padded with PAD up to num_tokens_after_padding (block_table.py:195-227), matching what our KV write expects. Re-target.

9. MRV2 dummy runs now execute attention — redesign of warmup dummy shapes

V1 0.28 _dummy_run passes attn_metadata=None unless force_attention or FULL graphs, which our code relies on in two places: the decoder warmup comment (spyre_model_runner.py:999-1003: "_dummy_run delegates upstream, which passes attn_metadata=None, so forward returns before touching a kernel") and the pooling force_attention=True override (1308-1323). MRV2 _dummy_run (V31/.../gpu/model_runner.py:785-940) has no force_attention; it always calls prepare_dummy_attn + model_state.prepare_attn unless skip_attn (allowed only with is_profile, 799-802, 1808-1830, 1857-1881). Dummy shape: num_reqs = min(num_tokens, max_num_reqs), tokens split evenly (819-833), seq_len == query_len, zeroed block tables (page 0, block_table.py:182-193), PAD slot mapping (229-238), is_prefilling all False (input_batch.py:127-221).

Consequence: each _dummy_run(compile_size) in our decoder warmup now runs the real Spyre attention path through our builder with e.g. 256 requests x 2 tokens. Those take the per-sequence loop (query_len 2 rounds to the first query bucket > 1, i.e. min(512, max_num_batched_tokens)), so per layer you get up to max_num_seqs opaque kernel calls plus host mask builds per warmup size, and possibly lazy compiles of shapes outside variants(). Redesign options: (a) override _dummy_run (or our dispatch hook, since dummy_num_reqs = batch_desc.num_reqs or num_reqs, model_runner.py:1794) to make decoder warmups num_reqs=1; (b) run decoder warmups with skip_attn=True, is_profile=True (also disables the KV connector and forces eager dispatch, need_eager=is_profile); (c) accept attention in the dummy and drop the separate recording pass for those shapes. Upside: publish_null_slots (spyre_model_runner.py:1324-1326, attn_layer.py:86-94) becomes redundant because the builder's publish runs on the dummy's PAD slot mapping, and the pooling-model clamp for V1's bogus dummy seq_lens (spyre_attn.py:726-733) is no longer needed (MRV2 dummy has seq_len == query_len). Redesign (small code, but it changes the warmup contract).

10. Encoder-only / encoder attention (bge, MiniLM, granite-30m; gemma-4 vision)

  • MRV2 supports encoder-only models natively: resolve_model_state_cls picks EncoderOnlyModelState when any Attention has attn_type == ENCODER_ONLY (model_states/__init__.py:42-50). It is not in _get_v2_model_runner_unsupported_features (V31/vllm/config/vllm.py:3051-3103); only DBO + encoder-only is excluded (3272). So no V1-fallback condition. Pooling goes through pool/pooling_runner.py.
  • Encoder groups are built outside init_attn_backend, in the model state constructor (encoder_only.py:56-89), with EncoderOnlyAttentionSpec(block_size=cache_config.block_size, ...) -> our builder's block_size assert and _encoder_only short-circuit (spyre_attn.py:331-341, 689-711) still hold. Builders are created at load_model time (before initialize_kv_cache), so install_encoder runs earlier than in V1 — fine as long as it precedes our block compile (it does: init_model_state runs inside super().load_model, before our _compile_for_spyre).
  • They pass causal default True (comment: "The encoder builder forces causal=False", encoder_only.py:205-219); our encoder path ignores causal and always uses is_causal=False (spyre_encoder_attn.py:225, 291). No change.
  • layer.kv_cache = torch.empty(0, device=device) for encoder layers (encoder_only.py:64-66) — SlotMapping._resolve_device filters empty caches (attn_layer.py:50-62). No change.
  • build_encoder_plan reads query_start_loc.cpu() / seq_lens.cpu() (spyre_encoder_attn.py:396-397); MRV2 passes the unsliced input_batch.query_start_loc ([:num_reqs_padded+1]) — OK since NONE mode has no request padding. The _forced_encoder_plan warmup rationale ("dummy splits tokens evenly over max_num_seqs", spyre_model_runner.py:1182-1206) still matches MRV2's _dummy_run.
  • Hook point moves to EncoderOnlyModelState.prepare_attn (#7). _record_attention_graphs never saw encoder builders' graphs anyway (SpyreEncoderAttentionImpl.record_graphs, spyre_encoder_attn.py:502). gemma-4 vision tower keeps HF attention (spyre_model_runner.py:294-321) and goes through MRV2's mm/encoder_runner.py — mm slice.
  • Re-target.

11. Batched-decode eligibility and batch ordering

V1: builder sets reorder_batch_threshold=1 when SPYRE_BATCHED_DECODE (spyre_attn.py:388-390) and V1 reorders decodes first (V28/.../gpu_model_runner.py:1164-1190). MRV2 ignores reorder_batch_threshold (no reference under vllm/v1/worker/gpu/) and always orders the batch by sort_batch_req_ids (V31/.../gpu/model_runner.py:2398-2410, same on main 2442-2454): spec-verify first, then num_tokens == decode_query_len (decodes), then the rest ascending by token count. So decodes lead, as our dispatch needs: split_decodes_and_prefills (spyre_attn.py:872-876) and the identity assert query_row_ids[:num_decode_seqs] == range(num_decode_seqs) (905) hold.

is_prefilling is always passed by MRV2 (default.py:229), as it already is by V1 0.28 (V28/.../gpu_model_runner.py:2435-2511), so treat_short_extends_as_decodes=False in both. Minor: MRV2 sorts a 1-token chunked-prefill tail among the decodes by token count but marks it is_prefilling, and split_decodes_and_prefills cuts the decode prefix at the first such row (V31/vllm/v1/attention/backends/utils.py:858-870), so any decodes sorted after it fall to the per-seq loop. Correct, slightly less batched coverage; V1 reorder has the same shape. Optional tweak: pass treat_short_extends_as_decodes=True, since a 1-query extend over existing context is decode-shaped for our kernels. No change required.

12. Things that are unaffected

  • SpyreAttnBucketer (SI/spyre_inference/v1/attention/spyre_attn_bucketer.py) — reads only cache_config, model_config, scheduler_config, parallel_config. No change.
  • Attention kernels, online softmax, mask/tile construction, head-major store and kv_write_index (spyre_head_major_attn.py:184-200), attn_layer.install / _spyre_attention_forward (attn_layer.py:96-178), forward_includes_kv_cache_update=False (spyre_attn.py:1058) — MRV2 still passes the per-layer slot mapping via set_forward_context(slot_mapping=slot_mappings_by_layer) (model_runner.py:1985-1997). No change.
  • get_supported_kernel_block_sizes = [MultipleOf(64)] -> prepare_kernel_block_sizes (V31/vllm/v1/worker/utils.py:464-505) still yields kernel_block_size == block_size (blocks_per_kv_block == 1, matching our BLOCKS_PER_KV_BLOCK assumption). MRV2 may pad block-table width (get_block_table_width, token_alignment=128, model_runner.py:614-619); our builder only indexes columns it needs. No change.

13. Note for the compile slice

At v0.31, _get_v2_model_runner_unsupported_features lists CompilationMode.STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE ("stock torch.compile", V31/vllm/config/vllm.py:3056-3057), and use_v2_model_runner also returns False when Triton is missing (vllm.py:746-750) — both true for our default config. Our worker picks the runner class itself, but every config-side branch on vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner (e.g. vllm.py:597, 1070, 1169, 1266, 1291, 1627, 2051) would see False unless VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1 is forced. On main the stock-compile entry is gone and MRV2 calls compile_model_with_stock_torch itself (MAIN/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py:535-536) — which our load_model override must keep from double-compiling. Our attention-compile gating (_record_attention_graphs on CompilationMode.NONE, builder batched= on STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE, spyre_model_runner.py:1013-1015, 1157) is unaffected.

Suggested order of work (attention/KV slice)

  1. Fix initialize_kv_cache_tensors for the new KVCacheTensor (layers, shared backing size) and add supported_kv_cache_layouts() — needed for any bump past v0.28, V1 or MRV2.
  2. SpyreBlockTables + torch prepare_pos_seq_lens (method-level re-impl, no pointer tables).
  3. Spyre initialize_kv_cache override: window split + builders, SpyreBlockTables, our allocator, .device-safe kv_caches.
  4. Dispatch override for token buckets; model-state prepare_attn wrapper for the encoder plan.
  5. Rework warmup dummy runs now that MRV2 executes attention in them; drop publish_null_slots and the pooling seq_lens clamp once verified.
A6. Batching, input prep, bucketing, buffers, async (06_batching_inputprep.md)

06 — Batching, input preparation, shape bucketing, host/device buffers, async execution

Scope: what TorchSpyreModelRunner does today on top of the V1 GPUModelRunner (vLLM v0.28.0 pin) for batch formation, input prep, bucketing, CPU<->Spyre buffers and async output; and how each piece maps onto Model Runner V2 (vllm/v1/worker/gpu/) at v0.31.0 and main (v0.31.1rc0-149-gbab0ac61f).

Paths: SI=/home/senuser/spyre-inference/spyre_inference, G31=vllm-v0.31.0/vllm/v1/worker/gpu, GM=vllm-main/vllm/v1/worker/gpu. Except where noted, v0.31.0 and main are identical for the slice files. input_batch.py, dp_utils.py and model_states/default.py have no diff. block_table.py, buffer_utils.py, states.py and async_utils.py differ by 4–12 lines, none of which matters here. main's NonUvaBuffer copy is non_blocking=True, and main adds use_dense_all_token_ids for ngram.

Classification legend: no change / re-target (same idea, new hook) / redesign / blocker.

0. Headline findings

  1. None of the V1 hooks we override exist in MRV2. _make_buffer/CpuGpuBuffer, _preprocess, _determine_batch_execution_and_padding, get_dp_padding, _sync_device, _build_attention_metadata, _pool, _init_device_properties, self.input_batch (persistent V1 InputBatch) and the patch target vllm.v1.worker.block_table._compute_slot_mapping_kernel are all absent from G31/ (grep is empty). Every override in SI/v1/worker/spyre_model_runner.py lines 209-263, 1235-1305, 1521-1624, 1706-1807 and 1877-1925 has to be re-homed.
  2. MRV2 keeps persistent request state on self.device, and the device copy is the source of truth. This covers num_computed_tokens, last_sampled_tokens, all_token_ids, total_len, block tables and slot mappings. A Triton kernel (post_update) advances that state after sampling, and next-step decode input_ids are read back from it on device (combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens). The host keeps only "optimistic upper bound" mirrors (G31/states.py:57-62, G31/model_runner.py:1196-1214). Spyre cannot hold this state: int32 is not supported, there is no index.Tensor, and there are no Triton or pointer tables. The only viable mapping is device=cpu, exactly as today: all MRV2 state and input buffers live on the CPU, and the model wrapper does the H2D at the model boundary.
  3. The input-prep Triton kernels are the "Spyre-native input prep" work. Spyre has no Triton (pyproject bans it: triton; sys_platform == 'never', pyproject.toml:77-78). With the placeholder, @triton.jit returns the bare Python function, so kernel[(grid,)](...) raises TypeError. 8 kernels sit on the always-on decoder path and need CPU reimplementations; 4 more sit on optional paths. Upstream's CPU MRV2 gets these by shipping triton-cpu plus a global monkey-patch shim (vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py), which is a template for us.
  4. Bucketing slots cleanly into CudaGraphManager.dispatch(). MRV2 has exactly one padding decision, dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp -> cudagraph_manager.dispatch() -> BatchExecutionDescriptor.num_tokens. The runner then uses num_tokens_after_padding = max(num_tokens, batch_desc.num_tokens) (G31/model_runner.py:1326). A SpyreBucketManager whose dispatch returns BatchExecutionDescriptor(cg_mode=NONE, num_tokens=bucket, num_reqs=num_reqs) replaces _determine_batch_execution_and_padding + _spyre_bucket_batch_descriptor. Classification: re-target, and it is simpler than today.
  5. Async scheduling does not have to be disabled. The platform can still force scheduler_config.async_scheduling=False. MRV2 always returns AsyncOutput/AsyncPoolingOutput objects built on torch.cuda.Event/torch.cuda.Stream/torch.cuda.set_stream (G31/async_utils.py:115-261), and they need a shim on a CPU-only torch. With device=cpu the step is synchronous, so async scheduling is correct but buys little. One real hazard: AsyncPoolingOutput issues .to("cpu", non_blocking=True) on Spyre tensors when pooling stays on device, and fences them only with a CUDA event that the shim turns into a no-op. That fence must become torch.spyre.synchronize().
  6. Config gate, flagged for the config slice. VllmConfig.use_v2_model_runner returns False when HAS_TRITON is False (vllm-v0.31.0/vllm/config/vllm.py:~754, main :754). Forcing VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1 makes _validate_v2_model_runner raise "Model Runner V2 requires Triton" (main config/vllm.py:3242-3245). Our worker builds its own runner, so subclassing MRV2 works with the flag left False. But the config and the GPU worker would believe they drive V1 (gpu_worker.py:478,507,873,959; max_concurrent_batches at config/vllm.py:590-601). This is a blocker only if v0.32 drops the no-Triton fallback; otherwise it is a re-target.

1. What we do today (V1, v0.28.0)

Mechanism Where What it does
self.device = cpu, real device in _spyre_device SI/v1/worker/spyre_model_runner.py:571-614 Whole V1 runner built on CPU under _torch_cuda_wrapper() (stubs torch.cuda.Stream/torch.Event during init).
SpyreCpuGpuBuffer + _make_buffer :209-263, :1900-1925 Float buffers: .cpu host, .gpu on Spyre fp16 (H2D non_blocking=True); int/bool: .gpu is .cpu. Only consumer of a Spyre .gpu is inputs_embeds (passed to wrapper as staging buffer, :733). Tests: tests/runtime/test_spyre_cpu_gpu_buffer.py.

_compute_slot_mapping_impl + _FuncWrapper :144-206, patched at :636-642 Pure-torch replacement of V1 block_table._compute_slot_mapping_kernel (Triton), with kernel[grid](...) syntax shim. Per-request Python loop with .item().

_SpyreModelWrapper.embed_input_ids :474-546 Pads input_ids to the body bucket (TP all_reduce schedules), embeds on Spyre, stages result in the Spyre inputs_embeds.gpu buffer so upstream's inputs_embeds.gpu[:n].copy_() is a self-copy (torch-spyre#4730/#4883).

_preprocess override :1521-1624 Pooling rectangular path: expands packed ids/positions/token_type_ids/embeds into the dense [B*L] grid (host Python lists, #981), RoBERTa position offset.
_dummy_run override :1307-1343 force_attention=True for pooling; publishes null KV slots; forces D2H.
_pool override :1706-1807 Pools on Spyre; reads V1 self.input_batch, optimistic_seq_lens_cpu; asserts not self.use_async_scheduling.

Attention metadata consumer SI/v1/attention/backends/spyre_attn.py:672-910 Reads common_attn_metadata.seq_lens, query_start_loc, block_table_tensor, slot_mapping and does .item()/.tolist() on them — requires them to be host tensors. Publishes the bucket-padded slot_mapping (:868-870). reorder_batch_threshold=1 when batched decode (:388).
Async scheduling implicit Decoder runs with V1 default async_scheduling=True (v0.28 config/vllm.py:1234); works because every "async" stream op is a CPU no-op. Pooling defaults to False upstream; we assert it.

2. MRV2 mechanisms, one by one

2.1 Request state — RequestState (G31/states.py:9-130)

  • all_token_ids StagedWriteTensor[max_num_reqs, max_model_len] int32, uva_instead_of_gpu=True (:34-39); prompt_len/prefill_len UvaBackedTensor (:49-50); total_len, num_computed_tokens StagedWriteTensor on device (:52-60); host mirrors num_computed_tokens_np (optimistic upper bound), num_computed_prefill_tokens (:57-62); last_sampled_tokens [max_num_reqs,1] int64 on device (:65-67); draft_tokens, next_prefill_tokens on device.
  • Slot-indexed (free list), no persistent-batch condense/swap; update_requests writes only the numpy mirror of num_computed_tokens (G31/model_runner.py:1205-1210); the device value is advanced by post_update after sampling (G31/input_batch.py:556-654) or post_update_num_computed_tokens for pooling (:657-686).
  • Spyre: cannot live on Spyre (int32, scatter/gather by index, Triton). With device=cpu it is all host memory; all_token_ids is max_num_reqs*max_model_len*4 B (e.g. 32×32k → 4 MiB) — fine.
  • Classification: no change (pass device=cpu), provided the kernels below are replaced.

2.2 Buffers — buffer_utils.py (G31/buffer_utils.py)

  • UvaBuffer (:29-38) raises unless is_uva_available(); is_uva_available = is_pin_memory_available() or current_platform.is_cpu() (vllm/utils/platform_utils.py:50-57). Our platform: CpuPlatform.is_pin_memory_available() → False (platforms/cpu.py:583) and _enum = OOT so is_cpu() False → UVA unavailable.
  • Fallback NonUvaBuffer (:41-58) allocates _uva on current_platform.device_type — ours is "cpu" (SI/platform.py:118-119) — so it is a redundant CPU→CPU copy per access. Works; upstream CPU instead swaps UvaBuffer for a zero-copy alias (vllm/v1/worker/cpu/buffer_utils.py, installed by cpu/shm.py:96-98).
  • UvaBufferPool round-robins max_concurrency (= max_concurrent_batches, G31/model_runner.py:229) slots; StagedWriteTensor.apply_write (:196-227) and FusedStagedWriter.apply (:251-297) launch the Triton _apply_write_kernel (:300-335) — pointer-table kernel (_load_ptr of data_ptr() stored as uint64, :338-342, block_table.py:91-95).
  • async_tensor_h2d (vllm/utils/torch_utils.py:703-) — fine on CPU with PIN_MEMORY=False.
  • Classification: UvaBuffer/UvaBackedTensor/pools: no change (NonUva path) or re-target (alias shim like upstream CPU, saves a copy per step). apply_write / fused writer: redesign (CPU impl — a numpy scatter of contents[cu_start:cu_end] into gpu[row, start:start+len]; ~30 LOC). Our SpyreCpuGpuBuffer / _make_buffer: delete (no MRV2 consumer).

2.3 Input buffers & InputBatch (G31/input_batch.py:19-220)

  • InputBuffers: input_ids int32, positions int64, is_padding bool, query_start_loc, seq_lens, dcp_local_seq_lens — all on device (:19-35). With device=cpu these are host tensors handed straight to the model (G31/model_runner.py:1915-1921); our wrapper's int→Spyre-int64 conversion keeps working.
  • InputBatch dataclass (:38-124) carries both host (*_np, seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound) and device views; input_ids/positions sliced to num_tokens_after_padding; the pad tail is stale (not zeroed) except slot_mapping (PAD) — same as V1.
  • make_dummy (:126-220) — even split, seq_lens = query_lens, uses torch.cumsum(out=...) on device; fine on CPU.
  • Classification: no change for buffers; our consumers of V1 self.input_batch (_pool) → re-target to InputBatch + PoolingRunner (pooling slice).

2.4 Input-prep kernels (the core work)

Called from prepare_inputs (G31/model_runner.py:1318-1518), prepare_attn (:1520-1538), postprocess_sampled (:1636-1664), postprocess_num_computed_tokens (:2291-2297), sampler.

Kernel Def Call site Always on? CPU replacement
_apply_write_kernel (single + MULTI_GROUP) buffer_utils.py:300 StagedWriteTensor.apply_write :215, FusedStagedWriter.apply :285 (every add_requests, every step via block_tables.apply_staged_writes model_runner.py:1694) yes numpy ragged scatter; override methods (pointer tables unusable).
_gather_block_tables_kernel block_table.py:241-278 gather_block_tables :153-180 ← prepare_attn model_runner.py:1527 yes out[:n, :] = src[idx_mapping]; zero padded rows; per group. Override method.
_compute_slot_mappings_kernel block_table.py:281-366 compute_slot_mappings :195-227 ← model_runner.py:1532 yes vectorised req_idx = repeat_interleave(arange, qlens); bt[idx[req], pos//kbs]*kbs + pos%kbs; PAD tail to max_num_batched_tokens; -1 idx → PAD. Replaces our V1 _compute_slot_mapping_impl (SI/...:144-193, which loops with .item()). Override method.
_prepare_prefill_inputs_kernel input_batch.py:269-343 model_runner.py:1414 (if has_prefill) yes (prefill) copy all_token_ids[req, nc:nc+q] into input_ids[qs:qe] + lookahead tokens.

_combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens_kernel input_batch.py:404-503 model_runner.py:1436 yes logits_indices = query_end - num_logits + arange; write last_sampled_tokens[req] into the decode slot when seq_len > prefill_len. This is how decode input ids are produced — no host token list.
_post_update_kernel input_batch.py:555-654 postprocess_sampled model_runner.py:1649 yes (generate) update last_sampled_tokens, total_len, all_token_ids append, output_bin_counts (penalties), num_computed_tokens += q - rejected.
_post_update_num_computed_tokens_kernel input_batch.py:657-686 pool() model_runner.py:2291-2297 yes (pooling) nc[idx] += q.
_get_num_sampled_and_rejected_kernel input_batch.py:506-552 sample/sampler.py:193, rejection_sampler.py:392 yes (generate) where(seq_len < prefill_len, 0, …). (Sampler slice, listed for completeness.)
_expand_idx_mapping_kernel input_batch.py:689-725 model_runner.py:1406 spec-decode only skip (we reject drafters, SI/...:673-676).
_prepare_rope_positions_kernel mm/rope.py:144 DefaultModelState.prepare_inputs (M-RoPE models) MM M-RoPE only needed for Qwen-VL-style models.
_apply_prompt_embeds_kernel model_states/prompt_embeds.py:99 --enable-prompt-embeds optional defer.
cp_utils.py kernels (2) cp_utils.py DCP only no skip.

Patching mechanics: the free functions are imported by name into model_runner.py (G31/model_runner.py:111-121) and sampler.py (sample/sampler.py:20), so they must be patched in both the defining and the importing module namespaces, or the call sites overridden (prepare_inputs is ~200 lines — copying it is the worst option). The BlockTables/StagedWriteTensor/FusedStagedWriter methods are best handled by subclass or method patch. Today's single-symbol _FuncWrapper trick (SI/...:196-206,636-642) does not scale: half these kernels take pointer tables.

Implementation note: SI/v1/worker/spyre_shape_bucketer.py:340-341 and SI/...:1509-1510 document (#981) that every extra CPU aten op on the per-step path lengthens torch-spyre's shared eager-op guard chain. The replacements should therefore be numpy on the .numpy() views of the CPU tensors (zero-copy for int32/int64), not torch ops, and vectorised over requests (no per-request .item() like our current slot-mapping impl).

Alternative: adopt triton-cpu like upstream CPU (git log 373592ef5 "[CPU] Ship triton-cpu wheel"); would make every kernel (incl. sampler slice) run unmodified, but flips HAS_TRITON=True across all of vLLM (changes custom-op/kernel selection globally and makes use_v2_model_runner default True) and contradicts the explicit ban in pyproject.toml:77-78 — needs an explicit decision and an inductor/torch-spyre interaction check.

Classification: redesign (8 always-on kernels, pure host reimplementation) — or re-target if triton-cpu is accepted.

2.5 Batch ordering (sort_batch_req_ids, G31/model_runner.py:2398-2410)

  • Orders decode → short-extend → prefill on every step (no persistent-batch reorder / reorder_batch_threshold plumbing). Matches what SpyreAttentionMetadataBuilder needs (split_decodes_and_prefills).
  • Subtle change: MRV2 always passes is_prefilling (model_states/default.py:229), so our builder's treat_short_extends_as_decodes=common_attn_metadata.is_prefilling is None (SI/.../spyre_attn.py:875) becomes False; 1-token prefill chunks sort among decodes (key ties) and split_decodes_and_prefills (vllm/v1/attention/backends/utils.py:858-866) then cuts at the first one → decodes after it take the per-sequence path. Correct, possibly slower for batched decode. no change (correctness) / tune later.

2.6 Attention metadata inputs (model_states/default.py:169-238, attn_utils.py:429-555)

  • Eager/piecewise (cg_mode != FULL) uses unpadded num_reqs/num_tokens for CommonAttentionMetadata (default.py:181-188), query_start_loc (device) + query_start_loc_cpu, seq_lens (device), seq_lens_cpu_upper_bound, block_table_tensor, slot_mapping (padded to num_tokens_after_padding, PAD tail — block_table.py:306-315, model_runner.py:1532-1537).
  • With device=cpu, all "device" fields are host tensors — our builder's .item()/.tolist() keep working; seq_lens is exact (computed from host num_computed_tokens), not just the upper bound. Padded slot_mapping is what _slot_mapping.publish expects (SI/.../spyre_attn.py:868-870). no change for decoder.
  • MRV2 dummy runs build attention metadata unless skip_attn (profile only) (model_runner.py:1810-1830, 1856-1882), unlike V1 which passed attn_metadata=None unless force_attention. Our force_attention plumbing in _dummy_run (SI/...:1319-1321) becomes unnecessary; dummy runs will now reach the attention kernels with seq_len == query_len (context 0) — check against _record_attention_graphs expectations. re-target.
  • Pooling encoder plan (_build_attention_metadata override, SI/...:1103-1180): no such hook; move into a Spyre ModelState.prepare_attn (subclass of EncoderOnlyModelState, model_states/encoder_only.py:162-217), reading input_batch.num_tokens_after_padding instead of the num_tokens_padded kwarg / _encoder_buffer_rows. re-target.

2.7 Padding / shape bucketing (cudagraph_utils.py, dp_utils.py)

  • The only padding source is dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp (dp_utils.py:213-325) → cudagraph_manager.dispatch (cudagraph_utils.py:499-529) → BatchExecutionDescriptor (:63-77). With CUDAGraphMode.NONE (our backend is AttentionCGSupport.NEVER, SI/.../spyre_attn.py:320; resolve_cudagraph_mode_and_sizes → NONE, config/compilation.py:1386-1388) _init_candidates returns early and dispatch returns the unpadded count.
  • dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp asserts cudagraph_manager is not None unless need_eager (dp_utils.py:278-281); the manager is built in initialize_kv_cache (model_runner.py:720-728).
  • Consumers of the padded size: prepare_inputs (:1326, num_reqs_padded = batch_desc.num_reqs or num_reqs :1395), compute_slot_mappings(num_tokens_padded=...), InputBatch.make_dummy(batch_desc.num_tokens) (:1795), forward context num_tokens + BatchDescriptor(num_tokens=num_tokens_after_padding) (:1971-1990), model_state.prepare_inputs_embeds slice (default.py:133).
  • Where bucketing slots in: subclass ModelCudaGraphManager (or CudaGraphManager) as SpyreBucketManager; dispatch() returns BatchExecutionDescriptor(cg_mode=NONE, num_tokens=bucketer.find_bucket(n) or n, num_reqs=num_reqs); needs_capture() → False. Install it after super().initialize_kv_cache(). Keep num_reqs=num_reqs so no request padding (FULL-only semantics). SpyreShapeBucketer itself: no change. compile_sizes stays our bucket carrier (it is ignored for STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE cudagraphs; MRV2 worker's own warmup loop over compile_sizes, gpu_worker.py:830-871, is bypassed by our compile_or_warm_up_model override, SI/v1/worker/spyre_worker.py:177-193). Note main adds compile_model_with_stock_torch in MRV2 load_model for STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE — we override load_model, but the load-model slice must make sure that is not double-applied.
  • Logit-row bucketing stays in the wrapper's compute_logits (sample() still does hidden_states[input_batch.logits_indices] → self.model.compute_logits, model_runner.py:1578-1579). no change.
  • Classification: re-target (≈40 LOC; deletes _determine_batch_execution_and_padding, _spyre_bucket_batch_descriptor, get_dp_padding).

2.8 DP (dp_utils.py)

  • dp_size==1 returns before any collective (dp_utils.py:290-291); DP>1 is rejected by the platform (SI/platform.py check_and_update_config). no change.

2.9 Model inputs on the same device as state?

  • Yes — MRV2 hands input_buffers.input_ids/positions (device) straight to self.model(**model_inputs) (model_runner.py:1915-1921, 2007) and builds metadata from the same device. With device=cpu that is the V1 situation we already handle: _SpyreModelWrapper.__call__ does the H2D (SI/...:387-414). no change.
  • Exception: inputs_embeds. MRV2 allocates encoder_runner.inputs_embeds (mm/encoder_runner.py:58-60) and DefaultModelState.inputs_embeds (default.py:49-51) on self.device (CPU) and writes self.inputs_embeds[:n] = x (encoder_runner.py:300-305). Our embed_input_ids returns a Spyre tensor, so this becomes a D2H, and the CPU float inputs_embeds then reaches the model unconverted (wrapper converts only int tensors, SI/...:392-398). It also loses the torch-spyre#4730 self-copy trick that needs the buffer on Spyre (SI/...:483-546). Fix: after model_state construction, replace those buffers with Spyre-resident ones (and pass to the wrapper as inputs_embeds_buffer), or convert floats in the wrapper. re-target (multimodal).
  • Pooling _preprocess grid expansion (SI/...:1521-1624) → ModelState.prepare_inputs, whose returned dict overrides input_ids/positions and adds token_type_ids (model_runner.py:1917-1921; EncoderOnlyModelState.prepare_inputs encoder_only.py:120-145). Note inputs_embeds is computed before prepare_inputs and only input_ids is dropped if embeds exist — the embeds-path grid (CLIP) must go through prepare_inputs_embeds. re-target.

2.10 Streams, events, async output (async_utils.py, model_runner.py)

  • torch.cuda.Stream(self.device) in __init__ (model_runner.py:220) — covered today by _torch_cuda_wrapper only during super().__init__.
  • main_stream → torch.cuda.current_stream(self.device) (:555-558, cached property, used per step).
  • AsyncOutput.__init__ (async_utils.py:115-171): torch.cuda.Event(blocking=True), torch.cuda.set_stream, x.to("cpu", non_blocking=True).numpy(), copy_event.record/synchronize. AsyncPoolingOutput same (:220-261). StepTimingCollector events only under collect() (adaptive verification; unused).
  • On a CPU-only torch these raise (dummy _CudaEventBase). Upstream CPU MRV2 solves it with a process-wide shim imported by the CPU worker (vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py:30-92: placeholders for torch.Event, torch.cuda.Event/Stream/set_stream/current_stream/stream, Tensor.pin_memory/record_stream, a sync async_tensor_h2d, UvaBuffer alias, tl.debug_barrier).
  • Spyre-specific hazard: sampler outputs are host tensors (logits come back to CPU), so the no-op event is safe for generate. AsyncPoolingOutput with _pooling_on_spyre issues non-blocking D2H from Spyre and relies on the event for ordering — the placeholder must call torch.spyre.synchronize() in synchronize() (torch-spyre has internal streams/events but no torch Event API; KB wiki/artifacts/designs/stream-synchronization.md). Replaces our _sync_device usage.
  • Classification: re-target (≈60–100 LOC shim, or import upstream cpu/shm.py + a Spyre-sync event override; importing upstream's internal module is cheaper but couples us to it).

2.11 Async scheduling

  • Default stays on for decoders (config/vllm.py:1576-1635), off for pooling (:1578-1587).
  • MRV2 is built for it: inputs come from on-device state, the host only keeps optimistic mirrors. With device=cpu every kernel replacement runs synchronously in program order, so results are exact; the only thing "async" buys is the scheduler/output thread overlapping the next schedule with get_output.
  • It can be disabled. Set scheduler_config.async_scheduling = False in TorchSpyrePlatform.check_and_update_config when it is None (the same knob as --no-async-scheduling); MRV2 still returns AsyncOutput, and the executors call get_output() synchronously (executor/uniproc_executor.py:104-110, multiproc_executor.py:991-993).
  • Recommendation: keep async on for decoders (it works today under V1 and the CPU-device design makes it safe), keep it off for pooling, and drop the _pool assert once the pooling fence (2.10) is a real Spyre sync. no change, plus the fence.

2.12 Scheduler (SI/v1/core/scheduler.py)

  • Upstream Scheduler still has max_num_running_reqs and num_waiting_for_streaming_input on v0.31.0 and main (v1/core/sched/scheduler.py:129, 244, 911). MRV2 does not change the scheduler. no change.

2.13 Warmup / dummy runs

  • MRV2 _dummy_run(num_tokens, ...) (model_runner.py:785-940) dispatches through the cudagraph manager, so dummy runs at bucket sizes get exactly the bucket via SpyreBucketManager. It builds dummy attention metadata. It returns (hidden_states, hidden_states[logits_indices]), the same tuple shape our warmup consumes (SI/...:956-966).
  • Our _dummy_run keyword plumbing (force_attention) is gone; skip_attn is profile-only. The publish_null_slots + D2H-forcing wrapper stays. _dummy_sampler_run uses InputBatch.make_dummy plus MRV2 sampler states (sampler slice). re-target.

3. Size estimate for "Spyre-native input prep" (assuming device=cpu, no triton-cpu)

Item Class New/moved LOC Notes
CUDA stream/event/pin shim + Spyre-sync event for pooling re-target 60–100 or import vllm.v1.worker.cpu.shm + small override
StagedWriteTensor.apply_write / FusedStagedWriter.apply redesign 30–50 numpy ragged scatter
BlockTables.gather_block_tables / compute_slot_mappings redesign 50–80 vectorised numpy; deletes V1 _compute_slot_mapping_impl
prepare_prefill_inputs, prepare_pos_seq_lens, combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens redesign 80–120 must be bit-exact (decode token ids come from here)
post_update, post_update_num_computed_tokens, get_num_sampled_and_rejected redesign 50–80 incl. output_bin_counts update for penalties
Patch installation (two namespaces per free function) + guard test that every @triton.jit on the hot path is replaced redesign 40–60 protects against vLLM bumps silently re-routing to a placeholder
SpyreBucketManager (dispatch → bucket) re-target 30–50 replaces 3 V1 overrides
inputs_embeds buffers on Spyre (MM / prompt-embeds) re-target 20–40
Pooling grid → ModelState.prepare_inputs / prepare_attn re-target 100–150 moved mostly existing code
Unit tests (CPU-only, kernel semantics vs hand-built cases; bucket dispatch; grid) — 300–500 no Triton reference available locally
Deletions — −250 SpyreCpuGpuBuffer, _make_buffer, _FuncWrapper, _compute_slot_mapping_impl, padding/dp/sync overrides, their tests

Rough total: about 450–700 LOC of production code (about 300 of it new kernel replacements) plus 300–500 LOC of tests. That is about 2–3 engineer-weeks, including on-hardware validation of decode correctness and per-step host overhead (the replacements run on every step, and their cost lands on TTFT/ITL). With triton-cpu the kernel rows mostly disappear; the shim, bucket manager and re-targets remain (about 1 week), but the dependency and HAS_TRITON change need a separate decision.

Order of risk: (1) the HAS_TRITON/use_v2_model_runner config gate (blocker if v0.32 removes the V1 fallback without relaxing the gate); (2) bit-exactness of combine_sampled_and_draft_tokens/post_update, since decode tokens no longer come from the scheduler; (3) the pooling non-blocking D2H fence; (4) the multimodal inputs_embeds buffer device.

A7. Sampling, logits, pooling, multimodal, spec decode, LoRA (07_sampling_pooling_mm.md)

07 — Sampling, logits, pooling, multimodal/encoder runner: V1 → MRV2

Scope: spyre-inference @ 8403f33 (pins vLLM v0.28.0) against MRV2 in vllm/v1/worker/gpu/ at v0.28.0, v0.29.0, v0.31.0 and main. Paths: SI = /home/senuser/spyre-inference, V31 / VM / V28 = the vllm-v0.31.0 / vllm-main / vllm-v0.28.0 checkouts under /home/senuser/.claude/jobs/709f9980/tmp/.

Classification key: no change | re-target (the logic survives, but the hook moves) | redesign (a new Spyre-side implementation of an MRV2 component is needed) | blocker (cannot ship without an upstream change or a large new subsystem).

0. Cross-cutting facts that frame everything below

  1. MRV2 sampling is Triton end to end, and nothing falls back to torch. Every sampled token, greedy ones included, goes through gumbel_sample → _gumbel_sample_kernel (V31/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/sample/gumbel.py:326,356; Sampler._sample_random, sample/sampler.py:324-353). Greedy is temperature=0 inside that kernel; there is no argmax branch. The kernel files (and @triton.jit counts) at v0.31: gumbel (13), logprob (3: top-k log-softmax, ranks, fill-token-ids), penalties (2: penalties, bincount), min_p, logit_bias, bad_words, prompt_logprob, output (sampling mask), thinking_budget (3), watermark (11), batch_shard (5), trace_replay, plus structured_outputs.py:124 (bitmask), mm/rope.py:144 (M-RoPE positions) and 7 kernels in input_batch.py (post_update, get_num_sampled_and_rejected, …) that the sampler path calls. main has the same set (the only diffs are in sampler/gumbel/prompt_logprob/output). The only torch pieces are apply_top_k_top_p (shared with V1, V31/vllm/v1/sample/ops/topk_topp_sampler.py:449) and torch.topk in compute_topk_scores.
  2. We have no Triton, by design. SI/pyproject.toml:77-78 pins triton; sys_platform == 'never', and SI/spyre_inference/platform.py:33-34 evicts any triton module. vllm.triton_utils therefore installs TritonPlaceholder, whose jit returns the raw Python function (V31/vllm/triton_utils/importing.py _dummy_decorator). Launching kernel[grid](...) on it then fails, because a plain function cannot be subscripted.
  3. Upstream does not select MRV2 without Triton. VllmConfig.use_v2_model_runner returns False when not HAS_TRITON (V31/vllm/config/vllm.py:746). Forcing VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1 raises ValueError("Model Runner V2 requires Triton.") in _validate_v2_model_runner (V31 config/vllm.py:3315-3318, called at :2051; main :3242-3245, called at :2048). The flag also drives the scheduler and input processor (v1/core/sched/scheduler.py use_v2_model_runner). So the engine reports "V1" even if our worker builds an MRV2-derived runner. Blocker (cross-slice): it needs an upstream platform hook ("V2 without Triton, kernels overridden") or a stopgap patch. At v0.31 "stock torch.compile" is also on the V2-unsupported list (V31 config/vllm.py:3057); it is gone on main.
  4. There is an override seam upstream, new since v0.29 and absent at v0.28. vllm/triton_utils/dispatcher.py register_kernels({"mod.attr": impl}) swaps a Triton kernel, and every alias of it, for a Python callable launched as kernel[grid](...). It is invoked through Platform.register_triton_kernel_overrides() (V31/vllm/platforms/interface.py:374), which worker_base.py:275 calls (main :282). CpuPlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides (V31/vllm/platforms/cpu.py:651-700) registers C++ fallbacks only for V1 kernels (slot mapping, eagle, rejection sampler, mamba memcpy). It registers none of the v1/worker/gpu/** kernels. Upstream's CPU MRV2 (V31/vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py, 16 lines) simply assumes triton-cpu is present. This seam is the natural vehicle for a Spyre torch-on-CPU port of the sampler kernels.
  5. Today, sampling runs on the host. The lm_head matmul runs on Spyre (SpyreUnquantizedLMHeadMethod.apply, custom_ops/parallel_lm_head.py:49-56). SpyreLogitsProcessor D2Hs the logits: in _apply_head at TP1, or after the on-device all-gather in _gather_logits at TP>1 (custom_ops/logits_processor.py:24-49). From there the V1 Sampler, its logits processors, logprobs and the grammar bitmask all run as torch ops on CPU tensors, because the runner keeps self.device = cpu (spyre_model_runner.py:604-614). MRV2 samples on self.device (gpu/model_runner.py:1556-1634), so a CPU-device MRV2 runner keeps the same placement. What breaks is only the Triton launches.

1. Sampling and logits

1.1 What we have today (V1)

Piece Where Notes
SpyreTopKTopPSampler swapped into self.sampler.topk_topp_sampler SI/spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_model_runner.py:616-619, SI/spyre_inference/v1/sample/topk_topp_sampler.py:35-61 Sort-free top-k (allow_cpu_sync=True), plus a log-space Exp(1) "Gumbel" draw in fp32/fp64 that avoids the softmax and fp16 log underflow. Tested in tests/runtime/test_spyre_topk_sampler.py, which imports V1 Sampler, SamplingMetadata and LogitsProcessors.
_SpyreModelWrapper.compute_logits spyre_model_runner.py:422-449 Pads the sampled-row count onto logits_row_buckets (spyre_shape_bucketer.py:409-412), does the H2D of the rows, then trims. Upstream slices hidden_states[logits_indices] on CPU because __call__ already D2H'd the model output (:405-408).
SpyreLogitsProcessor (OOT LogitsProcessor) custom_ops/logits_processor.py:24-49 Returns CPU logits on both TP paths.
SpyreParallelLMHead (OOT) custom_ops/parallel_lm_head.py:59-104 Pads the vocab to 64·TP; dynamic=False compile per row bucket.
Sampler warmup per row width spyre_model_runner.py:949-965 (self._dummy_sampler_run(widest_hidden_states[:rows])) Compiles the lm_head at each row bucket.

1.2 What MRV2 does

  • Sampler is built in load_model (V31 gpu/model_runner.py:438-509). It does not use TopKTopPSampler. The comment at :444 reads "V2 bypasses TopKTopPSampler", so our topk_topp_sampler swap has nothing to attach to.
  • Pipeline (sample/sampler.py:233-322): the logits are copied to fp32 only if some request needs processing. Then logit_bias → penalties → bad_words → custom LPs → thinking-budget → temperature → min_p, all Triton. Then apply_top_k_top_p (torch when HAS_TRITON is False) and the gumbel_sample Triton kernel.
  • On the torch fallback, apply_top_k_top_p passes allow_cpu_sync=current_platform.is_cpu() (topk_topp_sampler.py:458-459). We are PlatformEnum.OOT, so we get the sorting top-k path. That is the waste SpyreTopKTopPSampler exists to avoid.
  • The RNG semantics differ. MRV2 noise is a murmur3 hash of (seed, pos, vocab offset), so the draw is deterministic per position and independent of batch composition (gumbel.py:98-160). Ours is torch.Generator-based, as in V1. A torch port should reproduce the murmur3 stream if we want the seed semantics MRV2 users will expect.
  • _dummy_sampler_run(hidden_states) keeps the same one-argument signature (V31 gpu/model_runner.py:942-953), so our row-width warmup loop can stay. It now builds InputBatch.make_dummy and calls the Triton sampler.
  • compute_logits is still called as self.model.compute_logits(sample_hidden_states) after hidden_states[input_batch.logits_indices] (:1578-1579). Our wrapper's padding and D2H contract carries over unchanged.
  • The sampler output path calls the Triton kernels get_num_sampled_and_rejected and post_update (input_batch.py; sampler.py:200-206, model_runner.py:1636-1660). They belong to the input-batch slice (06), but they sit on every sampled step.

1.3 Verdicts

Item Verdict Work
lm_head / SpyreLogitsProcessor / SpyreParallelLMHead no change Model-layer OOT ops, independent of the runner.
_SpyreModelWrapper.compute_logits row padding no change Same call shape. The wrapper must be in place before MRV2 builds the sampler and model state (see §3.3).
SpyreTopKTopPSampler redesign Delete it. Put sort-free top-k plus the log-space draw into a Spyre Sampler subclass that overrides _sample_random, or into a register_kernels override of _gumbel_sample_kernel. Rewrite tests/runtime/test_spyre_topk_sampler.py against the MRV2 Sampler.
Every MRV2 sampler Triton kernel (temperature, gumbel, min_p, logit_bias, penalties+bincount, bad_words, logprob ×3, prompt_logprob, output mask, bitmask) blocker → redesign Port each to torch-on-CPU via Platform.register_triton_kernel_overrides() + register_kernels (v0.29+). That is roughly 15 kernels for the default path, before watermark, thinking-budget and batch-shard (all opt-in; reject them in check_and_update_config). Plus the input_batch.py kernels (slice 06). The dispatcher calls impls with the kernel's positional args (raw pointers become tensors), so each port is a re-implementation, not a wrapper.
use_v2_model_runner False without Triton blocker Upstream hook needed; see §0.3.

2. Logprobs, prompt logprobs, logits processors, structured outputs

Feature Spyre today (V1) MRV2 v0.31 / main Verdict
Sample logprobs Used. tests/e2e/test_model_quality.py:121, tests/e2e/test_kv_cache_determinism.py:87 request logprobs=N. V1 computes them in torch on CPU. compute_topk_scores = torch.topk + Triton _topk_log_softmax_kernel + _ranks_kernel (+ _fill_logprob_token_ids_kernel for per-request token ids) (sample/logprob.py:84-190). logprobs_mode handled (sampler.py:174-189). redesign (3 kernel ports)
Prompt logprobs Not tested (no prompt_logprobs anywhere in SI/tests or SI/spyre_inference). V1 _get_prompt_logprobs_dict would call our compute_logits on prompt-sized chunks. Those exceed logits_row_buckets (capped at max_num_reqs), so it would compile per shape. PromptLogprobsWorker (sample/prompt_logprob.py:30-226): Triton _prompt_logprobs_token_ids_kernel + compute_topk_scores, CHUNK_SIZE=1024 calls to compute_logits. redesign (1 kernel plus the logprob ports). The 1024-row chunks hit the same un-bucketed lm_head shape issue as in V1. Either reject prompt_logprobs or add a 1024 row bucket.
Built-in LPs (min_p, logit_bias / allowed_token_ids, min_tokens / stop-token masking, bad_words, penalties) Implicitly supported via V1 torch code on CPU. Untested on Spyre except defaults. Each is a Triton kernel in an MRV2 LogitsProcessor (logit_bias.py, penalties.py, bad_words.py, min_p.py; min_tokens is folded into LogitBiasState stop-token masking). Penalties also depend on output_bin_counts maintained by post_update (Triton). redesign (kernel ports)
Custom logits processors (--logits-processors, vllm.logits_processors entry points) Not used. V1 interface (vllm.v1.sample.logits_processor). v0.28/v0.29: MRV2 unsupported, so upstream falls back to V1 (V28 config/vllm.py "custom logits processors"). v0.31+: supported via a new MRV2-only interface vllm.v1.worker.gpu.sample.logits_processor.LogitsProcessor; V1-interface plugins are rejected (sample/logits_processor/loader.py:37-75). no change for Spyre (unused). Document that V1-interface LPs stop working.
Structured outputs (grammar bitmask) Not tested, but should work: V1 apply_grammar_bitmask has an explicit CPU branch using xgrammar's CPU kernel with an fp16→fp32 round-trip (V28/vllm/v1/structured_output/utils.py:151-175). StructuredOutputsWorker (V31 gpu/structured_outputs.py:38-150; main :75-196): torch.cuda.Stream() at construction and torch.cuda.stream(...) / current_stream().wait_stream per step, plus Triton _apply_grammar_bitmask_kernel. Built unconditionally for every generate model (model_runner.py:501-507). redesign. Override the kernel (or subclass the worker to call xgr.apply_token_bitmask_inplace on CPU) and keep a torch.cuda shim around construction (we already wrap super().__init__ in _torch_cuda_wrapper, spyre_model_runner.py:66,607, but MRV2 builds this in load_model, not __init__).
return_sampling_mask, enable_trace_replay, watermarking, thinking budget (reasoning_config), batch-sharded sampling Not used. MRV2 features, all Triton. Reject in check_and_update_config (re-target, small).

3. Multimodal / encoder runner

3.1 Today (V1)

  • Models: Ministral-3-14B (Pixtral tower), gemma-4-26B-A4B (tests/e2e/test_multimodal.py:30,34), granite-vision-4.1-4b (tests/e2e/test_granite_vision.py:39), BLIP-2 / CLIP patches (SI/spyre_inference/multimodal/__init__.py:33-72). The tower patches are model-level monkeypatches applied in load_model after .to(spyre) (spyre_model_runner.py:713): no change.
  • _SpyreModelWrapper.embed_multimodal (:451-472) H2Ds float mm kwargs and sets capture_scalar_outputs.
  • _SpyreModelWrapper.embed_input_ids (:474-546) buckets the token count (TP>1 all_reduce schedules), H2Ds ids, is_multimodal and mm embeds, and stages the result in self.inputs_embeds.gpu[:padded] and returns a prefix view (commit 2623972). Upstream's inputs_embeds.gpu[:n].copy_(…) then becomes a self-copy that torch-spyre skips, which avoids torch-spyre#4730 (narrowed-destination H2D) and a copy_from_d2d compile per distinct n. The buffer is wired at :722-733 (inputs_embeds_buffer=self.inputs_embeds.gpu); it lives on Spyre only because our _make_buffer override places float buffers there (:1900-1925).
  • _warmup_input_embedding (:975-993) compiles the embedding step that dummy runs skip.

3.2 MRV2 (v0.31 / main)

  • init_model_state(vllm_config, self.model, encoder_cache, device) runs inside GPUModelRunner.load_model (V31 gpu/model_runner.py:414-416). ModelState.__init__ builds an EncoderRunner(model=self.model, device=self.device) (model_states/interface.py:45-95).
  • EncoderRunner.execute_mm_encoder calls self.model.embed_multimodal(**batch) after group_and_batch_mm_kwargs(device=self.device) (mm/encoder_runner.py:155-180).
  • EncoderRunner.inputs_embeds = torch.zeros(max_num_tokens, hidden, device=self.device) (:58), and get_inputs_embeds does self.inputs_embeds[: x.shape[0]] = x (:294-305). This is the same narrowed-row copy that 2623972 works around, but into a different buffer, allocated on self.device (CPU for us), not via _make_buffer.
  • Text-only models never call embed_input_ids (model_states/default.py:100-133 is called only when uses_inputs_embeds, model_runner.py:1886), the same as V1.
  • get_rope_state is only for uses_mrope (mm/rope.py:122-141). None of our mm models (Pixtral/Mistral3, Gemma4, Granite4-Vision, CLIP) is M-RoPE, so the Triton _prepare_rope_positions_kernel is never reached: no change.
  • The encoder CUDA-graph manager is only built when cudagraph_mm_encoder is set (default False, V31 config/compilation.py:542). Gemma4 declares supports_encoder_cudagraph=True (gemma4_mm.py:1015-1024), so the platform should pin it off: re-target (one line).
  • The model can supply its own state class via model.get_model_state_cls() (model_states/__init__.py:28-33). Our wrapper's __getattr__ forwards to the model, so a get_model_state_cls defined on _SpyreModelWrapper is a clean hook to inject Spyre ModelState subclasses.
  • Gemma-4 multimodal is supported by MRV2 at v0.31 and main: nothing in _get_v2_model_runner_unsupported_features excludes it, and since v0.29 there is no "default V2 architecture" gate. Its mm_prefix_clamp_sliding_window / mm-prefix bidirectional ranges are handled in gpu/attn_utils.py and model_states/default.py. Note: our attention ignores mm-prefix bidirectional ranges in both runners (no mm_prefix handling anywhere in SI/spyre_inference). This is pre-existing and belongs to the attention slice.

3.3 Verdicts

Item Verdict Work
Vision-tower patches (multimodal/*.py, models/gemma4.py) no change No runner coupling.
Wrapper installation order re-target MRV2 captures self.model in init_model_state and the EncoderRunner inside load_model. Our wrapper is installed at the end of our own load_model (:720-733). Either do Spyre load → wrap → then init_model_state (override load_model fully), or re-point model_state.model and model_state.encoder_runner.model afterwards. Otherwise embed_multimodal gets CPU pixel_values and embed_input_ids bypasses bucketing.
embed_input_ids staging (2623972) re-target Pass model_state.encoder_runner.inputs_embeds as inputs_embeds_buffer, and allocate that buffer on Spyre at the model dtype (Spyre ModelState override, or replace the attribute after construction). Confirm that buf[:n] = buf[:n] (MRV2's __setitem__) hits the same torch-spyre self-copy skip as V1's .copy_. Note the also-Spyre DefaultModelState.inputs_embeds for prompt-embeds (default.py:46-50), which is unused by us.
embed_multimodal H2D no change Same call. Only the wrapper ordering above matters.
_warmup_input_embedding re-target Same need (MRV2 dummy runs use dummy_inputs_embeds, default.py:94-98). Call it from the MRV2 warmup.
Encoder cache, gather_mm_embeddings (CPU is_mm_embed) no change Host-side bookkeeping.
Prompt embeds Not supported today (upstream tests skip use_prompt_embeds/enable_prompt_embeds, tests/plugin/spyre_testing_plugin/upstream_tests.yaml). MRV2 at v0.31 supports it (model_states/prompt_embeds.py), but EncoderOnlyModelState.supports_prompt_embeds=False. no change (keep it rejected).

4. Pooling

4.1 Today (V1)

  • Models (tests/e2e/test_encoder_models.py:35-81, plus upstream test_embedding.py): embed — granite-embedding-125m/278m/30m, multilingual-e5-large, all-roberta-large-v1, all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (L12 upstream), bge-base-en-v1.5; CLS / MEAN / LAST pooling. classify (rerank via llm.score): bge-reranker-v2-m3, bge-reranker-large. token_classify: dslim/bert-base-NER. Plus CLIP pooling (embeds-only input path in _preprocess).
  • Runner overrides, none of which exist in MRV2:
    • _build_attention_metadata (:1103-1180) picks rectangular vs ragged encoder plans and attaches encoder_plan.
    • _determine_batch_execution_and_padding (:1235-1284) pads to the token budget.
    • _preprocess (:1521-1620) does the rectangular grid expansion of ids, positions, embeds and token_type_ids, plus the RoBERTa position offset.
    • _rectangular_pooling (:1626-1660).
    • _dummy_pooler_run_task (:1662-1704) builds the cursor on CPU.
    • _pool (:1706-1817) builds the cursor on CPU, runs CLS/MEAN directly on the grid, otherwise _unpad_encoder_hidden, calls late_interaction_runner, and does the Spyre-safe D2H through copy_pooler_output_to_cpu. It asserts not use_async_scheduling.
    • _dummy_run (:1308-1343) forces force_attention=True and toggles D2H. The warm-up helpers _warm_pooler_row_widths, _warm_encoder_inline_paths and _warm_encoder_unpack (:1346-1487) also live here.
  • Pooler-module patches (SI/spyre_inference/v1/pool/spyre_pooler.py, configure_pooling_for_spyre :797) are model-level: no change, apart from one V1-private import. copy_pooler_output_to_cpu imports vllm.v1.worker.gpu_model_runner._copy_pooler_output_to_cpu (:111-113).

4.2 MRV2

  • Pooling has been supported since MRV2 v0.28. Since v0.29 there is no "runner_type == generate" default gate (V28 _is_default_v2_model_runner_model returned False for pooling; it is gone in v0.29/v0.31/main). PoolingRunner (V31 gpu/pool/pooling_runner.py, 208 lines; main 215, which adds a flash late-interaction toggle) supports embed, classify, token_embed, token_classify and embed&token_classify (:20-22). All our tasks are covered; only plugin is not.
  • EncoderOnlyModelState (model_states/encoder_only.py) builds non-causal attention groups and their metadata, token_type_ids (from compressed_token_type_ids), and dummy block-table and slot tensors. It is selected automatically when any layer is ENCODER_ONLY (model_states/__init__.py:44-50).
  • The PoolingRunner.pool slices hidden_states[: input_batch.num_tokens] (:138). That is a dim-0 slice of a Spyre tensor, which our _pool docstring calls unsafe. It then builds the cursor with device=hidden_states.device and query_start_loc_gpu (:144-150), so the cursor would land on Spyre or mismatch devices and raise. Our pools require host cursors.
  • GPUModelRunner.pool (V31 model_runner.py:2249-2290) always returns AsyncPoolingOutput (async_utils.py:220-261). That class uses torch.cuda.Event and streams, .unbind()s the pooler output (dim-0 views on Spyre), and calls .to("cpu", non_blocking=True), which is not a valid Spyre D2H (spyre_pooler.py:105-109). post_update_num_computed_tokens is Triton.

4.3 Verdicts

Item Verdict Work
Pooler module patches (CLS/MEAN/LAST/AllPool, heads, fp16 downcast) no change —
copy_pooler_output_to_cpu V1-private import re-target Inline the finished-mask logic.
_pool + _dummy_pooler_run_task redesign Write a SpyrePoolingRunner(PoolingRunner) that overrides pool and _dummy_pooler_run_task (CPU cursor, no Spyre dim-0 slice, grid-direct CLS/MEAN, unpad). Override GPUModelRunner.pool to D2H via convert before wrapping, or return a sync ModelRunnerOutput (we already forbid async scheduling for pooling). Swap the runner in after load_model (self.pooling_runner = …, model_runner.py:509-510).

Inject it via the wrapper's get_model_state_cls. CLIP (embeds-only) needs the same in prepare_inputs_embeds.

5. Spec decode and LoRA

Item Today MRV2 Verdict
Spec decode Rejected: if hasattr(self, "drafter"): raise NotImplementedError (spyre_model_runner.py:673-676). MRV2 has no drafter. It always sets self.speculator (None unless speculative_config, model_runner.py:269-274), so our guard silently becomes a no-op. MRV2 supports eagle/eagle3/mtp/dflash/dspark/draft_model and, on main, ngram (VM config/vllm.py unsupported list: suffix, medusa, mlp_speculator, custom_class). The rejection sampler and all of spec_decode/ are Triton. re-target: reject speculative_config is not None in check_and_update_config, or test self.speculator is not None.
LoRA Rejected: if self.lora_config: raise (:670-671). LoraState is always built and lora_utils.py is shared via LoRAModelRunnerMixin. The punica kernels are platform/Triton anyway. no change (the guard still works). Moving it to the platform check is cleaner.
