7. Suggested order (for this slice)

  1. Platform: reject spec decode / watermark / thinking budget / trace replay / sampling mask / batch-shard / cudagraph_mm_encoder. Fix the drafter guard. Inline _copy_pooler_output_to_cpu. All of these work on V1 today.
  2. Implement TorchSpyrePlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides() with torch ports of the default-path sampler kernels. Unit-test them against the Triton reference on a GPU box, or against V1 Sampler semantics, on CPU tensors. This can land before the runner switch, since the hook exists from v0.29.
  3. MRV2 runner: wrapper-before-init_model_state; Spyre ModelState subclasses (encoder grid + plan, Spyre-resident inputs_embeds); SpyrePoolingRunner; Spyre Sampler subclass (sort-free top-k, log-space draw); StructuredOutputsWorker shim.
  4. Get upstream to accept "V2 without Triton when the platform overrides the kernels" (or patch use_v2_model_runner / _validate_v2_model_runner from the platform as a stopgap).
A8. Compilation, warmup, graph capture, memory, TP, sleep, profiler (08_compile_warmup_tp.md)

08 — Compilation, warmup, graph capture, memory profiling, TP, sleep, profiler (MRV2 migration, #906)

Scope: spyre_inference/v1/worker/{compile_guard.py,spyre_model_runner.py,spyre_worker.py}, spyre_inference/platform.py (compile bits), spyre_inference/distributed/spyre_communicator.py vs. MRV2 in vllm/v1/worker/gpu/ at v0.31.0 and main (bab0ac61f, 2026-10-08). Paths: SI/ = /home/senuser/spyre-inference, 31/ = vllm-v0.31.0, main/ = vllm-main, 28/ = vllm-v0.28.0.

Legend: no change / re-target (same idea, different hook) / redesign / blocker.

0. Headline findings

  1. Config gate: vLLM will not select MRV2 for Spyre on its own, and refuses it if forced. VllmConfig.use_v2_model_runner (31/vllm/config/vllm.py:701-764; main :709-772) returns False when not HAS_TRITON (31 :746, main :754) and, on v0.31.0, when mode == STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE (_get_v2_model_runner_unsupported_features, 31 :3056-3057). spyre-inference pins triton; sys_platform == 'never' (SI/pyproject.toml:77-78) so HAS_TRITON is False, and the platform forces STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE (SI/platform.py:289-301). Setting VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=1 runs _validate_v2_model_runner (31 :3316-3324, main :3242-3251), which raises "Model Runner V2 requires Triton." (and on 0.31.0 also "stock torch.compile"). This matters even though TorchSpyreWorker.init_device constructs the runner itself (SI/spyre_worker.py:163): ~40 non-runner call sites key off vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner (scheduler 31/vllm/v1/core/sched/scheduler.py:337,1378,1410,1697; async_scheduler.py:52; input_processor.py:99,135; max_concurrent_batches vllm.py:597; resolve_cudagraph_mode_and_sizes compilation.py:1500). Running an MRV2 runner under a config that says V1 is an engine/worker contract mismatch. Blocker (needs upstream: a platform hook to opt into MRV2 without Triton, or Triton-free MRV2; MRV2 has 95 @triton.jit kernels across 30 files under 31/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/ — other slices own that, but it is what makes the gate fail).
  2. STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE in MRV2 only exists from v0.31.1rc0 (vllm PR #59079, commit 4e59d53ab). On main, MRV2 load_model ends with compile_model_with_stock_torch(self.model, ...) (main/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py:535-536 → main/vllm/compilation/wrapper.py:53-61: model.compile(fullgraph=True, backend=init_backend(...)), no dynamic=False), and the config forces cudagraph_mode=NONE (_disable_cudagraphs_for_v2_stock_torch_compile, main/vllm.py:3220-3240, called at :2049). v0.32 will contain it; v0.31.0 does not. Spyre must suppress that whole-model compile (it would nest over the per-block compiles, pick automatic-dynamic shapes → SymInts that the Spyre backend rejects, and is not registered with compile_guard). Re-target (patch/override; see §1).
  3. CUDA graphs are cleanly inert for Spyre. CpuPlatform (and thus TorchSpyrePlatform) has support_static_graph_mode() == False (31/vllm/platforms/interface.py:1245-1247), so VllmConfig.__post_init__ sets cudagraph_mode = NONE (31/vllm.py:1913-1979, else-branch :1979). With NONE: CudaGraphManager.pool = None (31/cudagraph_utils.py:174; CUDAGraphMode.__bool__ exists, compilation.py:~109), _init_candidates returns early (:251-253), needs_capture() False (:388), dispatch() returns an unpadded cg_mode=NONE descriptor (:499-529), capture_model short-circuits with a warning (model_runner.py:1019-1033), profile_cudagraph_memory is only invoked for cuda_alike/xpu (gpu_worker.py:631-635). No change for capture itself.
  4. But the padding hook moves into the CUDA-graph manager. Spyre's body-bucket padding is injected via V1's _determine_batch_execution_and_padding (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:1235-1305), which does not exist in MRV2. MRV2 pads only through dispatch_cg_and_sync_dp(self.cudagraph_manager, ...) (31/model_runner.py:1740; 31/dp_utils.py:213-325) → cudagraph_manager.dispatch(), and prepare_inputs takes num_tokens_after_padding = max(num_tokens, batch_desc.num_tokens) (model_runner.py:1326). With NONE and no override, every distinct token count is a new shape → one block recompile each (dynamic=False) → compile_guard fires after arming. Re-target: subclass ModelCudaGraphManager with a dispatch() that returns BatchExecutionDescriptor(cg_mode=NONE, num_tokens=bucket(num_tokens), num_reqs=num_reqs) once the bucketer is warmed, and install it after super().initialize_kv_cache() (manager is built at model_runner.py:720-728). Highest-priority item for the recompile surface.
  5. MRV2 dummy runs run attention. V1 _dummy_run passes attn_metadata=None unless force_attention (28/gpu_model_runner.py:6125), which is why Spyre records attention kernels separately (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:995-1042) and passes force_attention=True for pooling (:1319-1320). MRV2 _dummy_run has no force_attention (silently swallowed by **kwargs, 31/model_runner.py:785-797), always builds dummy metadata unless skip_attn, and raises if skip_attn=True without is_profile=True (:798-801). Dummy batch: num_reqs=min(num_tokens, max_num_reqs), seq_len == query_len, zeroed block tables, slot mappings filled with PAD_SLOT_ID (input_batch.py:127-186; block_table.py:182-193, 229-238). So each body-bucket warmup dummy now reaches SpyreAttentionImpl and compiles attention variants at those dummy shapes, and the KV write sees -1 slots (Spyre clamps to the null slot, SI/attn_layer.py:68-73, so the traced graph matches a real step — publish_null_slots becomes redundant except for profile runs). Re-target warmup; still keep _record_attention_graphs for the variant set.

1. Compilation

# Spyre today (V1, v0.28) MRV2 (31 / main) Class
1.1 Platform forces STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE unless eager; warns on VLLM_COMPILE/DYNAMO_TRACE_ONCE; appends custom_ops "all" (SI/platform.py:286-307). Same CompilationMode enum; CpuPlatform.check_and_update_config rewrites only VLLM_COMPILE (31/platforms/cpu.py:339-363). no change (but see 0.1/0.2: on 0.31.0 this mode disables MRV2).
1.2 load_model fully overrides V1 and never calls super() (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:644-734), so V1's own stock compile (self.model.compile(fullgraph=True, backend) 31/gpu_model_runner.py:5379-5389) never runs. MRV2 load_model (31/model_runner.py:367-527) does model load + init_model_state (:414) + sampler/rejection/prompt-logprobs/structured-output workers + PoolingRunner + EPLB/offloader; on main it then calls compile_model_with_stock_torch (main :535-536). redesign — Spyre can no longer skip super().load_model() without re-implementing ~150 lines of state setup. Recommended: call super with vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner.compile_model_with_stock_torch patched to a no-op (or to _compile_for_spyre). Do not flip compilation_config.mode to NONE around super: SpyreAttentionImpl reads the mode at construction (CLAUDE.md), which happens inside model_loader.load_model.
1.3 _compile_for_spyre / _compile_blocks: per-block block.compile(backend="inductor", fullgraph=..., dynamic=False), whole-model fallback via torch.compile(..., dynamic=False); SPYRE_COMPILE_GRANULARITY (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:791-876). Nothing in MRV2 compiles submodules; has_compiled_submodule only looks for TorchCompileWithNoGuardsWrapper (31/cudagraph_utils.py:133-139), so Spyre's blocks read as "not compiled" → piecewise_capture_available=False (model_runner.py:699-701) — irrelevant under NONE. no change to the function; re-target its call site to after super().load_model() + device move.
1.4 After compile, self.model = _SpyreModelWrapper(...) (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:722-734). MRV2 hands self.model to init_model_state (model_runner.py:414), PoolingRunner(self.model, ...) and EncoderRunner (31/gpu/mm/encoder_runner.py:46), which call self.model.embed_input_ids / embed_multimodal / pooler directly (model_states/default.py:124; encoder_runner.py:168,300; pool/pooling_runner.py:151,195). re-target — wrapping after super leaves those holders pointing at the raw model, bypassing the wrapper's CPU→Spyre conversion and token bucketing in embed_input_ids (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:474-546) → H2D/device errors and one embed_input_ids compile per distinct prompt length. Re-point model_state.model, encoder_runner.model, pooling_runner.model (or wrap before init_model_state). Also inputs_embeds_buffer=self.inputs_embeds.gpu must move to model_state.inputs_embeds / encoder_runner.inputs_embeds (default.py:94-98,125).
1.5 _set_spyre_compilation_settings (inductor freezing toggle) (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:1928-1952); _raise_dynamo_recompile_limits (SI/platform.py:92-111, re-applied in SI/spyre_worker.py:122). Pure torch config; MRV2 adds no torch._dynamo.reset, set_stance, mark_dynamic (grep of main/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/ and gpu_worker.py: only spec_decode/adaptive_verification.py:75 module-level torch.compile, spec-decode only). no change.
1.6 compile_or_warm_up_model override applies the torch-spyre#1420 addm* decomposition workaround (SI/spyre_worker.py:177-200). Worker-level; base version (31/gpu_worker.py:829-1040) adds warmup_kernels (MRV2 only, if kernel_config.enable_jit_warmup, :873-879), capture_model (:881-883), trigger_inductor_lazy_init, _maybe_activate_jit_monitor (Triton-only hooks, 31/vllm/utils/jit_monitor.py:51-75), freeze_gc_heap, enable_gpu_sync_check. no change (keep overriding). Optional: adopt freeze_gc_heap() / set_random_seed re-seed. Never call base warmup_kernels (§2.4).

2. Warmup

# Spyre today MRV2 Class
2.1 warming_up_model (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:878-972) is invoked by Spyre's worker; MRV2 has no warming_up_model (CPU MRV2's is a 16-line profile_run wrapper, 31/vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py:10-16). — no change (Spyre-owned), but its body re-targets below.
2.2 Body buckets: self._dummy_run(size) largest-first, decoder; pooling with force_attention=True (SI :911, :957). _dummy_run(num_tokens, *, skip_attn, uniform_decode, context_len, is_profile, ...) → execute_model(dummy_run=True) (31/model_runner.py:785-940). Attention metadata always built (§0.5). need_eager=is_profile (only profile runs bypass dispatch). Returns (hidden_states, hidden_states[logits_indices]) like V1. re-target: keep calls but drop force_attention; expect attention compiles inside each dummy. The Spyre _dummy_run override (SI :1307-1343) still works (positional num_tokens), but the publish_null_slots pre-step is only needed for skip_attn profile runs. Dummy seq_len=query_len=num_tokens/num_reqs lands in already-bucketed KV/query buckets, so it should not add variants beyond SpyreAttnBucketer.variants(), but verify on HW (SPYRE_COMPILE_GUARD=error).
2.3 _dummy_sampler_run(widest[:rows]) per row bucket (SI :961-965); _dummy_pooler_run, _warm_pooler_row_widths, _warm_encoder_inline_paths, _warm_encoder_unpack (SI :1345-1520). MRV2 _dummy_sampler_run(hidden_states) → model.compute_logits + MRV2 Sampler(logits, InputBatch.make_dummy(...)) (31/model_runner.py:942-954) — Triton sampler; Spyre's SpyreTopKTopPSampler swap targets V1's sampler.topk_topp_sampler (SI :616-619), which MRV2 bypasses (model_runner.py:444 comment). _dummy_pooler_run → pooling_runner.dummy_pooler_run (:956-959). re-target (depends on sampling/pooling slices).
2.4 — warmup_kernels (31/gpu/warmup.py:212-453): real SchedulerOutputs through worker.execute_model/sample_tokens: prefill of num_reqs = min(max_num_seqs, max_num_batched_tokens // 2) requests × 2 tokens, then decode of all / 2 / 1 requests, SamplingParams.for_sampler_warmup(), structured-output bitmask (Triton). do not adopt as is: token counts 2*num_reqs are fine only after the padding override (§0.4); it exercises Triton sampling + bitmask kernels; it must run before compile_guard.arm. Could be an optional end-to-end smoke step later.
2.5 _record_attention_graphs uses self._attn_group_iterator() and group.metadata_builders[0] (SI :1044-1055), static_forward_context, _spyre_kv_caches. MRV2 self.attn_groups: list[list[AttentionGroup]] from init_attn_backend (31/attn_utils.py:195-262; same AttentionGroup class, 31/vllm/v1/worker/utils.py:259-269). No _attn_group_iterator. KV allocated by module function init_kv_cache (attn_utils.py:367-410) inside initialize_kv_cache (model_runner.py:750); self.kv_caches keeps only caches whose .device == self.device (:759-761). re-target: iterate chain.from_iterable(self.attn_groups); populate _spyre_kv_caches from Spyre's own allocation (KV slice owns replacing init_kv_cache; V1's initialize_kv_cache_tensors/initialize_attn_backend/_split_attn_groups_by_layer_window hooks don't exist, SI :1057-1101, :1817-1868).
2.6 _warmup_input_embedding calls wrapper embed_input_ids(zeros(n, int32)) (SI :974-993) because V1 dummy hands a zeroed inputs_embeds. MRV2 dummy also uses model_state.dummy_inputs_embeds(n) (31/model_runner.py:1889-1893; default.py:94-98) — same gap. Real steps call self.model.embed_input_ids(input_ids[:num_tokens]) unpadded (default.py:106-126). no change in intent; requires 1.4 re-pointing so the wrapper (and its bucketing) is the thing model_state calls.
2.7 Pooling forced-rectangle plan via _build_attention_metadata override (SI :1103-1206). Metadata built in model_state.prepare_attn → attn_utils.build_attn_metadata (model_runner.py:1855-1868); no runner-level hook. re-target (to model_state subclass or builder) — attention/pooling slice.

3. Memory profiling

# Spyre today MRV2 Class
3.1 determine_available_memory returns cache_config.kv_cache_memory_bytes (SI/spyre_worker.py:168-175), never calls profile_run. Base worker calls profile_run + memory_profiling / torch.cuda allocator settings (31/gpu_worker.py:571-660, _scoped_allocator_max_split :367-392). MRV2 profile_run = _dummy_run(max_num_tokens, skip_attn=True, is_profile=True) + sampler/pooler + torch.accelerator.synchronize() (model_runner.py:961-991). no change (keep override; worker-level).
3.2 self.model_memory_usage = 0 (SI :666). MRV2 load_model wraps in DeviceMemoryProfiler() → current_platform.get_current_memory_usage (31/vllm/utils/mem_utils.py:82-101) → CpuPlatform NUMA host-RAM reading (31/platforms/cpu.py:761-770). no change (works via CpuPlatform; value is host RSS, informational only).
3.3 torch.accelerator.{empty_cache,synchronize,empty_host_cache} → no-ops, get_memory_info → psutil fallback (SI/platform.py:57-89). MRV2 uses these in profile_run, capture_model, shutdown, warmup_kernels (model_runner.py:988,1038-1039,1076,2302,2332; warmup.py:453). no change (already patched process-wide).
3.4 _torch_cuda_wrapper() around V1 __init__ only (SI :607-608), patching torch.Event / torch.cuda.Stream. MRV2 creates torch.cuda.Stream(self.device) in __init__ (31 :220; main :224), main_stream = torch.cuda.current_stream (31 :555-558), async output uses torch.cuda.Event/streams (31/gpu/async_utils.py:36-45,121-134,226-232,269-277) on every step, and torch.Tensor.pin_memory/record_stream. CPU MRV2 relies on process-wide patches in vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py (31 :29-104, identical on main), imported first by cpu_worker.py:5; Spyre's worker derives from gpu_worker.Worker, so that module is never imported. re-target: a scoped context manager is insufficient; Spyre needs permanent stream/event placeholders (mirror cpu/shm.py, minus its async_tensor_h2d/UvaBuffer CPU-only patches, which the buffer slice must judge for Spyre).

4. Graph capture / CUDA graph manager

# Item Class
4.1 cudagraph_mode forced NONE by support_static_graph_mode()==False (31/vllm.py:1979) — no Spyre change needed; on main _disable_cudagraphs_for_v2_stock_torch_compile is a second guarantee. use_cuda_graph=False / cascade_attn_enabled=False (SI :622-623) are V1 attributes with no MRV2 counterpart — drop. no change / drop
4.2 CudaGraphManager.capture uses graph_capture, torch.cuda.CUDAGraph, set_graph_pool_id / main capture_pool (31/cudagraph_utils.py:399-491; main adds capture_pool, capture_outside_cumem_pool) — never reached under NONE. BreakableCUDAGraphWrapper only if VLLM_USE_BREAKABLE_CUDAGRAPH and piecewise mode (:186-191). no change (unreachable)
4.3 Padding must move into dispatch() (§0.4). DP path sync_cudagraph_and_dp_padding is skipped at dp_size==1 (dp_utils.py:290-291); get_dp_padding override (SI :1883-1884) becomes dead. re-target
4.4 capture_model would log "Skipping encoder and decoder CUDA graph capture" if called (model_runner.py:1027-1033). Spyre's worker never calls it; keep it that way. Encoder CUDA graphs (encoder_runner.has_cudagraph()) stay off unless cudagraph_mm_encoder is set — consider rejecting that knob in the platform. no change

5. Tensor parallel / collectives

# Item Class
5.1 SpyreCommunicator (SI/distributed/spyre_communicator.py) is selected by get_device_communicator_cls (SI/platform.py:416-423) and used through GroupCoordinator by model layers, use_custom_op_collectives()=False (SI/platform.py:448-453). MRV2 runner code issues no TP collectives: grep of 31/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/ finds only PP (pp_utils.py broadcasts on side torch.cuda.Streams, :74,179-280), PCP (pcp_manager.py:753), DP (dp_utils.py) and batch-sharded sampling (sample/batch_shard.py:154,311,562-639). PP/DP are rejected (SI/platform.py:~690-708); PCP default 1; batch-sharded sampling defaults False (31/vllm/config/vllm.py:3180-3185) and needs compute_logits_local. no change (recommend rejecting enable_batch_sharded_sampling and PCP>1 in check_and_update_config)
5.2 No pynccl / custom all-reduce / graph_capture context on the NONE path. disable_custom_all_reduce is CPUWorker-only (31/cpu_worker.py:104); unchanged for Spyre. no change
5.3 init_device override (env vars for libspyre_comms, deferred torch_spyre import, torch.spyre.set_device, init_worker_distributed_environment) (SI/spyre_worker.py:99-166) is worker-level and independent of the runner. Base 0.31 init_device additionally does init_workspace_manager (31/gpu_worker.py:497-504); MRV2 only touches workspaces under spec-decode lanes and in capture_model's lock_workspace() (model_runner.py:1079-1081, unreachable). no change
5.4 Compiled-graph collectives depend only on traced shapes being bucketed (all_reduce flatten + 64-aligned all_gather); the §0.4 padding override is what keeps the in-graph collectives on shapes warmup compiled. covered by §0.4

6. Sleep mode / cumem

# Item Class
6.1 sleep / wake_up are no-ops (SI/spyre_worker.py:202-206). Base 0.31 routes through sleep_mode_backend (31/gpu_worker.py:236-341); MRV2 runner has no sleep logic. no change
6.2 _maybe_get_memory_pool_context → nullcontext() (SI/spyre_worker.py:91-97). Still required: base 0.31 checks is_cpu() (31/gpu_worker.py:356) which is False for OOT, then calls get_mem_allocator_instance(). Same context is now also passed as kv_cache_allocation_context to model_runner.initialize_kv_cache (gpu_worker.py:806-816) and used by init_kv_cache (attn_utils.py:377). no change
6.3 Worker.discard(tags) (31/gpu_worker.py:339-340) calls sleep_mode_backend.discard (cumem); Spyre does not override it. CPUWorker raises (31/cpu_worker.py:179-180). no change w.r.t. MRV2; minor hardening: override to raise.

7. Torch profiler

# Item Class
7.1 profile() override builds TorchProfilerWrapper(..., activities=["CPU","PrivateUse1"]) (SI/spyre_worker.py:208-227). Signature unchanged at 31 (vllm/profiler/wrapper.py:191-198) and main (:224-231); base now uses create_worker_profiler with device-type defaults {"cpu": ("CPU",)} (31 :627-631, :675-697). no change (worker-level).
7.2 monkey_patch_torch_profiler_activity_map (SI/spyre_worker.py:60-74) is already a no-op: "PrivateUse1" is in TorchProfilerActivityMap at 28/vllm/profiler/wrapper.py:166-169 and at 31/main. delete (cleanup, independent of MRV2)
7.3 _get_cudagraph_capture_context (31/gpu_worker.py:1027-1040) creates a graph-capture profiler for MRV2 — only in base compile_or_warm_up_model, which Spyre overrides. no change

8. compile_guard.py — dependencies and recompile surface

What it depends on (SI/spyre_inference/v1/worker/compile_guard.py): only torch internals — torch._dynamo.callback.callback_handler start callbacks (:170-185, :204-215), the _compile frame in torch/_dynamo/convert_frame.py and its code local (:355-373), the eager-op trampoline file torch/_dynamo/external_utils.py (:95), TORCH_DYNAMO_RESUME_IN_PREFIX (:97-102), OptimizedModule._orig_mod / _torchdynamo_orig_callable unwrapping (:376-439). It imports nothing from vLLM V1 except the logger. Coupling to V1 is only via its callers: registration in _compile_for_spyre / _compile_blocks (SI/spyre_model_runner.py:853,874) and arming in compile_or_warm_up_model after warming_up_model (SI/spyre_worker.py:192-196). Module itself: no change. Call sites: re-target with 1.2/1.3.

How MRV2 changes the recompile surface (shapes reaching watched callables):

Source V1 (today) MRV2 Risk / action
Body token count Padded by _determine_batch_execution_and_padding after warmup Padded only by cudagraph_manager.dispatch; NONE → unpadded High — every prompt length recompiles every block class; fix via §0.4.
input_ids / positions Slices of persistent buffers at num_input_tokens; wrapper casts int32/int64 → Spyre int64 (SI :387-398) input_batch.input_ids / positions slices at num_tokens_after_padding (input_batch.py:174-175; model_runner.py:1898-1906); same dtypes No change once padded.
M-RoPE positions [3, N] from V1 mrope buffers rope_state.get_positions(num_tokens_after_padding) (default.py:146-159) Same shape family; verify the rope_state buffer lives where the wrapper expects.
inputs_embeds (MM / prompt-embeds) V1 _preprocess → wrapper embed_input_ids (bucketed, staged into inputs_embeds.gpu) model_state.prepare_inputs_embeds calls self.model.embed_input_ids(input_ids[:num_tokens]) unpadded and slice-assigns into model_state.inputs_embeds[:n] (default.py:106-133) High unless 1.4 is done: new embed_input_ids + copy_from_d2d compile per distinct n, and the narrowed-destination H2D that torch-spyre#4730 rejects.
intermediate_tensors kwarg None None (model_runner.py:1901) No change.
Attention kernels Lazy only via _record_attention_graphs; dummies skip attention Dummies run attention (§0.5) with seq_len=query_len, zero block tables, PAD_SLOT_ID slots Medium — extra compiles during warmup (before arming, harmless); confirm they stay inside the recorded variant set.
Logits rows Wrapper pads to logits_row_buckets (SI :422-449) sample() calls self.model.compute_logits(...) on the wrapper No change if the wrapper stays self.model.
New compile sites — main: compile_model_with_stock_torch whole-model compile (wrapper.py:53-61); spec-decode adaptive_verification.py:75 Suppress the first (1.2); the second is unreachable (no spec decode). An unsuppressed whole-model model.compile would report as UNKNOWN (never fatal) and hide behind block compiles.
skip_compiled forward-context flag — Set for enc-dec with scheduled encoder inputs (model_runner.py:1729-1734) Ignored by plain torch.compile blocks; enc-dec not supported on Spyre anyway.

A9. Cost of staying on V1, history, strategies, phased plan (09_history_strategy.md)

09: Upgrade-path pain (V1 through 0.31) and MRV2 migration strategy

Scope: (a) what breaks in our current V1-based runner/worker on 0.29 to 0.31/main regardless of MRV2; (b) candidate migration strategies, a phased plan, and an EPIC breakdown.

Path abbreviations: V28=/home/senuser/.claude/jobs/709f9980/tmp/vllm-v0.28.0, V29, V31, MAIN (vllm-main is bab0ac61f, 2026-10-09), SI=/home/senuser/spyre-inference.

0. TL;DR

  • V1 is not frozen. Between v0.28.0 and main, gpu_model_runner.py had 41 commits (net −611 lines), and the methods we override changed in ways that break us. At least three hard breaks land in 0.29.0 and one more in 0.31.0, independent of MRV2. Staying on V1 through 0.31 is cheap but not free: roughly 1 to 2 PRs per bump, about the same as recent bumps.
  • Past vLLM bumps (0.23 to 0.28) were cheap: each was 1 PR, merged in 1 to 7 days, about 40 to 300 LOC of non-lockfile change. But the runner has grown 4x (477 to 1952 lines) and its super()-wrapped surface grew from 2 to 9 methods since 0.23. Most of that growth came after the 0.28 bump (+1022/−200 lines over 37 commits), so the next bump is the first to test the new surface.
  • MRV2 has a hard Triton gate (HAS_TRITON) and about 25 Triton kernels on the default path. Spyre bans Triton (SI/pyproject.toml:77-78, SI/spyre_inference/platform.py:33). On Spyre, vllm_config.use_v2_model_runner therefore resolves to False (V31/vllm/config/vllm.py:746-749). Forcing it on raises "Model Runner V2 requires Triton." (V31/vllm/config/vllm.py:3316-3318). Upstream now has the way out: the Triton kernel dispatcher (Platform.register_triton_kernel_overrides, vllm#43048, first in v0.31.0).
  • Recommended route: Strategy 1 (subclass MRV2 GPUModelRunner with device=cpu, as vLLM's own CPU MRV2 runner does) plus Strategy 4 in parallel (upstream asks). Use a time-boxed Strategy 3 (vendor V1) only as the 0.32 contingency.

(a) Cost of staying on V1 through 0.29 to 0.31

a.1 What we touch

TorchSpyreModelRunner (SI/spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_model_runner.py:560) overrides 14 V1 GPUModelRunner methods (an AST diff against V28; there are 22 more Spyre-only methods): __init__(571), load_model(644, full replacement, no super), initialize_attn_backend(1057), _build_attention_metadata(1103), _determine_batch_execution_and_padding(1235, full signature pinned), _dummy_run(1308), _preprocess(1521), _dummy_pooler_run_task(1662), _pool(1706, mostly a copy of upstream), initialize_kv_cache_tensors(1817, full replacement), _init_device_properties(1873), _sync_device(1877), get_model(1886), _make_buffer(1900). It also overrides get_dp_padding(1883), which no longer exists on V1 in any of 0.28/0.29/0.31/main and is dead code. It sets self.use_cuda_graph(620), which is also dead: V1 never reads it.

Other V1-internal imports:

  • vllm.v1.worker.cpu_model_runner._torch_cuda_wrapper (spyre_model_runner.py:65). This is a V1 CPU runner, so it goes when V1 does.
  • vllm.v1.worker.gpu_model_runner._copy_pooler_output_to_cpu (SI/spyre_inference/v1/pool/spyre_pooler.py:111).
  • vllm.v1.worker.block_table._compute_slot_mapping_kernel monkeypatch (spyre_model_runner.py:629-642).
  • vllm.v1.utils.CpuGpuBuffer, vllm.v1.worker.utils.{bind_kv_cache,AttentionGroup}.
  • PoolingMetadata/PoolingCursor (subclassed as SpyrePoolingCursor, spyre_pooler.py:236).

The worker TorchSpyreWorker(gpu_worker.Worker) (SI/spyre_inference/v1/worker/spyre_worker.py:77) fully replaces init_device, determine_available_memory and compile_or_warm_up_model. It no-ops sleep/wake_up and _maybe_get_memory_pool_context, and wraps profile.

a.2 Churn per overridden method (lines changed in the upstream method body vs v0.28.0)

method 0.29 0.31 main impact on us
initialize_kv_cache_tensors 34 42 42 BREAK (0.29): new kwarg + KVCacheTensor schema change (below)
_build_attention_metadata 1 54 61 super-wrapped; num_tokens_padded kwarg still there (MAIN gpu_model_runner.py:2272). OK
_dummy_run 0 25 25 super-wrapped, new kwargs (randomize_inputs, is_padding). Passthrough OK
load_model 6 40 40 we fully replace it. 0.31 adds jit_warmup_registry.activate() and lookback_token_ids; silently skipped (low risk today)
__init__ 65 42 53 super-wrapped under _torch_cuda_wrapper
_determine_batch_execution_and_padding 0 0 13 signature unchanged; main drops SP padding (#60517)
_pool 5 5 5 our copy diverges: upstream uses cursor.get_finished_mask() (PoolingCursor gained partial_prefill/finished_mask fields). Drift, not a break
_preprocess 0 2 2 return arity unchanged (6-tuple). OK
profile_run 0 4 4 new randomize_inputs kwarg; we don't override it
_dummy_pooler_run_task, _dummy_sampler_run, _dummy_pooler_run, _make_buffer, get_model, _sync_device, _attn_group_iterator 0 0 0 stable

Worker (gpu_worker.Worker, lines changed vs 0.28): init_device 11/18/44, determine_available_memory 33/62/66, compile_or_warm_up_model 0/38/42, initialize_from_config 13/24/36, profile 0/38/52, load_model 0/8/8.

a.3 Concrete breaks and traps found (with fixes)

  1. 0.29 BREAK: initialize_kv_cache_tensors(..., kv_cache_allocation_context=...). The caller now passes a kwarg (V29/vllm/v1/worker/gpu_model_runner.py:7524-7528, from vllm#53508 "[MRV2] Isolate sleep-mode KV allocations"). Our override (self, kv_cache_config, kernel_block_sizes) (SI .../spyre_model_runner.py:1817) then raises TypeError. Fix: accept and ignore the kwarg (or **kwargs).
  2. 0.29 BREAK: KVCacheTensor schema rewrite (vllm#51718 "[6/N][KV-Cache Layout Refactor] Standardize KV cache layout").
    • 0.28 had size (per-tensor) and shared_by: list[str] (V28/vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.py:929-937).
    • 0.29 has size (total backing allocation), layers (distinct layers in L order), layer_stride and block_stride (V29/vllm/v1/kv_cache_interface.py:1165-1184).
    • Our allocation loop reads kv_cache_tensor.shared_by[0] and computes num_blocks = kv_cache_tensor.size // spec.page_size_bytes (spyre_model_runner.py:1846-1856). The first raises AttributeError; the second is semantically wrong under the new meaning (it over-counts by len(layers)).
    • bind_kv_cache also gains kv_cache_groups (MAIN/vllm/v1/worker/utils.py:653-659).
    • The same PR rewrote tests/v1/attention/test_attention_backends.py (+273 lines). That is the recurring KV-layout trap for patch_backend_list (docs/contributing/vllm-upgrade-procedure.md, step 3e).
    • Initialize_from_config now also calls record_kv_cache_layout(...) (main).
  3. 0.29 BREAK (latent until the first slot-mapping call): the slot-mapping monkeypatch is dead. 0.28 launched the module global _compute_slot_mapping_kernel[(num_reqs+1,)](...). In 0.29 this became a ComputeSlotMappingKernel(VllmTritonJitKernel) whose __call__ does self.kernel[(num_reqs+1,)](*args, ...) (V29/vllm/v1/worker/block_table.py:200-228, 518-529, from vllm#50174 JIT-warmup infra and #53564).
    • Our assignment block_table._compute_slot_mapping_kernel = ... (spyre_model_runner.py:640) now creates an unused attribute.
    • Under TritonPlaceholder, jit returns the bare function (V29/vllm/triton_utils/importing.py:115-121), so self.kernel[...] raises TypeError: 'function' object is not subscriptable.
    • Fix for 0.29/0.30: patch block_table.ComputeSlotMappingKernel.kernel with _FuncWrapper.
    • Fix for 0.31+: see item 4.
  4. 0.31 TRAP: inherited CpuPlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides. vllm#43048 (first tag v0.31.0) adds Platform.register_triton_kernel_overrides() (V31/vllm/platforms/interface.py:374), called from WorkerWrapperBase before the worker is built (V31/vllm/v1/worker/worker_base.py:275).
    • CpuPlatform overrides it when not HAS_TRITON (V31/vllm/platforms/cpu.py:651) and registers ComputeSlotMappingKernel.kernel and other kernels to torch.ops._C.* C++ impls (V31/vllm/utils/cpu_triton_utils.py:12-37).
    • TorchSpyrePlatform(CpuPlatform) (SI/spyre_inference/platform.py:114) inherits it. We build with VLLM_TARGET_DEVICE=empty and skip _C (platform.py:210), so the first slot-mapping call fails.
    • Fix: override register_triton_kernel_overrides on TorchSpyrePlatform and register our pure-PyTorch _compute_slot_mapping_impl via vllm.triton_utils.dispatcher.register_kernels (V31/vllm/triton_utils/dispatcher.py:147). This is the same mechanism MRV2 needs, so the 0.31 fix is the first brick of the migration.
  5. 0.31 silent behaviour change: set_torch_threads_for_runtime() moved. In 0.28 it ran in Worker.compile_or_warm_up_model (V28/vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py:867), which we override, so we never ran it. In 0.31 it runs in Worker.load_model (V31/vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py:561, vllm#55891), which we inherit. It sets torch intra-op threads to 1 before our warmup/compile and for serving, unless an externally set OMP_NUM_THREADS is detected. This interacts with our thread clamp (SI/spyre_inference/threading_config.py). Expect possible host-side (sampling/compile) slowdowns. Verify, and override if needed.
  6. main (0.32) only:
    • Worker.profile(..., *, delay_iterations, max_iterations). Our profile override lacks the kw-only params. The executor pre-checks the worker signature (MAIN/vllm/v1/executor/abstract.py:295-309), so this only fails when the user passes them. vllm#57460 (0.31) unifies "platform-aware torch profiling", which may make our profiler override removable.
    • Worker._make_model_runner() hook (vllm#59200, post-0.31, MAIN/vllm/v1/worker/gpu_worker.py:542). This is a cleaner seam than our full init_device copy.
    • V1 _pool gains a non-CUDA branch (if not current_platform.is_cuda_alike()) and an async copy-stream branch.
  7. Not broken, verified stable through main:
    • CommonAttentionMetadata fields we read (query_start_loc[_cpu], seq_lens, num_reqs, num_actual_tokens, max_query_len, max_seq_len, block_table_tensor, slot_mapping, causal, is_prefilling). Only the private _seq_lens_cpu/_num_computed_tokens_cpu were removed, and dcp_local_seq_lens_cpu was renamed ..._upper_bound.
    • split_decodes_and_prefills signature.
    • build_pooling_cursor signature.
    • The _preprocess 6-tuple.
    • _build_attention_metadata kwargs.
  8. Engine-level: on 0.29+ use_v2_model_runner is computed even though our worker hard-constructs TorchSpyreModelRunner (spyre_worker.py:162). With no Triton it resolves to False and logs "Model Runner V2 requires Triton; using the V1 model runner instead". So the issue's VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER=0 stopgap is unnecessary, and setting it to 1 would be harmful: it raises at config validation. Be careful: once V1 is removed (0.32), every config path that branches on use_v2_model_runner will assume MRV2 (e.g. max_concurrent_batches, MAIN/vllm/config/vllm.py:588).

Since the 0.28 bump there have been 37 commits on the runner (encoder rectangular path, pooling on device, batched-decode recording, FP8, MM). Most new overrides (_preprocess, _build_attention_metadata, _pool, _dummy_pooler_run_task, initialize_attn_backend) post-date the last bump. So the historical "1 small PR per bump" figure underestimates the 0.29 bump.

Estimated cost of staying on V1 through 0.31:

  • 0.29: about 1 PR, 1 to 2 weeks (items 1 to 3, plus the upstream test-plugin KV-layout rework).
  • 0.30: small.
  • 0.31: about 1 PR (items 4 and 5).

The cost is modest. V1 is officially deprecated ("not accepting MRV1-specific improvements", v0.29.0 release notes) and slated for removal in v0.32. V1 upstream churn is mostly deletions plus shared-infra changes (KV layout, JIT warmup, profiler). Shared infra will keep moving under MRV2 too.

(b) What MRV2 actually allows

MRV2 GPUModelRunner (MAIN/vllm/v1/worker/gpu/model_runner.py, 2454 lines) is CUDA-bound in its constructor:

  • torch.cuda.Stream(self.device) at :224.
  • The main_stream property returns torch.cuda.current_stream (:575-577).
  • It builds RequestState(:275) and InputBuffers(:315) directly. model_state (:420) and Sampler (:474) are created in load_model. ModelCudaGraphManager is created in initialize_kv_cache (:738).

Other CUDA-isms:

  • async_utils.py:36-45,134,232,269-277: torch.cuda.Event/set_stream.
  • structured_outputs.py:91-134: copy stream.
  • pp_utils.py:73-74 and ubatch_utils.py:327-329: PP/DBO only.
  • buffer_utils.py:29-39: UvaBuffer needs pinned memory. NonUvaBuffer (:41) fallback since vllm#56908 (v0.30.0).

Triton kernels on the default (non-spec-decode) path (@triton.jit count):

  • input_batch.py (7): prepare_prefill_inputs, prepare_pos_seq_lens, combine_sampled_and_draft, get_num_sampled_and_rejected, post_update, post_update_num_computed_tokens, expand_idx_mapping.
  • block_table.py (2): gather_block_tables, compute_slot_mappings.
  • buffer_utils.py (2): apply_write.
  • sample: gumbel.py (14 jit helpers; 2 entry kernels: temperature, gumbel_sample), logprob.py (3), penalties.py (2), min_p.py, logit_bias.py, bad_words.py, output.py (compact mask).
  • model_states/prompt_embeds.py and mm/rope.py.

That is about 20 entry kernels, all launched as kernel[grid](...). That launch form is exactly what KernelOverride.__getitem__ in the dispatcher supports (V31/vllm/triton_utils/dispatcher.py). It explicitly handles the TritonPlaceholder (no-Triton) case by reading the plain function's signature.

Precedent 1: vLLM's own CPU MRV2.

  • MAIN/vllm/v1/worker/cpu/model_runner.py is a 16-line subclass of MRV2 GPUModelRunner (since vllm#43225, v0.22). It works with device=cpu because cpu/shm.py globally patches the torch CUDA/stream/event/pin_memory APIs and UvaBuffer (MAIN/vllm/v1/worker/cpu/shm.py:60-104).
  • It relies on Triton-CPU for the kernels, which Spyre does not have.
  • That is structurally our current design (runner self.device = cpu, floats on Spyre at the model boundary), so MRV2-on-CPU is the closest template.

Precedent 2: XPU subclasses MRV2 in about 12 lines (MAIN/vllm/v1/worker/xpu_model_runner.py:31).

Precedent 3: vllm-ascend NPUModelRunner(GPUModelRunner) (https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm-ascend/blob/main/vllm_ascend/worker/v2/model_runner.py, about 57 KB plus attn_utils.py 82 KB, pcp_manager.py 28 KB):

  • It enters super().__init__ under torch_cuda_wrapper().
  • It then del self.req_states / self.input_buffers / self.speculator and rebuilds Ascend subclasses (to get seq_lens_cpu/num_computed_tokens_cpu).
  • It overrides prepare_inputs, execute_model, sample, initialize_kv_cache, _dummy_run and profile_run.
  • README lists gaps: custom KV allocate/reshape, graph wrapper (https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm-ascend/blob/main/vllm_ascend/worker/v2/README.md).
  • RFC: https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm-ascend/issues/15254. Ascend has triton-ascend, so it did not need kernel rewrites at scale.

Upstream pluggability work:

  • RFC vllm#51212 "Model Runner V2 Pluggable Design" (wangxiyuan, open), https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/51212.
    • L1 is the Triton dispatcher (RFC #45133, closed; implemented in #43048, in v0.31.0).
    • L2 is Platform.get_v2_runner_components() returning V2RunnerComponents (RequestState, InputBuffers, InputBatch, CudaGraphManager, Sampler, PCPManager).
    • L3 is prepare_inputs pre/post hooks. LucasWilkinson pushed back on L3 as too invasive.
    • Open PRs: #52719 "[Platform][MRV2] Make MRV2 Components Pluggable" (wangxiyuan, +249/−27, updated 09-24) and #53895 "[MRV2] Add platform-provided runner component factory" (+250/−13, updated 09-10).
  • Tracking issues: vllm#41286 (V1 to V2 migration), vllm#47172 (MRV2 remaining TODOs). The latter shows STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE mode on MRV2 merged in #59079 (2026-09-29, after v0.31.0, so first in 0.32), non-pinned memory (#56908, v0.30), and encoder-only attention / full pooling compatibility done.
  • v0.29.0 release notes: "Model Runner V1 Deprecation ... targeting v0.32 for its removal" (https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm/releases/tag/v0.29.0).

Hard upstream gates we must get relaxed or bypassed:

  • HAS_TRITON in use_v2_model_runner (V31/vllm/config/vllm.py:746) and in _validate_v2_model_runner (:3316-3318, run when use_v2_model_runner is True, :2051). If V1 is deleted and these checks stay, no Triton-less platform can start.
  • Today vllm-cpu without Triton-CPU has the same problem, so this is a shared ask.

(b) Candidate strategies

S1: Subclass MRV2 GPUModelRunner (device=cpu host bookkeeping, Spyre at the model boundary)

  • How:
    • TorchSpyreModelRunnerV2(vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_runner.GPUModelRunner) with device=cpu.
    • Patch the torch.cuda stream/event APIs (a _torch_cuda_wrapper scoped to __init__ plus main_stream, or a scoped version of cpu/shm.py).
    • Register pure-PyTorch impls for about 20 MRV2 Triton kernels via register_triton_kernel_overrides (0.31+).
    • Replace Sampler with a Spyre/CPU sampler built on SpyreTopKTopPSampler.
    • Replace ModelCudaGraphManager with a "Spyre bucket manager" that maps BatchExecutionDescriptor padding onto SpyreShapeBucketer (this replaces today's _determine_batch_execution_and_padding hijack).
    • Override initialize_kv_cache (paged per-layer SpyrePagedKVCache, attention-group split by sliding window), load_model (head-pad installs, .to(spyre), per-block compile, _SpyreModelWrapper), and prepare_inputs/execute_model slices for the encoder rectangular grid and RoBERTa position offset.
    • Pool via pooling_runner.
  • Pros:
    • Upstream-sanctioned path, same as CPU/XPU/Ascend.
    • We inherit async scheduling, structured outputs, logprobs and the upstream fixes.
    • The L2 components factory (#52719/#53895) would remove the del-and-rebuild hacks once merged.
  • Cons:
    • MRV2 churns heavily: about 150 commits and about 10k lines added in vllm/v1/worker/gpu/ from 0.28 to 0.31 (0.28 to 0.29: +2600/−747; 0.29 to 0.30: +3589/−689; 0.30 to 0.31: +2316/−798; 0.31 to main: +1403/−298).
    • MRV2 is "async-first": it assumes device-side state (idx_mapping, positions, seq_lens on device). Our int tensors must stay on host, which works only because device=cpu.
    • Kernel overrides are keyed by qualified names ("vllm.v1.worker.gpu.input_batch._post_update_kernel"), a new brittle surface. Each rename silently falls back to the placeholder, then TypeError.
    • The STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE + MRV2 combination only arrives in 0.32 (#59079).
  • Effort: L (about 6 to 10 engineer-weeks, incl. kernel ports + tests).
  • Risk: medium. Kernel-override drift and prepare_inputs copy drift until L2/L3 hooks land.

S2: Standalone Spyre runner implementing the worker-facing interface, borrowing MRV2 parts

  • How: a new class (not subclassing either runner) implementing what Worker/WorkerBase call.
    • Calls from MAIN gpu_worker.py: load_model, get_kv_cache_spec, initialize_kv_cache, execute_model, sample_tokens, pool, profile_run, _dummy_run, _dummy_sampler_run, _dummy_pooler_run, get_model, get_supported_tasks, take_draft_token_ids, update_max_model_len, reset_mm_cache, reset_encoder_cache, capture_model, profile_cudagraph_memory, model_memory_usage, and the LoRA methods.
    • Reuse CPU-safe pieces: RequestState, InputBuffers, BlockTables, attn_utils.build_attn_metadata, pooling metadata, and the V1 Sampler or MRV2 sampler with overrides.
  • Pros:
    • We own the step loop. The Spyre-specific bucketing, encoder grid and warmup-recording logic become first-class instead of hijacked hooks.
    • Insulated from MRV2 internal refactors except for the parts we import.
    • Easier to reason about synchronous host↔Spyre staging.
  • Cons:
    • We re-implement about 1.5 to 2.5k lines of scheduling-diff bookkeeping (add_requests/update_requests/ finish_requests, async output, logprobs, structured outputs, prompt logprobs, spec-decode hooks).
    • We silently miss upstream features and bug fixes. This is the "every backend copies the runner" anti-pattern RFC #51212 calls out.
    • The worker interface itself is informal: methods appear across releases, e.g. _init_kv_zero_meta, get_encoder_timing_stats, release_late_interaction_query_cache.
  • Effort: L/XL (8 to 12 weeks). Risk: medium-high for feature parity; low for upstream churn.

S3: Vendor/freeze V1 GPUModelRunner into spyre-inference

  • How: copy gpu_model_runner.py (7502 lines on main) plus the private dependencies we need (gpu_input_batch.py 1146, block_table.py 548, utils.py 830, mixins, cpu_model_runner._torch_cuda_wrapper, _copy_pooler_output_to_cpu), about 13.5k lines total, at the last release that has it (0.31.x). Then trim aggressively (no CUDA graphs, DBO, PCP/DCP, spec decode, mamba, EPLB).
  • Pros:
    • Zero behaviour change.
    • Unblocks 0.32 immediately.
    • Our 14 overrides keep working.
  • Cons:
    • 94 vllm.* imports in the file; shared infra (KV config schema, attention metadata, scheduler output, pooling metadata, warmup registry) keeps moving, so the vendored copy rots every bump.
    • Upstream code outside the runner imports gpu_model_runner/gpu_input_batch, e.g. v1/attention/backends/utils.py, structured_output/utils.py, platforms/cpu.py: 28 files on main. When V1 is removed those modules drop V1 types too.
    • No path to new features (MRV2-only: watermarking, HiSparse, new samplers).
    • It defers the real work.
  • Effort: S/M to stand up (1 to 2 weeks), then a growing tax per bump. Risk: low short-term, high long-term.
  • Use only as a contingency if S1 is not ready when we must take 0.32.

S4: Upstream platform hooks into MRV2 (enabler, in parallel with S1)

Concrete asks, in priority order:

  1. Relax the HAS_TRITON gates when the platform has registered overrides for all MRV2 kernels, or add a Platform.supports_mrv2_without_triton() hook (V31/vllm/config/vllm.py:746, 3316).
  2. Land the L2 component factory: review and co-author #52719 / #53895. We need sampler_cls, cudagraph_manager_cls (bucket manager), input_buffers_cls and request_state_cls.
  3. Replace raw torch.cuda.Stream/Event/current_stream in the MRV2 constructor and in async_utils with current_platform-aware or torch.accelerator equivalents. This removes the need for torch_cuda_wrapper/shm.py-style global patching.
  4. Upstream pure-PyTorch reference impls for the MRV2 Triton kernels (e.g. vllm/v1/worker/gpu/torch_ref/) registered by CpuPlatform when no Triton-CPU. This helps vllm-cpu too, so it is likely to be accepted. We could then inherit them from CpuPlatform instead of maintaining them.
  5. A KV-cache allocation hook (allocate_kv_cache override per platform). Ascend has the same gap ("_allocate_kv_cache & _reshape_kv_cache" in its README).
  6. (Maybe) a narrow prepare_inputs post hook for the encoder-grid expansion (L3; contentious upstream).
  • Pros: shrinks S1 to a thin subclass, shared with Ascend/CPU, lowers per-bump tax.
  • Cons: timeline not in our control. The RFC has been open since 08-06 and the PRs are still open.
  • Effort: M (review cycles). Risk: schedule.

Recommendation: S1 + S4, with S3 pre-planned as a fallback for the 0.32 bump only.

Phased plan

Phase 0: stay on V1 for 0.29 to 0.31 (stopgap).

  • 0.29 bump:
    • Accept the kv_cache_allocation_context kwarg.
    • Port initialize_kv_cache_tensors to the new KVCacheTensor{layers, layer_stride, block_stride} schema (derive num_blocks from kv_cache_config.num_blocks; each layers[i] gets its own pages).
    • Patch ComputeSlotMappingKernel.kernel.
    • Rework patch_backend_list for the #51718 test-layout change.
    • Delete dead get_dp_padding/use_cuda_graph.
    • Do not set VLLM_USE_V2_MODEL_RUNNER. Add a test asserting use_v2_model_runner is False and that our runner is selected.
  • 0.30: routine.
  • 0.31:
    • Override TorchSpyrePlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides (do not inherit CpuPlatform's _C impls).
    • Audit set_torch_threads_for_runtime in Worker.load_model.
    • Consider adopting _make_model_runner when available.

Phase 1: foundations (can start now, on 0.31 dev branch / vllm main).

  • Pure-PyTorch impls of the about 20 MRV2 kernels, unit-tested against the Triton reference on a GPU CI box, or against the CPU MRV2 with Triton-CPU in a nightly job. Register them via the dispatcher.
  • A kernel-name coverage test that fails if any @triton.jit on the MRV2 default path lacks an override. That guards the qualified-name drift.

Phase 2: MRV2 skeleton.

  • TorchSpyreModelRunnerV2 with device=cpu. Decoder-only, eager (enforce_eager), TP1, greedy plus top-k/top-p via the Spyre sampler.
  • Gated by a SPYRE_MODEL_RUNNER=v2 env var. The worker selects the runner.

Phase 3: compile + bucketing.

  • Spyre bucket manager in place of ModelCudaGraphManager. Maps padding onto SpyreShapeBucketer.
  • Warmup recording of attention graphs (_record_attention_graphs), per-block compile.
  • Needs #59079 (STOCK_TORCH_COMPILE on MRV2), so it targets vLLM 0.32.

Phase 4: KV cache + attention.

  • Head-major/token-major paged allocation through an initialize_kv_cache override.
  • Sliding-window group split, batched decode, null-slot publication.

Phase 5: pooling/encoder.

  • Rectangular encoder grid (today's _build_attention_metadata/_preprocess/_pool overrides) on MRV2's pooling runner and encoder-only path (#49331).

Phase 6: multimodal + TP>1 + FP8 + LoRA parity, then flip the default.

  • Delete the V1 runner when vLLM 0.32 lands.
  • Contingency: if Phase 3 is not green by the 0.32 release, vendor V1 (S3) for one release.

Test strategy:

  • Run the same e2e suite (tests/e2e, attention, pooling, distributed) parametrized over runner=v1|v2 while both exist. Use a golden-output comparison V1 vs V2 on Spyre (token-exact greedy for decoder; cosine for pooling).
  • Add a compile-guard check that V2 records no new graphs after warmup (reuse compile_guard).
  • Upstream MRV2 tests in upstream_tests.yaml (tests/v1/worker/gpu/...), especially input-batch and sampler tests, run against our kernel overrides.
  • Kernel-override unit tests on CPU (no Spyre needed) so most of Phase 1/2 runs in regular CI.
  • On a non-Spyre host "all green" proves nothing for device paths (CLAUDE.md). Keep the hardware-gated jobs.

Suggested EPIC breakdown (sub-issues)

  1. [0.29 bump] KV-cache allocation for the new KVCacheTensor schema plus the kv_cache_allocation_context kwarg.
  2. [0.29 bump] Fix the slot-mapping kernel patch (ComputeSlotMappingKernel.kernel). Remove dead overrides (get_dp_padding, use_cuda_graph).
  3. [0.29 bump] Upstream attention-backend test plugin: KV layout #51718.
  4. [0.31 bump] TorchSpyrePlatform.register_triton_kernel_overrides (do not inherit CpuPlatform _C impls). Torch-threads audit.
  5. Pure-PyTorch MRV2 kernel library plus a dispatcher registration and coverage test (about 20 kernels: input_batch, block_table, buffer_utils, sampler family, prompt_embeds, mm rope).
  6. MRV2 runner skeleton (device=cpu, CUDA stream/event shims, worker selection flag), decoder eager TP1.
  7. Spyre sampler on MRV2 (sort-free top-k/top-p, Gumbel replacement, logprobs).
  8. Spyre bucket/graph manager replacing ModelCudaGraphManager (bucketer dispatch, warmup, attention-graph recording, compile guard). Depends on vLLM #59079.
  9. KV cache + attention groups on MRV2 (initialize_kv_cache, sliding-window split, head-major/token-major).
  10. Pooling/encoder rectangular path on MRV2 pooling runner.
  11. Multimodal (inputs_embeds staging, gemma4/granite-vision) on MRV2.
  12. TP>1, FP8 and LoRA parity on MRV2.
  13. Upstream engagement: HAS_TRITON gate relaxation, components factory (#52719/#53895), accelerator-agnostic streams, torch reference kernels for CPU.
  14. Contingency: vendor V1 runner for one release (only if 8 to 10 are not done by the 0.32 bump).
  15. Flip default to V2. Delete the V1 runner and the _torch_cuda_wrapper/CpuGpuBuffer/_copy_pooler_output_to_cpu imports. Update docs, the upgrade-vllm procedure and CLAUDE.md.
